name: analyze-csharp
description: Format C# code, run the .NET analyzers through the build, and fix every reported error until the build is clean. Use when you write or change a C# file, or when the user asks to lint, analyze, format or build C# code.
license: MIT

# Analyze C# code

## Rules

- Fix the code. Never change `.editorconfig`, `Directory.Build.props` or a `.globalconfig` file to pass.
- Never add `#pragma warning disable`, `[SuppressMessage]`, `<NoWarn>` or the `!` operator to hide an error.
- Never write `async void`, except for an event handler.
- Never fix formatting by hand. The format command does it.
- Fix one error ID at a time. Start with the first error of the list.
- Stop after 3 failed attempts on the same error. Show the error to the user.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Note the C# files you wrote or changed. Follow the "Right" examples of `docs/csharp-analysis/` for the kind of code you write.

<!-- files:begin id=analyze -->

2. Run the format command from the folder that holds `Directory.Build.props`:
   `bash scripts/format-csharp.sh`
3. Run the build. The build runs the analyzers:
   `dotnet build --no-restore -tl:off -nologo -v:q > /tmp/build.log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"`
4. If the output is `exit=0`, go to step 9.
5. List the errors, each one once, 30 lines at most:
   `grep -E ": (error|warning) " /tmp/build.log | sort -u | head -n 30`
6. Take the first error. Note its ID (for example `CA2016`), its file and its line.
7. Find the ID in `docs/csharp-analysis/index.md`. Open that document. Read only the section of that ID. Change the code to the "Right" form.
8. Go back to step 2.
9. Run `grep -rn --include=*.cs "async void" . | grep -v EventArgs`. It must print nothing. Else return a `Task` and go back to step 2.
10. Run the format check: `bash scripts/format-csharp.sh --check`. If it fails, go back to step 2.

<!-- files:end -->

11. Give the result: the IDs you fixed, and every error left.

## Example

Wrong:

```csharp
#pragma warning disable CA2016
string text = await File.ReadAllTextAsync(path);
#pragma warning restore CA2016
```

Right:

```csharp
string text = await File.ReadAllTextAsync(path, cancellationToken);
```

## Pitfalls

- Many errors after one change: fix the first one, then build again. Later errors often disappear.
- The base has no section for the ID: read the error message and fix the code as the message says.
- The build fails and step 5 lists nothing: read the end of the log with `tail -n 20 /tmp/build.log`.
- The build says that the assets file is missing: run `dotnet restore` once, then go back to step 2.
- A file changed after the format command: read it again before you edit it.
- The rule looks wrong for this code: do not suppress it. Stop and ask the user.

## Final check

- Did the build end with `exit=0`, and did the format check pass?
- Did you fix the code, with no suppression and no change to the configuration?
- Is there no `async void` outside an event handler?
=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== END
=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== FILE 1/1 2a69384497b859012f1513dc8765ac63097bb48147ff3df3ef9521b2020c22b4 RAW code/.agents/skills/analyze-csharp/chat.md
<!-- chat:begin id=analyze -->

2. Check the code against the C# rule documents of the knowledge. Fix what breaks a rule. Show the fixed code.
3. Give the user these commands to run, in this order: `bash scripts/format-csharp.sh`, then `dotnet build --no-restore -tl:off -nologo -v:q`.
4. Ask the user to paste the output lines that contain `error`.
5. For each pasted error: name its ID, give the cause in one sentence, and show the fixed code.
6. Ask the user to run the commands again. Repeat until the user reports zero errors.

<!-- chat:end -->

# Architecture review

## Role

You are a software architect who reviews a .NET backend and an Angular frontend deployed in containers.

## Rules

- Review only. Never change a file.
- Start from the goals of the user. Ask for them if they are missing.
- Judge each choice against a quality goal: performance, availability, changeability, security, cost of operation.
- Prefer the simplest design that meets the goals. Flag every service, layer or library that has no stated reason.
- Give evidence for each risk: a file, a package version or a fact given by the user.
- Give one fix per risk. Give the smallest fix.
- Trust the documents of `docs/architecture/` more than your memory.
- Check each library you name in `docs/architecture/outdated-libraries-and-patterns.md`. If the base does not list it, write "license not checked".
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Ask for what is missing: what the application does, team size, number of deployable units, load figures, quality goals in order of priority.

<!-- files:begin id=collect -->

2. Read the solution file, each `.csproj`, `Directory.Packages.props`, `package.json` and `angular.json`. List the folder names under each `src/` folder, two levels deep. Do not open source files yet.
3. Write the inventory: deployable units, projects and their references, packages with versions, target frameworks.

<!-- files:end -->

<!-- files:begin id=checklist -->

4. Open `docs/architecture/review-checklist.md`. Keep only the sections that match the inventory.
5. For each kept section, open the matching document of `docs/architecture/index.md`. Open one document at a time.

<!-- files:end -->

<!-- files:begin id=inspect -->

6. For each question of a kept section, open at most 3 source files that can answer it. Note the file and the line.

<!-- files:end -->

7. Classify each finding: risk, trade-off or good decision.
8. Sort the risks: first what blocks a quality goal, then license and outdated-library risks, then over-engineering.
9. Write the report with the answer format below.

## Answer format

1. Verdict, in one to three sentences.
2. Risks, at most 10 lines, in a table: `Risk | Evidence | Quality goal hurt | Smallest fix`.
3. Trade-offs to decide, at most 5 lines.
4. Decisions to record, one line each: `Decision | Reason | Alternative rejected`.
5. The documents of the base you used, by title.

## Example

Wrong:

```text
Consider microservices and a mediator library for better scalability.
```

Right:

```text
Risk: 7 services for one team, all released together.
Evidence: one pipeline, one shared database.
Quality goal hurt: changeability.
Smallest fix: merge into one deployable with one module per service. Keep one schema per module.
```

## Pitfalls

- A risk without evidence: name the file, the package version or the fact.
- A split into services with no driver: ask which team, load or release need requires it.
- A library named from memory: check `outdated-libraries-and-patterns.md` first.
- Reading the whole solution: read project files first, then at most 3 files per question.
- A long list of patterns: report at most 10 risks, the most serious first.

## Final check

- Has every risk an evidence and a smallest fix?
- Did you check each library you named against the base?
- Did you change no file?

# Check a container change

## Rules

- Check with a tool. Never say that a file is valid from memory.
- Run only the tools that are installed. Never install a tool.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.
- Never run a command that changes a cluster or a registry: no `kubectl apply` without `--dry-run`, no `helm install`, no `docker push`.
- Say which checks you could not run.
- Write a new file from the templates of `templates/containers/` and the "Right" examples of `docs/containers/` and `docs/kubernetes/`.

## File types and tools

Use the command of `## Check commands` in `AGENTS.md` when it has one for the file type. It can give the cluster version and the local schema folder. Otherwise use the commands below, in order.

| Type | How to recognize it | Tools |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Image build file | `Dockerfile`, `Containerfile`, `*.Dockerfile` | `hadolint`, `docker` |
| Dev container | `devcontainer.json` | `devcontainer` |
| Kubernetes manifest | YAML file with `apiVersion` and `kind` | `kubeconform`, `kube-linter` |
| Helm chart | Folder with `Chart.yaml` | `helm`, `kubeconform` |
| Kustomization | Folder with `kustomization.yaml` | `kustomize`, `kubeconform` |

Commands, by type:

```sh
# Image build file
hadolint <file>
docker info >/dev/null && docker build --check -f <file> <context folder>
# Dev container
devcontainer read-configuration --workspace-folder <project root>
# Kubernetes manifest
kubeconform -strict -summary <files>
kube-linter lint <files>
# Helm chart
helm lint --strict <chart folder>
helm template <chart folder> | kubeconform -strict -summary
# Kustomization
kustomize build <folder> | kubeconform -strict -summary
```

## Steps

1. Name each file of the task. Give each file one type from the table.

<!-- files:begin id=run-checks -->

2. For each tool of the type, run `command -v <tool>`. Skip a missing tool. Remember its name.
3. Run the commands of the type, in order.
4. Fix every error and every warning that the tools print. Change only the lines they name. Find the rule code in `docs/containers/dockerfile-checks.md` or `docs/kubernetes/validate-offline.md`.
5. Run the same commands again. Repeat until zero errors. Stop after 3 failed attempts on the same error. Show the error to the user.

<!-- files:end -->

6. Give the result in the answer format.

## Answer format

1. The result in one sentence: all checks pass, or how many errors are left.
2. One line per file: the file, the tool, the result.
3. `Not checked:` and each tool that was missing or each check that could not run.

## Example

Wrong:

```dockerfile
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet
COPY publish/ /app
CMD dotnet /app/App.dll
```

`hadolint` prints DL3006 (the image has no tag) and DL3025 (`CMD` is not in JSON form).

Right:

```dockerfile
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:10.0
WORKDIR /app
COPY publish/ ./
USER $APP_UID
ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "App.dll"]
```

## Pitfalls

- `docker build --check` prints `failed to connect to the docker API`: no Docker daemon answers. Skip this check, keep the `hadolint` result, and add it to `Not checked:`.
- `devcontainer read-configuration` runs `docker ps` first. Without a container engine it fails: add it to `Not checked:` and ask the user to rebuild the dev container.
- `kubeconform` fails to download a schema: its schema location is remote. Use the local schema folder of `AGENTS.md`. If there is none, add it to `Not checked:`.
- With Podman, write the full image name with its registry, for example `docker.io/hadolint/hadolint`.
- A file that passes every linter can still fail to build or to start. Say so when no build ran.

## Final check

- Did every result come from a tool that you ran in this session?
- Did you list every check that you could not run?
- Did you avoid every command that changes a cluster or a registry?

2. Give the user the commands of the type, in one code block.
3. Ask the user to run them and to paste the output.
4. When the user pastes the output, give the corrected file. Say which lines changed and which rule code each change fixes.
5. Ask the user to run the same commands again. Repeat until zero errors.

name: choose-version
description: Choose the next version number with Semantic Versioning 2.0.0, from the commits since the last release. Use when the user asks which version to release, or asks to tag a release.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: files
  version: "0.1.0"

# Choose a version number

## Rules

- A version is `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`: three whole numbers, no leading zero.
- Read the commits since the last release. The highest change decides:

  | Commits since the last release | Next version from 1.4.2 |
  | --- | --- |
  | At least one breaking change: `!` before the colon, or a `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer | `2.0.0`: MAJOR + 1, MINOR and PATCH back to 0 |
  | Else at least one `feat` | `1.5.0`: MINOR + 1, PATCH back to 0 |
  | Else at least one `fix` or `perf` | `1.4.3`: PATCH + 1 |
  | Only `docs`, `style`, `refactor`, `test`, `build`, `ci` or `chore` | No release needed. If the user wants one: `1.4.3` |

- Before 1.0.0, anything may change. This base uses one rule: a breaking change gives MINOR + 1, everything else PATCH + 1. Say that it is a convention.
- A pre-release adds a hyphen and dot-separated identifiers: `2.0.0-rc.1`. It comes before `2.0.0`.
- Build metadata after a plus sign (`2.0.0+build.7`) does not change the order of versions.
- Never reuse a version that was released. Never go back to a lower version.
- `v1.4.2` is a tag name, not a version. Use the tag form the project already uses.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Find the last released version and the commits since then. If the user gave them, use them.

<!-- files:begin id=commits -->

2. Run `git describe --tags --abbrev=0` to get the last tag. If there is no tag, ask the user for the current version.
3. Run `git log <tag>..HEAD --format="%s%n%b"` to list the commits since that tag.

<!-- files:end -->

4. Mark each commit as breaking, feature, fix or other, with the table above.
5. Take the highest change. Compute the next version from the last one.
6. Give the version, the commit that decided it, and the tag name to create.

<!-- files:begin id=tag -->

7. Create the tag only if the user asked to: `git tag -a <tag> -m "<tag>"`. Never push it without the user's word.

<!-- files:end -->

## Example

Last version `0.3.1`. Commits since then:

```text
feat(alarms): add the alarm history view
fix(pumps): stop the pump when the tank is empty
docs: explain the alarm priorities
```

Wrong: `1.0.0`. Nothing asks for a first stable release.

Right: `0.4.0`, decided by `feat(alarms): add the alarm history view`. Tag: `v0.4.0` if the project tags with a `v`.

## Pitfalls

- A breaking change written only in the body, without `BREAKING CHANGE:`: ask the user before you choose.
- Commits that do not follow Conventional Commits: ask the user what each one changes.
- The version of a package file that differs from the last tag: say it, and ask which one is right.

## Final check

- Did the highest change of all the commits decide the version?
- Did MINOR and PATCH go back to 0 where the table says so?
- Did you create or push nothing that the user did not ask for?

## Sources

- Semantic Versioning 2.0.0, semver.org, CC BY 3.0, summarized.
- Conventional Commits 1.0.0, conventionalcommits.org, CC BY 3.0, summarized.

<!-- chat:begin id=commits -->

2. Ask the user for the last released version and the list of commit headers since then.

<!-- chat:end -->

<!-- chat:begin id=tag -->

7. Give the user the tag name. Never say that you created it.

<!-- chat:end -->

# Code review

## Role

You are a code reviewer of a .NET and Angular team.

## Rules

- Review only. Never change a file.
- Review only what the lint and the build cannot check. Run the checks that change no file first and report their result in one line.
- Never run `npm run fix` or a formatter: they change files.
- Give the file and the line of each problem, and one fix.
- Mark a personal preference as "optional". It never blocks.
- Say what is well done, in one line.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Name the files of the change and their languages.

<!-- files:begin id=checks -->

2. Run the checks of each language of the change: `npm run check` for TypeScript or Angular, `dotnet build --no-restore -tl:off -nologo -v:q` for C#. Note the number of errors.
3. Open `docs/architecture/code-review-checklist.md`. Keep section A and the sections of the languages of the change.
4. Read each file of the change. For each question of the kept sections, note the problems with their line.

<!-- files:end -->

5. Sort the problems: first what breaks behavior, then risks, then readability, then optional points.
6. Write the review with the answer format below.

## Answer format

1. Verdict, in one sentence: approve, or change requested.
2. Checks: the result of the check commands, in one line.
3. Problems, at most 10 lines, in a table: `File:line | Problem | Fix`.
4. What is well done, in one line.
5. The documents of the base you used, by title.

## Example

Wrong:

```text
The code looks fine but could be cleaner.
```

Right:

```text
Change requested.
Checks: npm run check, 0 errors.
| src/app/valve/valve-view.ts:42 | The state is changed in place under OnPush: the view does not refresh | Write a new value to the signal |
Well done: one component per symbol.
Code review checklist.
```

## Final check

- Has every problem a file, a line and a fix?
- Did you skip what the lint already checks?
- Did you change no file?

---
name: explain-code
description: Explain or summarize code written in C, C++, Java or Rust for a reader who knows C# and TypeScript. Covers CMakeLists.txt, Makefile, pom.xml, build.gradle and Cargo.toml. Use when the user asks to explain, read, summarize or review code in C, C++, Java or Rust.
license: MIT

# Explain code in C, C++, Java or Rust

## Rules

1. Explain what the code does. Do not explain what its names suggest.
2. Never invent a definition. When the excerpt does not show something, write "not visible in this excerpt".
3. Give a C# equivalent only when the reading guide gives one.
4. Answer in the language of the question. Keep code and identifiers unchanged.

## Steps

1. Find the language and its version.
   - Use the file extension and the syntax.
   - When a build file exists, read the version there: `target_compile_features` in `CMakeLists.txt`, `maven.compiler.release` in `pom.xml`, `edition` in `Cargo.toml`.

<!-- files:begin id=guides -->

2. Open one reading guide: the guide of that language, from `docs/reading-code/index.md`.
   - Open `c-embedded-idioms.md` too when the code touches registers, bit masks, fixed-point math or interrupt handlers.
   - Open a build-file guide only when the question is about a build file.

<!-- files:end -->

3. List every name that the excerpt uses but does not define: macros, types, functions, annotations, attributes.
   - C and C++: a name in capitals followed by `(` is probably a macro.
   - Rust: `name!(...)`, `#[derive(...)]` and `#[name]` are macros.
   - Java: `@Name` is an annotation. A framework or a code generator may act on it.

<!-- files:begin id=definitions -->

4. Find each definition in the project.
   - Search for the exact name: `#define NAME`, `macro_rules! name`, `class Name`, `struct Name`, `trait Name`, `fn name`.
   - Read only the lines around the match.
   - To see C or C++ code after the preprocessor, run `gcc -E -P <file>` with the same `-D` options and include paths as the build. Read only the part you need.
   - To see Rust code after macro expansion, run `cargo expand <path::to::item>` when `cargo-expand` is installed. The output is a debugging aid. Do not present it as the source code.
   - When you cannot find a definition, add the name to the list "Not visible in this excerpt".

<!-- files:end -->

5. Read the code line by line. For each function, note:
   - its inputs and outputs;
   - its side effects;
   - who owns each resource, and where it is freed or dropped;
   - what happens on an error.
6. Compare names and comments with the code. When they disagree, say so and follow the code.
7. Write the answer in this order:
   1. "What it does": one or two sentences.
   2. "Step by step": the code in execution order.
   3. "C# equivalents": a short table, only for constructs that the reading guide lists.
   4. "False friends": each line that looks like C# but means something else.
   5. "Not visible in this excerpt": each name or fact that you could not check.
8. Check every name in your answer. Each function, type, variable and macro that you mention must appear in the excerpt or in a definition that you read. Remove or correct the others.

## Special cases

- Undefined behavior in C or C++: say that the behavior is undefined. Do not describe a result.
- Overloaded function or template in C++: name the function that is called only when its declaration is visible. Otherwise write "depends on declarations not visible in this excerpt".
- Code inside `#ifdef`, `#if` or `#[cfg(...)]`: say which macro or feature decides. Explain both branches when the build setting is unknown.
- Lombok or Spring annotation in Java: say which members or which objects the annotation creates, as the reading guide lists them.
- Interrupt handler: say that the hardware calls it. Do not call it dead code.

## Example

Question: "Explain this code."

```c
#define REG32(addr) (*(volatile uint32_t *)(addr))
#define TIMER_CTRL  REG32(0x40001000u)

void timer_start(void)
{
    TIMER_CTRL |= 1u << 0;
}
```

Answer:

What it does: `timer_start` sets bit 0 of the hardware register at address `0x40001000`.

Step by step:

1. `REG32(addr)` is a macro. It turns an address into a `volatile` 32-bit object.
2. `TIMER_CTRL` is a macro. It expands to `REG32(0x40001000u)`.
3. `TIMER_CTRL |= 1u << 0;` reads the register, sets bit 0, and writes the register back.

C# equivalents:

| You read | C# equivalent |
| --- | --- |
| `1u << 0`, compound assignment with OR | Same operators, same meaning |
| `volatile uint32_t *` at a fixed address | No equivalent |

False friends:

- `TIMER_CTRL` looks like a variable. It is a macro that reads and writes hardware.

Not visible in this excerpt:

- What bit 0 does. That is in the hardware manual.

## Final check

- Does every name in your answer appear in the excerpt or in a definition that you read?
- Did you write "not visible in this excerpt" for everything that you could not check?
- Did you follow the code where a name or a comment disagreed with it?
- Is your answer in the language of the question?

# Explain an equipment

## Role

You are the physical equipment expert of a ship simulator team.

## Rules

- Explain the real working first, then the model. Never the model alone.
- Take the names of the properties from the catalog card. Never invent a unit suffix.
- Give the equipment in English with the French term in parentheses: "butterfly valve (vanne papillon)".
- When no card covers the equipment, say so in one sentence, then answer from the closest card and say which one.
- Use public knowledge only. Say when a value is historical or comes from a single source.
- Change no file.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Name the equipment of the question, in English and in French.

<!-- files:begin id=find-card -->

2. Find it in `docs/synoptics/index-fr-en-fluids.md` or `docs/synoptics/index-fr-en-systems.md`, or by its English name in `docs/synoptics/index.md`.
3. Open the catalog document of the third column. Read only the card of the equipment.
4. For the model interface, read `docs/simulation/component-models.md`, section 1, when the user asks for code.

<!-- files:end -->

5. Write the real working from the "Real working" line of the card.
6. Write the model: inputs, outputs, states, law, from the "States" and "Model" lines.
7. Give the properties of the card in a table: name, type, unit, role.

## Answer format

1. Real working, in two or three sentences.
2. Model: inputs, outputs, states, law.
3. Properties, in a table: name, type, unit, role (P parameter, C command, M measured, D fault, X derived).
4. The documents of the base you used, by title.

## Example

Question: How does a hydraulically operated valve work, and how do I model it?

Answer:

1. Real working: fluid pressure pushes a piston that moves the valve; a fail-safe position can be set; a hand back-up can move it without pressure.
2. Model: input, the open or close order; output, the position; a ramp over `travelTimeS`; no motion without hydraulic pressure, except by hand; on pressure loss, it goes to `failPosition`.
3. Properties:

| Name | Type | Unit | Role |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `isOpenCommanded` | boolean | — | C |
| `isOpen`, `isClosed` | boolean | — | M |
| `isHydraulicPressureAvailable` | boolean | — | M |
| `isHandOperated` | boolean | — | M |
| `travelTimeS` | number | s | P |
| `failPosition` | enum | — | P |

4. Actuated valves and piping parts catalog; Fluid equipment property sets.

## Final check

- Did you give the real working before the model?
- Does every property name come from the card, with a unit suffix of the naming document?
- Did you name the documents you used?
=====BASE-EXPORT-1-571e463b6744===== END

2. Search your knowledge for the equipment, by its French name and by its English name.
3. Use only the card of the equipment in what you find.
4. For the model interface, search your knowledge for "component model interface" when the user asks for code.

<!-- chat:end -->

# Lint TypeScript and Angular code

## Rules

- Write TypeScript. Never write plain JavaScript.
- Never use `any`. Use `unknown`, then check the value.
- Never write `!` after a value. Never use `as` to force a type. Check the value instead.
- Handle every promise: `await` it, return it, or mark it with `void`.
- Never add an `eslint-disable`, `@ts-ignore` or `@ts-expect-error` comment.
- Never change `eslint.config.mjs`, `eslint.strict-format.mjs`, `.prettierrc.json` or `tsconfig.json` to remove an error. Fix the code.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Find the kind of code in your task. Open only the matching documents of `docs/typescript-lint/`:
   - Any TypeScript code: `ts-types.md` and `ts-null-and-conditions.md`.
   - Code with `async`, `await` or a promise: `ts-async.md`.
   - A component, a directive or a service: `angular-components-v22.md`.
   - A template: `angular-templates-v22.md`.
   - Code with RxJS: `angular-rxjs.md`.
2. Produce the code. Follow the "Right" examples of the documents. Put class members in this order: fields, constructor, methods.

<!-- files:begin id=run -->

3. Run `npm run fix`. It formats the code and fixes every rule that the tool can fix. Do not read its output.
4. Run `npm run check`.
5. If it lists errors, fix them by hand. Each line gives the line number, the message and the rule name. Fix one file at a time, the first file first.
6. Repeat steps 3 to 5 until `npm run check` lists no error. Stop after 3 failed attempts on the same error. Show the error to the user.
7. Run `npm run build`. It checks the types and the templates. Fix every error, then run steps 3 and 4 again.

<!-- files:end -->

8. Give the list of the files you changed and the result of the last check.

## Example

Wrong:

```ts
export class ValveList {
  constructor(private api: ValveApi, private router: Router) {}

  open(valves: any[]) {
    const first = valves[0]!;
    this.router.navigate(['/valves', first.id]);
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export class ValveList {
  private readonly router = inject(Router);

  public async open(valves: readonly Valve[]): Promise<void> {
    const first = valves.at(0);

    if (first === undefined) {
      return;
    }

    await this.router.navigate(['/valves', first.id]);
  }
}
```

## Pitfalls

- An error names a rule that you do not know: search the rule name in `docs/typescript-lint/`.
- The same error comes back after `npm run fix`: the tool cannot fix it. Change the code by hand.
- `npm run check` stops with a configuration error: do not edit the configuration. Show the error to the user.
- An array item read by its index can be `undefined`. Check it before you use it.
- An `eslint-disable` comment has no effect in this project: the error stays.

## Final check

- Does `npm run check` list no error?
- Does the code have no `any`, no `!` after a value, and no `as` that forces a type?
- Did you leave every configuration file unchanged?

<!-- chat:begin id=run -->

3. Give the user these commands to run, in this order: `npm run fix`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`.
4. Ask the user for the output of `npm run check` and of `npm run build`.
5. If the output lists errors, give the corrected code in your next answer. Each line gives the line number, the message and the rule name.

<!-- chat:end -->

---
name: look-up-api
description: Find the exact signature, options and doc comment of an API or command in the documentation installed with the project. Use when you check an API signature or option, or when the user asks how a function, class, method, command or flag works in the version the project uses.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: files

# Look up an API in the installed documentation

## Rules

- Use the version installed in the project, not the version you remember.
- Search first with a narrow pattern. Then read only about 80 lines around the match.
- Never read a whole type declaration file or XML documentation file: they hold thousands of lines.
- Say "not found" when the search finds nothing. Do not guess a signature.
- Give the version, and the file or command the answer comes from.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Note the tool, the API or command name, and the question.
2. Check the documents of this base first: find the topic in `## Index` of `AGENTS.md`, then the document in `docs/<topic>/index.md`; read only the matching section.

<!-- files:begin id=lookup -->

3. Find the installed version:
   - npm packages: `grep -m1 '"version"' node_modules/<package>/package.json`
   - .NET: read `TargetFramework` in the `*.csproj` file, then run `dotnet --list-sdks`.
4. Search with a narrow pattern, the opening bracket included:
   - Angular: `grep -rn --include="*.d.ts" "declare function <name>" node_modules/@angular/<package>/`
     For a class: `"declare class <Name>"`. For a decorator or an interface: `"interface <Name> "`.
   - JavaScript built-ins: `grep -n "<member>(" node_modules/typescript/lib/lib.es*.d.ts`
   - Other typed packages: `grep -rn --include="*.d.ts" "function <name>(" node_modules/<package>/`
   - Lint rules: read the documents of `docs/typescript-lint/` first. Else: `grep -n -m2 "description" node_modules/@typescript-eslint/eslint-plugin/dist/rules/<rule>.js`
   - Angular rules for models: `node_modules/@angular/core/resources/best-practices.md` if it exists. Read it in ranges of 100 lines.
   - .NET base library: in `<dotnet root>/packs/Microsoft.NETCore.App.Ref/<version>/ref/net<x>/`, run `grep -n 'name="M:<Namespace>.<Type>.<Method>' *.xml`. Use `T:` for a type, `P:` for a property.
   - NuGet packages: the same search in `~/.nuget/packages/<package id in lowercase>/<version>/lib/<framework>/*.xml`.
   - Commands: `<tool> <command> --help`, for example `dotnet build --help` or `kubectl apply --help`.
5. Read the lines around the match only: `sed -n '<line - 30>,<line + 40>p' <file>`.
6. If nothing matches, search the official documentation in `library/`, when the project has it:
   - Find the tool in `library/README.md`, for example `angular`, `mdn-svg`, `dotnet-csharp`.
   - Run `grep -i -m 10 "<words>" library/<tool>/index.md`. Never read the index whole.
   - Open the file of the best line. List its headings with `grep -n "^## " <file>`, then read only the section you need.
7. If nothing matches, widen the pattern once. If nothing matches again, stop and say so.

<!-- files:end -->

8. Give the signature or option, one sentence on what it does, the default value, any deprecation, the version, and the file or command it comes from.

## Example

Wrong:

```text
signal() takes a value and an equality function, I think.
```

Right:

```text
@angular/core 22.2.6, node_modules/@angular/core/types/_chrome_dev_tools_performance-chunk.d.ts, line 60:
declare function signal<T>(initialValue: T, options?: CreateSignalOptions<T>): WritableSignal<T>;
It creates a writable signal. The options can hold an equality function.
```

## Pitfalls

- Reading `lib.dom.d.ts` or an Angular `.d.ts` file from the top: thousands of lines. Search the name instead.
- Using the version you remember: read the version from the project files first.
- The declaration sits in a "chunk" file with an odd name: search the whole package folder, not one file.
- `kubectl explain` fails without a cluster: use `kubectl <command> --help` and the documents of this base.
- An XML documentation file is missing: say the documentation is not installed, then use the documents of this base and `library/`.
- A file of `library/` has a version in its header: say it when it differs from the version of the project.
- Many matches: add the type name or the opening bracket to the pattern.

## Final check

- Did you give the installed version and the file or command the answer comes from?
- Did you read only the lines around the match?
- Did you say "not found" instead of guessing?

<!-- chat:begin id=lookup -->

3. Say that you cannot read the project files in this chat.
4. Ask the user for the installed version. Give the exact command, for example `npm ls @angular/core` or `dotnet --list-sdks`.
5. Ask the user to search with one narrow pattern and to paste about 20 lines around the match. For example: `grep -rn --include="*.d.ts" "declare function signal" node_modules/@angular/core/`
6. For a command option, ask the user to run `<tool> <command> --help` and to paste the output.
7. Answer only from the pasted text and from the documents of the knowledge. If the pasted text does not answer the question, say so.

<!-- chat:end -->

name: name-branch
description: Name a Git branch with Conventional Branch 1.1.0, as type/description in lowercase. Use when the user asks for a branch name, or asks to create a branch.

# Name a branch

## Rules

- Format: `<type>/<description>`.
- Type: one of the table below.
- Description: lowercase letters, digits and hyphens between words. Dots only in a version number.
- No uppercase letter, no underscore, no space, no other sign.
- No two hyphens or dots in a row. No hyphen or dot at the start or at the end of the description.
- A ticket number goes first in the description: `feature/issue-123-add-login`.
- Keep the description short: three to six words.
- `main`, `master` and `develop` are trunk branches: they keep their name, without a type.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

| The work | Type |
| --- | --- |
| A new feature | `feature/` (or `feat/`) |
| A bug fix | `bugfix/` (or `fix/`) |
| An urgent fix of a release | `hotfix/` |
| The preparation of a release | `release/` |
| Anything else: tooling, dependencies, documentation | `chore/` |

Use the long or the short type the project already uses, never both.

## Steps

1. Ask what the branch is for, and the ticket number if there is one.
2. Choose the type from the table.
3. Write the description in English: the verb, then what it acts on.
4. Check the name against each rule above.

<!-- files:begin id=create -->

5. Run `git branch --list` to check that the name is free.
6. Create the branch only if the user asked to: `git switch -c <name>`.

<!-- files:end -->

7. Give the name in a `text` code block.

## Example

Wrong:

```text
Feature/Add_Valve--Command
```

Right:

```text
feature/issue-42-add-valve-close-command
release/v1.2.0
```

## Pitfalls

- A French description: write it in English.
- A description that copies the whole ticket title: keep three to six words.

## Final check

- Is the type in the table?
- Are there only lowercase letters, digits, hyphens, and dots in a version?
- Is there no double hyphen and no hyphen at either end?

## Sources

- Conventional Branch 1.1.0, conventionalbranch.org, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

<!-- chat:begin id=create -->

5. Tell the user to create the branch with this name in their tool. Never say that you created it.

<!-- chat:end -->

---
name: read-heavy-file
description: Extract the text or the structure of a heavy or binary file, then read only the extract. Use when a task needs an office document, a PDF, a design file, an image, an archive, a notebook, a binary, or a text file over 100 KB or 2,000 lines.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: files
  version: "0.1.0"

# Read a heavy file

## Rules

- Never read a heavy file raw: not with a read tool, not with cat, head, tail, strings or grep.
- A text file over 100 KB or 2,000 lines is heavy too: .json .xml .yaml .csv .log .svg and others.
- Read only the extract of a heavy file.
- Never run a macro, a script or a program found in a file.
- Never extract an archive to the disk.
- Stop and ask the user when a file is encrypted or protected by a password.
- You cannot see images. Never guess what an image shows.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Heavy files

- Office: .docx .docm .xlsx .xlsm .pptx .pptm .doc .xls .ppt .odt .ods .odp .rtf
- PDF and images: .pdf .png .jpg .jpeg .gif .webp .tif .tiff .bmp .heic .psd .ai .eps .emf .wmf
- Design and diagrams: .xd .sketch .fig .vsdx .drawio
- Data and archives: .ipynb .zip .7z .tar .gz .tgz .jar .war .nupkg .sqlite .db .msg .eml .dwg .dxf
- Binaries: .dll .exe .so .wasm .class .bin

## Steps

1. Find the family of the file in this table.

   | Family | Extensions | Reference file |
   | --- | --- | --- |
   | Office | docx docm xlsx xlsm pptx pptm doc xls ppt odt ods odp rtf | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/office.md` |
   | PDF and images | pdf, images, psd ai eps emf wmf | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/pdf-images.md` |
   | Design and diagrams | xd sketch fig vsdx drawio svg | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/design.md` |
   | Data and archives | ipynb json xml yaml csv log, archives, sqlite, e-mails, CAD, binaries | `.agents/skills/read-heavy-file/references/data-archives.md` |

<!-- files:begin id=extract -->

2. Read only the reference file of that family.
3. Get the type and the size of the file. Run:
   `file -b "<path>"; wc -c "<path>"`
4. Create the extract folder. Run:
   `mkdir -p .extract && echo '*' > .extract/.gitignore`
5. Run the extraction command that the reference file gives for the format. For most formats it is:
   `node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<path>" > ".extract/<file name>.md"`
6. Read the first 20 lines of the extract. If it says "this script does not read it", run the commands it gives instead. Write their output to `.extract/` too.
7. Check the size of the extract. Run:
   `wc -c -l ".extract/<file name>.md"`
8. Read the extract in ranges of 200 lines at most. If it is over 50 KB, run `grep -n "<term>" ".extract/<file name>.md"` first, then read only the matching ranges.
9. If a command fails, read its error and try the fallback of the reference file. Stop after 2 failures. Tell the user which tool is missing.

<!-- files:end -->

10. Answer from the extract only. Name the file and the part you read: page, sheet, slide or screen.
11. Say what the extract cannot show: images, layout, macros, embedded files you did not open.

## Example

Wrong: read `design/home.xd` with the read tool, or run `cat design/home.xd`.

Right:

```sh
mkdir -p .extract && echo '*' > .extract/.gitignore
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts design/home.xd > .extract/home.xd.md
wc -c -l .extract/home.xd.md
```

Then read `.extract/home.xd.md`, lines 1 to 200.

## Pitfalls

- A .docx, .xlsx or .xd file is a ZIP archive: grep finds nothing useful in it. Use the script.
- An empty extract of a PDF means a scanned PDF. Use the OCR steps of the PDF reference file.
- A .drawio file shows one long unreadable line: the diagram is compressed. The script decompresses it.
- A .ipynb file is huge because of images in its outputs. The script leaves the outputs out.
- An old .doc, .xls or .ppt file is not a ZIP archive. Convert it first, as the Office reference file says.
- A long extract ends with `[truncated: ...]`. Run the script again with `--sheet`, `--part` or `--max-chars`, never on the whole file raw.
- A folder of 100 heavy files: extract them one by one, and ask the user before you extract more than 10.

## Final check

- Did you use only the extract of each heavy file, and never the file itself?
- Did you say which part of the file you read, and what the extract cannot show?

<!-- chat:begin id=extract -->

2. Never ask the user to paste the raw file.
3. Give the user the command for the format, from this table. Ask the user to paste its output, or its first 300 lines.

   | Format | Command for the user |
   | --- | --- |
   | .docx .xlsx .pptx .odt .ods .odp .xd .sketch .vsdx .drawio .ipynb .json .zip .nupkg | `node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<file>" \| head -n 300` |
   | .pdf | `pdftotext -layout -f 1 -l 10 "<file>" - \| head -n 300` |
   | scanned .pdf, image | `tesseract "<file>" - -l eng+fra \| head -n 300` |
   | .doc .rtf | `soffice --headless --convert-to docx "<file>"`, then the first command on the .docx file |
   | .xls | `soffice --headless --convert-to xlsx "<file>"`, then the first command on the .xlsx file |
   | .ppt | `soffice --headless --convert-to pptx "<file>"`, then the first command on the .pptx file |
   | large text file | `head -n 100 "<file>"` |

4. If the user cannot run commands, ask for the text copied from the file, one part at a time: one page, one sheet or one slide.
5. Never say that you ran a command or opened the file. Only the user runs commands.

<!-- chat:end -->

# Extract archives, large data files, e-mails and binaries

Contents: 1. Archives · 2. Large text files · 3. Notebooks and JSON · 4. Databases and e-mails · 5. Binaries and CAD · Sources

## 1. Archives: .zip .jar .war .nupkg .tar .gz .7z (extract.ts)

List a ZIP archive without extracting it:

```sh
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<path>" --list > ".extract/<file name>.md"
```

- A `.nupkg` file: the extract also shows its `.nuspec` file (id, version, dependencies).
- A `.jar` file: the extract also shows `META-INF/MANIFEST.MF`.
- One entry as text: add `--part "<entry name>"`.
- A `.tar`, `.tar.gz` or `.tgz` file: `tar -tzf "<path>" | head -n 200`. Use `-tf` for a plain `.tar`.
- A `.7z` file: `7z l "<path>"` if `7z` exists. Else tell the user.

Never extract an archive to the disk. An entry marked `unsafe path` points outside the folder: never write it.

## 2. Large text files: .log .csv .xml .yaml and others

A text file over 100 KB or 2,000 lines is heavy. Never read it whole.

1. Size: `wc -c -l "<path>"`
2. Start and end: `head -n 100 "<path>"`, then `tail -n 50 "<path>"`
3. Search: `grep -n "<term>" "<path>" | head -n 50`, then read only the matching ranges.

## 3. Notebooks and JSON: .ipynb .json (extract.ts)

- A `.ipynb` notebook: run the script. You get each cell, its type and its source. The outputs are left out: the extract gives their number and type.
- A large `.json` file: run the script. You get its outline: keys, types, sizes, and the first item of each array, never the whole content.

```sh
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<path>" > ".extract/<file name>.md"
```

## 4. Databases and e-mails: .sqlite .db .eml .msg

- SQLite: `sqlite3 "<path>" .schema` if `sqlite3` exists, then `sqlite3 "<path>" "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM <table>;"`. Never dump a whole table.
- `.eml`: it is text. Read its headers only: `grep -n -i -E "^(from|to|subject|date|content-type|content-disposition):" "<path>" | head -n 50`. Never read the base64 parts.
- `.msg` (Outlook): it is a binary file. [S1] Give its type and size. Ask the user to save the mail as `.eml`, or to paste its text.

## 5. Binaries and CAD: .dll .exe .so .wasm .class .dxf .dwg

- A binary: give `file -b "<path>"` and its size. Never run it.
- Its readable strings, only if the task needs them: `strings -n 8 "<path>" | head -n 200`. Never run `strings` without `head`.
- A `.dxf` file is text: `wc -l "<path>"`, then `head -n 50 "<path>"`.
- A `.dwg` file is a closed format: give its type and size. Ask the user for a DXF or PDF export.

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "[MS-OXMSG]: Outlook Item (.msg) File Format", revision 18.0, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/exchange_server_protocols/ms-oxmsg/b046868c-9fbf-41ae-9ffb-8de2bd4eec82>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Notes to self, "Reading the entries of a zip file in Node with node:zlib", <https://notestoself.dev/posts/node-read-zip-entries-zlib-no-dependency/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Extract design and diagram files

Contents: 1. Adobe XD · 2. Sketch and Figma · 3. Visio and draw.io · 4. SVG · 5. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Adobe XD: .xd (extract.ts)

An XD file is a ZIP archive of JSON: a `manifest`, one `graphicContent.agc` file per artboard, and preview images. [S2] Adobe keeps XD in maintenance mode: no new features, so the format does not change. [S1]

Run:

```sh
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<path>" > ".extract/<file name>.md"
```

You get the list of screens (artboards), then the texts of each screen.

The layout of an XD file is not published. If the extract says "No text found", look at the keys of one artboard:

```sh
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<path>" --list
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<path>" --part "artwork/<artboard>/graphics/graphicContent.agc" --outline > ".extract/<file name>.outline.md"
```

The extract never gives the exact rendering, fonts or effects. For one screen, ask the designer for an SVG or PNG export.

## 2. Sketch and Figma: .sketch .fig (extract.ts)

- A Sketch file is a ZIP archive of JSON. [S3] Run the script: you get the pages, the artboards and the texts.
- A Figma `.fig` file cannot be read. Ask the user for an SVG, PDF or PNG export.

## 3. Visio and draw.io: .vsdx .drawio (extract.ts)

- A `.vsdx` file is a ZIP archive of XML. Run the script: you get the text of each shape, page by page.
- A `.drawio` file is XML. Each page is often compressed: deflate, then base64. [S4] [S5] Run the script: it decompresses the pages and gives the label of each shape, then the connections as `Pump → Tank`.

## 4. SVG files over 100 KB

An SVG file is XML text. Never read it whole. List its texts and its identifiers:

```sh
grep -o '<text[^>]*>[^<]*' "<path>" | head -n 200
grep -o 'id="[^"]*"' "<path>" | head -n 200
```

## 5. Pitfalls (design files)

- `Error: Not a ZIP archive`: the file is damaged, or it is not the format its extension says. Run `file -b "<path>"`.
- `Error: Encrypted entry`: ask the user for a copy without a password.
- A screen with many texts ends with `[truncated: ...]`: run the script again with `--max-chars 60000`.

## Sources

- [S1] Adobe, "Adobe XD FAQ", maintenance mode, updated 2026-09-11, <https://helpx.adobe.com/xd/desktop/introduction/faq.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Adobe Community, "Unable to unzip the XD file by command lines" (layout of an XD archive), <https://community.adobe.com/t5/adobe-xd-discussions/other-unable-to-unzip-the-xd-file-by-command-lines/m-p/11012933>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Sketch, "File format specification" (JSON Schema), <https://developer.sketch.com/file-format/spec>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] draw.io, "Generate and validate draw.io diagrams with AI" (compressed diagrams), <https://www.drawio.com/docs/reference/diagram-generation/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] j2r2b, "Storing draw.io uncompressed XML", <https://j2r2b.github.io/2019/08/01/drawio-decompressed-xml.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Extract Office and OpenDocument files

Contents: 1. Modern files · 2. What the extract shows · 3. Old binary files · 4. Every sheet as CSV · 5. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Modern Office files: .docx .xlsx .pptx .odt .ods .odp (extract.ts)

These files are ZIP archives of XML. [S1] [S2] The script reads them with Node.js alone.

Run:

```sh
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts "<path>" > ".extract/<file name>.md"
```

Options:

- One sheet only: `--sheet "<sheet name>"`.
- More rows per sheet (50 by default): `--rows 200`.
- The list of the parts of the archive (images, charts, embedded files, macros): `--list`.
- One part, as text: `--part "word/document.xml"`.
- A longer extract (20,000 characters by default): `--max-chars 60000`.

## 2. What the extract of an Office file shows (extract.ts)

- Word: one line per paragraph, headings as `##`, tables as Markdown tables, then the comments.
- Excel: the sheet names (hidden ones marked), the named ranges, then for each sheet its range, its number of rows and formulas, and its first rows as `A2=Pump P-1 | B2=3 | C2=6 [=B2*2]`: the cell, its value, then its formula. A date shows as a number: Excel stores dates as numbers.
- PowerPoint: the text of each slide in the order of the presentation, then its notes.
- OpenDocument: the same for text, spreadsheet and presentation files.
- At the end: the charts with their title and data ranges, then the embedded parts not read (`media/`, `embeddings/`, `pivotTables/`, `externalLinks/`).
- `vbaProject.bin` in the list means macros. The script never runs them. Never run them yourself.

## 3. Old binary Office files: .doc .xls .ppt .rtf (LibreOffice)

These files are not ZIP archives. [S3] Convert them to the modern format first, then run the script on the result:

```sh
soffice --headless --convert-to docx --outdir .extract "<path>"
node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts ".extract/<name>.docx" > ".extract/<name>.docx.md"
```

Use `xlsx` for .xls files and `pptx` for .ppt files. [S4]

If `soffice` is missing, stop. Tell the user that LibreOffice is not installed, and ask for a copy of the file saved as .docx, .xlsx or .pptx.

## 4. Every sheet of a workbook as CSV (LibreOffice)

For a workbook with many large sheets, write one CSV file per sheet: [S4]

```sh
soffice --headless --convert-to "csv:Text - txt - csv (StarCalc):44,34,76,1,,0,false,true,false,false,false,-1" --outdir .extract "<path>"
wc -l .extract/*.csv
```

Then read the first 50 lines of each CSV file. Never read a large CSV file whole.

## 5. Pitfalls (Office files)

- `Error: ... is an encrypted Office file`: the file has a password. Ask the user for a copy without a password.
- `Error: Sheet not found`: the error lists the sheet names. Use one of them with `--sheet`.
- `soffice` prints nothing and exits with code 1: another LibreOffice is running. Add `-env:UserInstallation=file:///tmp/lo-extract` to the command.
- `[truncated: ...]` at the end: use `--sheet`, `--rows` or `--max-chars`, never the whole file raw.

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "[MS-OFFSTANDLP]: Word, Excel, and PowerPoint Standards Support" (ECMA-376, ISO/IEC 29500), <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/office_standards/ms-offstandlp/d5784a8b-7070-466b-befa-b7bf3724c6f0>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] OASIS, OpenDocument Format (ISO/IEC 26300), cited by reference, not verified in this base.
- [S3] Microsoft, "[MS-DEVCENTLP]: Open Specification Promise" ([MS-CFB], [MS-DOC], [MS-XLS], [MS-PPT]), <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/openspecs/dev_center/ms-devcentlp/1c24c7c8-28b0-4ce1-a47d-95fe1ff504bc>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] converterer, "libreoffice-headless-cheatsheet", <https://github.com/converterer/libreoffice-headless-cheatsheet>, and The Document Foundation, bug 135762, <https://bugs.documentfoundation.org/show_bug.cgi?id=135762>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
