# Write a skill

## Rules

- One skill does one task. Two goals mean two skills.
- The `name` is the folder name: lowercase letters, digits and single hyphens, 64 characters at most.
- The `description` says what the skill does, then when to use it: "Use when ...". Use the words the user will type. 1024 characters at most.
- Keep the whole file at 150 lines or fewer.
- Write the steps as a numbered list. One action per step. Start each step with a verb.
- Write only what the model would get wrong without the skill.
- Give one default method. Do not list options.
- Add a `## Example` section: one wrong and one right result.
- Add a `## Pitfalls` section with the mistakes seen so far.
- End with `## Final check`: the rules the model must not miss, as questions.
- Wrap each step that reads or writes a file or runs a command between `<!-- files:begin -->` and `<!-- files:end -->`. The chat variant of the skill drops these steps.
- Write the other steps with neutral verbs: ask, produce, check, give.
- Never name a software. Never tell the model to search the web or to open a URL.

## Steps

1. Ask the user for: the task, one example input, the expected output, the mistakes seen so far.
2. Choose the `name`. Produce the `description`.
3. Produce the body with the template below.
4. Check the rules above one by one.

<!-- files:begin -->

5. Write the content to `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`.
6. Add one row to the table of the `## Skills` section of `AGENTS.md`: "| To <task> | load the skill `<name>` |".
7. Run `node scripts/check-base.ts`. Fix every finding. Repeat until zero findings.

<!-- files:end -->

8. Give three test prompts: two that must use the skill and one that must not.

## Template

```markdown
---
name: <skill-name>
description: <What it does>. Use when <trigger>.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: files
  version: "0.1.0"
---

# <Title>

## Rules

- <Rule. One sentence. Starts with a verb.>
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Ask <what the skill needs>.
2. Produce <the result>.
<!-- files:begin -->
3. Write <the result> to `<path>`.
4. Run `<check command>`. Fix every error.
<!-- files:end -->
5. Give <what the user gets>.

## Example

Wrong: <result that breaks a rule>

Right: <result that follows the rules>

## Pitfalls

- <Mistake seen>: <fix>.

## Final check

- <Most important rule, as a question.>
```

## Example

Wrong: a step that writes a file without the markers, and a description without a trigger.

```markdown
description: Commit messages.

1. Write the message to `.git/COMMIT_EDITMSG`.
```

Right:

```markdown
description: Write a commit message in the Conventional Commits format. Use when the user asks to commit or to write a commit message.

1. Produce the message.
<!-- files:begin -->
2. Write the message to `.git/COMMIT_EDITMSG`.
<!-- files:end -->
```

## Pitfalls

- A colon followed by a space inside the `description` breaks the YAML header: rewrite the sentence or put the value in double quotes.
- A `name` different from the folder name: some tools reject the skill.
- A description without "Use when": the model does not know when to load the skill.
- A skill without its trigger line in `AGENTS.md`: a small model does not load it by itself.
- A skill that explains what the model already knows: cut it.

## Final check

- Is the `name` equal to the folder name?
- Does the `description` contain "Use when"?
- Is every step that reads, writes or runs between the `files` markers?
- Is the file 150 lines or fewer?
- Is the trigger line in `AGENTS.md`?

---
name: write-tests
description: Write, fix or run unit and integration tests in C#, Angular or TypeScript with the runner and libraries the project already has, until they pass without being weakened. Use when the user asks for tests, when a test fails, or after you change code that has tests.
license: MIT
metadata:
  variant: files
  version: "0.1.0"
---

# Write and run tests

## Rules

- Never change, skip or delete a test to make it pass. Fix the code under test. Stop after 3 failed attempts and show the failure.
- Keep the test framework, the runner and the libraries the project has. Never add a test, assertion or mocking library without the user's agreement.
- Take each expected value from the request or the specification, never from the code under test.
- One behavior per test, named after it. Assert on the result; never print instead of asserting.
- Mock only the boundaries: network, clock, file system, database.
- Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Steps

1. Note the code to test and the behaviors the request asks for.

<!-- files:begin id=read -->

2. Find the runner of the project:
   - C#: read `global.json` (`"runner"`) and the test project (`xunit.v3`, `MSTest.Sdk`, `NUnit`, `TUnit`).
   - Angular or TypeScript: read the `test` target of `angular.json` and `package.json` (`vitest`, `karma`, `jsdom`, `happy-dom`).
3. Read one existing test of the project next to the code. Copy its style, its helpers and its assertion library.
4. Open the documents of the topic: `docs/dotnet-testing/index.md` or `docs/angular-testing/index.md`. Read the rules document, and `test-old-to-new.md` for the version of the project.

<!-- files:end -->

5. List the cases: the normal case, each limit, each error the request names.

<!-- files:begin id=run -->

6. Write the tests in the test file next to the code, or in the test project of the code.
7. Run only these tests, with the command of `run-tests.md` of the topic. Read the end of the output.
8. A test fails: decide whether the code or the test is wrong, from the request. Fix the code; fix a test only when it contradicts the request.
9. Run all the tests of the project once. Then run the check commands of `AGENTS.md`.

<!-- files:end -->

10. Give the tests written, the commands run and their result, and any behavior left untested.

## Example

Wrong:

```text
I updated the expected value in the test so that it matches the current output. All tests pass.
```

Right:

```text
Tests: ValveServiceTests.Close_sets_an_open_valve_to_closed, Close_refuses_an_unknown_valve.
Command: dotnet test --project tests/Valves.Tests.csproj --no-restore --filter-class "Valves.Tests.ValveServiceTests". Exit 0.
Close() left a closed valve unchanged; the request says it must raise an error. I fixed ValveService.Close().
```

## Pitfalls

- A command of another runner: `--logger trx` fails in MTP mode, `--browsers=ChromeHeadless` is Karma only. Read the runner first.
- `fakeAsync`, `detectChanges()` or `UseInMemoryDatabase` from memory: check `test-old-to-new.md` of the topic.
- A geometry (`getBBox`, `getScreenCTM`) asserted in jsdom or happy-dom: read `docs/angular-testing/svg-class-tests.md`.
- `ng test` without `--no-watch` never ends.

## Final check

- Did you leave every test as strong as the request asks, and fix the code instead of a test?
- Did you add no library the project did not have?
- Did you run your tests, then all the tests, and give their result?

<!-- chat:begin id=read -->

2. Ask the user for the runner of the project: the `"runner"` line of `global.json` and the test package of the test project for C#, or the `test` target of `angular.json` for Angular. Ask for one existing test of the project.
3. Search the knowledge for the rules and the old-to-new table of the stack.

<!-- chat:end -->

<!-- chat:begin id=run -->

6. Show the tests as code, with the file to put them in.
7. Give the user the command that runs only these tests, from the knowledge, and ask for the end of its output.
8. When the user pastes a failure, decide from the request whether the code or the test is wrong. Show the fix of the code; change a test only when it contradicts the request.
9. Give the command that runs all the tests.

<!-- chat:end -->

# AGENTS.md

## Rules

1. Answer in the language of the user's question. Never mix two languages in one answer.
2. Read this base in English, whatever the language of the question. Write code, comments, file content and commit messages in English.
3. If `AGENTS.project.md` exists at the project root, read it before your first task. It gives the rules of this project: follow them where they differ from this file. Never change `AGENTS.md` for a project rule: write it in `AGENTS.project.md`.
4. Never search the web. Never open a URL. Links in documents only cite sources.
5. Find the topic of your task in `## Index`. Open its index file, then only the documents your task needs. Never read the whole `docs/` folder. Trust these documents more than your memory. If they do not answer, search the official documentation: `grep -i -m 10 "<words>" library/<tool>/index.md` (tools in `library/README.md`), then read one section of one file. Never read a whole index or folder of `library/`.
6. Never read files that end with `.fr.md`. They are summaries for people. Open one only when a skill tells you to.
7. Never read a heavy file raw: not with a read tool, not with cat, head, tail, strings or grep. Load the skill `read-heavy-file` first, then read only the extract in `.extract/`.
   Heavy files: .docx .docm .xlsx .xlsm .pptx .pptm .doc .xls .ppt .odt .ods .odp .rtf .pdf .png .jpg .jpeg .gif .webp .tif .tiff .bmp .heic .psd .ai .eps .emf .wmf .xd .sketch .fig .vsdx .drawio .ipynb .zip .7z .tar .gz .tgz .jar .war .nupkg .sqlite .db .msg .eml .dwg .dxf .dll .exe .so .wasm .class .bin
8. A text file over 100 KB or 2,000 lines is heavy too. Before you read a log, data or export file you do not know, check its size with `wc -c -l "<path>"`.
9. Write TypeScript, never plain JavaScript. Configuration files of tools are the only exception.
10. Before you write Angular or TypeScript code, read the versions of `@angular/core` and `typescript` in `package.json`. Write only what the documents of the topics `angular` and `typescript` allow at these versions.
    Before you write C# code, read `TargetFramework` in the `*.csproj` file: it gives the C# version, as `docs/dotnet/csharp-changes.md` says. Never add a package of `docs/dotnet/paid-packages.md` without the user's agreement.
    Before you draw or code a synoptic symbol, or name its properties, read the three documents of the topic `synoptics`.
11. When a task matches a line of `## Skills`, load that skill before you start.
12. After you change code, run its check commands below, in the dev container, from the project root. Fix every error. Repeat until zero errors.
13. After you change a file of this base, run `node scripts/check-base.ts`. Fix every finding. Repeat until zero findings.
14. Stop after 3 failed attempts on the same error. Show the error to the user.

## Check commands

| Code changed | Commands, in this order |
| --- | --- |
| TypeScript or Angular | `npm run fix`, `npm run check`, `npm run build` |
| C# | `bash scripts/format-csharp.sh`, `dotnet build --no-restore -tl:off -nologo -v:q` |
| Dockerfile, dev container or Kubernetes | The commands of the skill `check-container-change` |
| Tests | Angular: `npx ng test --no-watch --no-progress --include <spec file>`; C#: `dotnet test` as `docs/dotnet-testing/run-tests.md` says. Your tests first, then all |

## Skills

| Task | Skill |
| --- | --- |
| To write or change TypeScript or Angular code | load the skill `lint-typescript` |
| To write or change C# code, or to fix a build error | load the skill `analyze-csharp` |
| To write, fix or run tests in C#, Angular or TypeScript | load the skill `write-tests` |
| To write or check a Dockerfile, a devcontainer.json, a Kubernetes manifest, a Helm chart or a kustomization | load the skill `check-container-change` |

| To explain how a ship equipment works, how to model it or name its properties | load the skill `explain-equipment` |
| To review code or a diff; to review an architecture, an API contract or a library choice | load the skill `code-review` for code, `architecture-review` for a design |
| To read a heavy file, or a folder of them | load the skill `read-heavy-file` |
| To write a commit message, or to commit | load the skill `write-commit-message` |
| To choose a version number or tag a release; to name or create a branch | load the skill `choose-version` for a version, `name-branch` for a branch |
| To write or shorten `AGENTS.md` | load the skill `write-agents-md` |
| To write a skill or an agent | load the skill `write-skill`, or `write-agent` for an agent |
| To add or update a document of the base | load the skill `write-base-document` |
| To write the system prompt of a chat model | load the skill `write-chat-model` |

## Index

One line per topic. Each line points to the index of the topic: one line per document, with its summary.
Open the index of your topic, then only the documents your task needs.

- [angular](docs/angular/index.md): What changed in Angular 17 to 22, and the old patterns to replace.
- [angular-testing](docs/angular-testing/index.md): Write and run Angular and TypeScript tests with the runner of the project, and test SVG classes.
- [architecture](docs/architecture/index.md): Architecture of .NET and Angular applications: decomposition, API contract, real-time push, messaging, patterns, outdated choices.
- [containers](docs/containers/index.md): Dockerfile rules, .NET and Angular images, Podman, dev containers and container problems.
- [csharp-analysis](docs/csharp-analysis/index.md): C# analysis rules that fail the build, with wrong and right examples.
- [dotnet](docs/dotnet/index.md): What changed in C#, .NET, ASP.NET Core and EF Core 6 to 11, and paid packages.
- [dotnet-testing](docs/dotnet-testing/index.md): Write and run C# tests that check something, at the runner and version of the project.
- [kubernetes](docs/kubernetes/index.md): Manifests, API versions, probes, resources, rollout, validation without a cluster and debugging.
- [maintenance](docs/maintenance/index.md): Update the documents of a tool to a new version.
- [reading-code](docs/reading-code/index.md): Read C, C++, Java and Rust as a C# developer, with embedded idioms and build files.
- [simulation](docs/simulation/index.md): Training simulator principles, time and scheduler, architecture, training functions, model interface and tests, HLA, FMI, deployment.
- [synoptics](docs/synoptics/index.md): Synoptic display rules, property names, symbol component, equipment catalogs with real working and model, French to English names.
- [typescript](docs/typescript/index.md): What changed in TypeScript 5.0 to 7.0, RxJS 7 and Node.js 22 to 26.
- [typescript-lint](docs/typescript-lint/index.md): TypeScript and Angular lint rules the tool does not fix, with wrong and right examples.

## Final check

- Is your answer in the language of the question, with no mix of two languages?
- Did you open only the documents of your topic, and no `.fr.md` file?
- Did you read heavy files only through their extract in `.extract/`?
- Did the check commands of the code you changed end with zero errors?
- Did you stay away from the web and from every URL?

# La base documentaire, en bref

Ce fichier est pour les humains de l'équipe. L'IA ne le lit pas : tout ce qu'elle lit est en
anglais.

## À quoi sert la base

- Elle aide une IA de code à travailler mieux sur un projet : des règles, des skills et des
  documents de référence qu'elle lit au bon moment.
- On la copie à la racine d'un projet. L'assistant de code lit alors `AGENTS.md` dès qu'on ouvre le
  projet, sans autre consigne.
- L'IA répond dans la langue de la question : en français si on lui écrit en français, en anglais
  si on lui écrit en anglais. Elle ne mélange jamais les deux.

## Ce que contient chaque dossier

| Chemin | Rôle |
| --- | --- |
| `AGENTS.md` | Le fichier d'entrée, chargé à chaque session : les règles vitales, une ligne par skill pour dire quand le charger, et la carte des sujets (une ligne par sujet). |
| `.agents/skills/<nom>/SKILL.md` | Un skill par dossier : une procédure pas à pas que l'IA charge pour une tâche précise. |
| `.agents/skills/<nom>/references/` | Les fiches détaillées d'un skill, une par cas : l'IA n'ouvre que celle dont elle a besoin. |
| `.agents/skills/<nom>/scripts/` | Les scripts d'un skill, en TypeScript, lancés par `node` sans paquet à installer. |
| `.agents/skills/<nom>/chat.md` | Le texte qui remplace, dans la version pour le chat, les étapes où l'IA lance une commande. |
| `.extract/` | Créé dans le projet par le skill `read-heavy-file` : les extraits des fichiers lourds. Git l'ignore. |
| `docs/template.md` | Le patron d'un document de la base : en-tête, sections, exemples faux et justes, sources. |
| `docs/<sujet>/` | Les documents de référence d'un sujet, un document par question. |
| `docs/<sujet>/index.md` | L'index du sujet, lu par l'IA : une ligne par document, avec son résumé. L'IA lit la carte d'`AGENTS.md`, puis l'index du sujet, puis seulement les documents utiles. |
| `docs/<sujet>/README.fr.md` | Le résumé en français du sujet, pour l'équipe : chaque fichier en une ligne. |
| `templates/<langage>/` | Des fichiers modèles, à copier et adapter. `templates/typescript-angular/` : la configuration ESLint, Prettier et `tsconfig` stricte d'un projet Angular 22, et les scripts `fix` et `check` à fusionner dans `package.json`. `templates/containers/` : `Dockerfile` .NET et Angular, dev container, Deployment et Service, réglages de hadolint. |
| `scripts/check-base.ts` | Le contrôle de la base, livré avec elle. |

## Les skills

| Skill | Ce qu'il fait |
| --- | --- |
| `analyze-csharp` | Écrit du C# conforme à l'analyse, puis lance le formatage et `dotnet build` jusqu'à zéro erreur. |
| `architecture-review` | Un agent qui revoit l'architecture d'une application .NET et Angular : il part des objectifs de qualité, liste les risques avec leur preuve et la plus petite correction, et ne change aucun fichier. |
| `code-review` | Un agent qui revoit une modification de code : ce que le lint ne voit pas, avec le fichier, la ligne et la correction, sans changer de fichier. |
| `explain-code` | Explique un extrait de C, C++, Java ou Rust, ou un fichier de build, à un développeur C# : ce que fait le code, pas à pas, les équivalents C#, les faux amis et ce que l'extrait ne permet pas de savoir. |
| `explain-equipment` | Un agent écrit comme un skill : il explique un équipement du bord par son fonctionnement réel, puis par son modèle, avec les noms de propriétés du catalogue. |
| `check-container-change` | Contrôle un `Dockerfile`, un `devcontainer.json`, un manifeste Kubernetes, un chart Helm ou une surcouche Kustomize avec l'outil de son type (hadolint, kubeconform, KubeLinter, `helm lint`), et dit ce qui n'a pas pu être contrôlé. |
| `look-up-api` | Cherche la signature exacte d'une API dans la doc installée avec le projet (types `.d.ts`, XML de .NET, `--help`), par recherche ciblée, sans lire de gros fichier. |
| `lint-typescript` | Écrit du TypeScript et de l'Angular conformes au lint, puis lance `npm run fix`, `npm run check` et `npm run build` jusqu'à zéro erreur. |
| `read-heavy-file` | Fait lire un fichier lourd par son extrait, jamais tel quel. |
| `write-commit-message` | Écrit un message de commit au format Conventional Commits, qui passe commitlint. |
| `choose-version` | Choisit le prochain numéro de version (SemVer) d'après les commits depuis la dernière version. |
| `name-branch` | Nomme une branche au format Conventional Branch (`feature/…`, `bugfix/…`). |
| `write-tests` | Écrit et lance des tests C#, Angular ou TypeScript avec le lanceur et les bibliothèques du projet ; corrige le code, jamais le test, pour qu'ils passent. |

## Les skills de rédaction

| Skill | Ce qu'il fait |
| --- | --- |
| `write-agents-md` | Écrit ou raccourcit `AGENTS.md`. |
| `write-skill` | Écrit un skill au format `SKILL.md`. |
| `write-agent` | Écrit un agent : un skill avec un rôle et un format de réponse. |
| `write-base-document` | Écrit un document de la base à partir de sources fournies, sa ligne d'index, et met à jour le résumé français du sujet. |
| `write-chat-model` | Écrit la consigne de base d'un modèle de chat : pour un sujet, avec sa base documentaire, ou pour une seule tâche (assistant restreint, qui refuse le reste). |

## Les sujets

| Dossier | Ce qu'il contient |
| --- | --- |
| `docs/angular/` | Ce qui a changé dans Angular de la 17 à la 22, pour écrire du code à la version du projet. |
| `docs/typescript/` | Ce qui a changé dans TypeScript (5.0 à 7.0), RxJS 7 et Node.js (22 à 26). |
| `docs/dotnet/` | Ce qui a changé dans C#, .NET, ASP.NET Core et EF Core de la 6 à la 11, et les bibliothèques devenues payantes. |
| `docs/containers/` | Les règles d'un `Dockerfile`, les images .NET et Angular, Podman, les dev containers et les problèmes de conteneur. |
| `docs/kubernetes/` | Les manifestes Kubernetes, les `apiVersion`, les sondes, les ressources et le temps réel, le déploiement, le contrôle sans cluster et le dépannage d'un Pod. |
| `docs/architecture/` | L'architecture d'une application .NET et Angular : découpage, contrat d'API, temps réel, messagerie, patrons .NET et Angular, bibliothèques et patrons dépassés, et les deux listes de revue. |
| `docs/reading-code/` | Lire du C, du C++, du Java et du Rust quand on connaît C# : un guide par langage, les idiomes de l'embarqué et les fichiers de build. |
| `docs/simulation/` | Les principes d'un simulateur d'entraînement, le temps et l'ordonnanceur, l'architecture, les fonctions d'entraînement (gel, instantané, rejeu, pannes), l'interface et les tests d'un modèle, HLA, DIS, DDS, FMI, le déploiement temps réel et la diffusion vers les synoptiques. |
| `docs/synoptics/` | Les règles pour représenter un équipement sur un synoptique, la convention de nommage des propriétés des symboles, la structure d'un composant symbole (gabarit `templates/typescript-angular/synoptic-symbol.ts`), et le catalogue des équipements de fluides avec son index français → anglais. |
| `docs/maintenance/` | La procédure pour mettre à jour les documents d'un outil qui change de version. |
| `docs/csharp-analysis/` | Les règles d'analyse C# que le build fait échouer, chacune avec un exemple faux et un juste. |
| `docs/typescript-lint/` | Les règles de lint TypeScript et Angular que l'outil ne corrige pas seul, chacune avec un exemple faux et un juste. |
| `docs/dotnet-testing/` | Écrire et lancer des tests C# : les deux modes de `dotnet test`, ce qui a changé (xUnit v3, MSTest 4, NUnit 5, paquets payants), les règles d'un test qui vérifie, les tests d'intégration. |
| `docs/angular-testing/` | Écrire et lancer des tests Angular : `ng test` avec Vitest ou Karma, ce qui a changé, le `TestBed` sans zone, et les tests des classes qui animent du SVG. |

## Écrire à la version du projet

- Avant d'écrire du code Angular, l'IA lit la version de `@angular/core` et de `typescript` dans
  `package.json`, et n'applique que les nouveautés de ces versions ou d'avant.
- `node scripts/check-versions.ts <dossier du projet>` liste les documents de la base en retard sur
  les versions d'un projet ; `docs/maintenance/update-a-tool.md` dit comment les mettre à jour.

## Lint TypeScript et Angular

- Copier dans le projet les fichiers de `templates/typescript-angular/`, fusionner
  `package.fragment.json` dans `package.json`, puis `npm install`.
- `npm run fix` corrige tout ce que l'outil sait corriger ; `npm run check` liste ce qui reste ;
  `npm run build` vérifie les types et les gabarits.
- Le formatage strict (ordre des imports, accolades, lignes vides) est actif par défaut ;
  `STRICT_FORMAT=0 npm run check` le coupe.
- Un commentaire `eslint-disable` n'a aucun effet : il faut corriger le code.

## Analyse C# et .NET

- Copier à la racine du projet .NET `Directory.Build.props` et `.editorconfig` de
  `templates/csharp/`, et `format-csharp.sh` dans son dossier `scripts/`.
- `bash scripts/format-csharp.sh` formate ; `dotnet build` lance l'analyse : chaque avertissement
  devient une erreur.
- La ligne `<StrictFormatting>true</StrictFormatting>` de `Directory.Build.props` passe au
  formatage strict avec CSharpier (à installer une fois : `dotnet tool install csharpier`).

## Conteneurs et dev container

- L'assistant de code tourne dans le dev container : il est installé dans son image, avec les outils
  de contrôle. Les commandes d'`AGENTS.md` sont donc celles d'un terminal ordinaire.
- Copier `templates/containers/devcontainer/` en `.devcontainer/` à la racine du projet ; mettre une
  fois dans `tools/` les binaires Linux de hadolint, kubeconform, kube-linter et helm, et dans
  `kubernetes-schemas/` les schémas JSON de la version du cluster ; donner le paquet npm de
  l'assistant dans `ASSISTANT_PACKAGE`.
- L'image installe aussi Poppler (`pdftotext`) et Tesseract (OCR en anglais et en français) pour
  lire les PDF et les images. Si le poste ne peut pas installer de paquets `apt`, mettre
  `EXTRACT_TOOLS` à `"false"` dans `devcontainer.json`.
- Ce qu'utilise le cluster de l'équipe (version, Ingress ou Gateway API, Helm ou Kustomize, dossier
  des schémas) s'écrit dans l'`AGENTS.md` du projet, pas dans la base.

## Lire les fichiers lourds

- L'IA ne lit jamais tel quel un fichier lourd ou binaire : Word, Excel, PowerPoint, OpenDocument,
  PDF, images, Adobe XD, Sketch, Visio, draw.io, notebooks, archives, e-mails, binaires. La liste
  complète est dans `AGENTS.md`.
- Un fichier texte de plus de 100 Ko ou de plus de 2 000 lignes compte aussi comme lourd.
- Le skill `read-heavy-file` lance d'abord une commande d'extraction, qui écrit un texte court dans
  `.extract/`, puis l'IA ne lit que ce texte, par morceaux de 200 lignes au plus.
- Le script `extract.ts` lit sans rien installer Word, Excel (feuilles, valeurs, formules),
  PowerPoint (diapositives et notes), OpenDocument, Adobe XD (écrans et textes), Sketch, Visio,
  draw.io, les notebooks, la structure d'un gros JSON et la liste d'une archive.
- Pour un PDF, une image ou un vieux fichier Office (`.doc`, `.xls`, `.ppt`), il donne la commande
  à lancer à la place : `pdftotext`, `tesseract` ou `soffice`, si ces outils sont installés.
- Sécurité : le script n'exécute rien de ce qu'il lit, n'écrit rien sur le disque depuis une
  archive, refuse les fichiers chiffrés et limite la taille de ce qu'il décompresse.
- Le script s'essaie à la main sur un fichier :
  `node .agents/skills/read-heavy-file/scripts/extract.ts mon-fichier.xlsx`.

## Vérifier la base

- La commande `node scripts/check-base.ts` contrôle toute la base : taille de `AGENTS.md`, format
  des skills et des documents, aucune demande d'aller sur le Web, rien en français pour l'IA.
- Elle demande Node 22.18 ou plus, sans paquet à installer.
- L'IA la lance d'elle-même après chaque changement de la base, et corrige jusqu'à zéro erreur.

## Amener la base sur un poste

- Dans le dépôt de la base, `node scripts/export.ts` écrit dans `export/` le fichier `import.ts`
  et quelques fichiers texte `base-1.txt`, `base-2.txt`… de 500 Ko au plus chacun (`--max-kb`
  change la limite).
- Sur le poste, on colle `import.ts` puis chaque partie dans un fichier du même nom, et on lance
  `node import.ts`. Le script vérifie chaque partie, dit laquelle a été abîmée par le collage, et
  n'écrit rien tant qu'elles ne sont pas toutes justes.
- `node import.ts --project` n'écrit que ce qu'il faut à la racine d'un projet : `AGENTS.md`,
  `.agents/`, `docs/`, `templates/`, les scripts de contrôle et les adaptateurs d'OpenCode.
- `--opencode 2` (ou `1` pour OpenCode 1.x) écrit en plus `opencode.json` à la racine : la lecture
  directe des fichiers lourds y est refusée, et les trois agents y sont déclarés.
- Les règles propres à un projet (version du cluster, conventions, commandes) s'écrivent dans
  `AGENTS.project.md` à sa racine, à partir de `templates/AGENTS.project.md` : 40 lignes au plus,
  en anglais. L'IA le lit avant sa première tâche ; une mise à jour de la base ne le touche jamais.
- `node scripts/export.ts --trial` ajoute à l'export le matériel des essais sur le poste :
  le dossier `trials/` (guides d'essai), les projets exemples et le jeu d'évaluation. Le dossier
  `export/` du dépôt contient l'export de la dernière livraison.
- L'export produit aussi la version pour le chat : chaque skill sans ses étapes qui lancent des
  commandes, et les documents de chaque sujet, sans les résumés en français.

## La doc officielle (`library/`)

- `library/` contient la doc officielle complète des outils, en Markdown simple, à une version
  notée : TypeScript, Angular, ESLint et ses greffons, RxJS, MDN sur SVG, `DOMMatrix` et Web
  Animations, Dev Containers, Node.js, C#, .NET, ASP.NET Core, EF Core, Kubernetes, Docker, Podman
  et le livre de Rust. Environ 90 Mo, en fichiers de 16 Ko au plus.
- Chaque outil a son index, `library/<outil>/index.md`, une ligne par fichier. L'index est long :
  on y cherche avec grep, on ne le lit jamais en entier.
- Chaque fichier garde sa source, son commit et sa licence dans son en-tête ; la licence MIT de la
  base ne s'applique pas à ce dossier (`THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`).
- `node scripts/fetch-library.ts` la reconstruit depuis les dépôts de doc (`library/sources.json`),
  `node scripts/check-library.ts` la contrôle.
- L'IA n'y va qu'en second : si les documents de la base ne répondent pas, elle cherche dans l'index
  d'un outil avec grep, puis lit une seule section d'un seul fichier. Le serveur de docs cherche
  aussi dedans, après les documents de la base.
- Sur le poste : `node scripts/export.ts --library core` ajoute à l'export le jeu de base
  (TypeScript, Angular, règles ESLint, RxJS, MDN sur SVG, Dev Containers) en parties compressées
  `library-<n>.txt`, qu'on colle comme les autres ; `all` ou une liste d'outils en emporte plus.
- Dans le chat : `OPEN_WEBUI_LIBRARY=core node adapters/open-webui/push.ts` crée une base
  documentaire par outil et la donne au modèle de son sujet.
- `node scripts/check-versions.ts <projet>` dit aussi quel outil de `library/` est en retard sur le
  projet.

## Dans le dépôt de la base, en plus

- `chat/` : le profil pour une interface de chat sans accès aux fichiers. Les consignes de base des
  modèles de chat sont dans `chat/models/` ; le reste est produit par l'export.
- `trials/README.md` : la séance d'essais sur le poste, dans l'ordre, avec les faits à relever d'abord
  (versions d'OpenCode et d'Open WebUI, fenêtre de contexte, appel d'outils).
- `research/` : les rapports de recherche d'où viennent les documents. Son `.gitignore` les garde
  hors de Git : ils ne sont jamais publiés.
- `chat/models/commit-assistant.md` : un assistant de commits pour les personnes qui ne sont pas
  développeurs. Il propose le message de commit, explique le type choisi, et refuse tout autre
  sujet en une phrase.
- `chat/models/` contient aussi quatre modèles par sujet : TypeScript et Angular, C# et .NET,
  conteneurs et Kubernetes, simulation et naval. Chacun cherche d'abord dans sa base documentaire
  et cite les documents utilisés sur une ligne `Sources:`.
- `adapters/` : ce qu'un logiciel précis exige en plus. Facultatif : la base marche sans.
- `adapters/opencode/v1/` et `v2/` : la configuration d'OpenCode pour ses versions 1.x et 2.x.
  Elle refuse la lecture directe des fichiers lourds et déclare les agents `architecture-review`,
  `code-review` et `explain-equipment`, qui ne modifient aucun fichier.
- `adapters/mcp/docs-server.ts` : un serveur de documentation en un seul fichier, sans paquet, à
  l'essai. Il cherche dans les index (`search_docs`) et rend une seule section d'un document
  (`read_doc`). Il est coupé par défaut ; on ne le garde que s'il fait au moins aussi bien que les
  fichiers seuls, sans plus de tokens.
- `scripts/check-public.ts` : avant de rendre le dépôt public, il vérifie qu'il ne contient que la
  base (ni `spec/`, ni rapport de recherche) et aucun terme d'une liste privée, dans les fichiers
  comme dans l'historique ; la liste reste hors du dépôt.
- `evaluation/` : le jeu de tâches et le protocole pour mesurer si l'IA réussit mieux avec la base
  que sans, sans plus de tokens ; `scripts/evaluation-summary.ts` en fait le bilan.
- `adapters/open-webui/push.ts` : sur le poste, après l'import, il crée ou met à jour dans Open WebUI
  les bases documentaires, les skills et les cinq modèles, lisibles par le groupe de l'équipe, sans
  aucun clic. L'adresse, le jeton, le modèle de base et le groupe se lisent dans l'environnement ;
  `--dry-run` montre ce qu'il ferait sans rien envoyer.

Comment l'IA écrit et lance des tests Angular qui vérifient vraiment, avec le lanceur du projet, et
comment elle teste les classes TypeScript qui animent les symboles des synoptiques.

# Run Angular tests with ng test

Contents: 1. Read the runner first · 2. Commands · 3. Configuration · 4. Lint rules for tests · Sources

## 1. Read the runner first (angular.json)

| Runner | State | Builder |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Vitest | Default and stable since Angular 21 | `@angular/build:unit-test` |
| Karma and Jasmine | Still supported; Karma deprecated since 2023 | `@angular/build:karma` |
| Jest, Web Test Runner | Deprecated in 21, builders removed in 22 | — |

[S42] [S44] [S46]

1. Read the `test` target of `angular.json`: its `builder`, and its `runner` option (`vitest` by default, or `karma`).
2. Read `package.json`: `vitest`, `jsdom` or `happy-dom`, `@vitest/browser-playwright`. Angular 22.0 asks for Vitest `^4.0.8`. [S47]
3. Keep the runner of the project. Never add or switch a test runner.

## 2. Commands (one run, short output)

| Case | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Everything, once | `npx ng test --no-watch --no-progress` |
| One file | `npx ng test --no-watch --no-progress --include src/app/valves/valve.spec.ts` |
| One test by its name | `npx ng test --no-watch --no-progress --filter "closes the valve"` |
| Coverage | add `--coverage`; report in `coverage/` |
| List the test files | `--list-tests` |
| Real browser | `--browsers chromium` (the `Headless` suffix or `CI` set: no window) |

[S42] [S43]

- `--watch` is `true` in an interactive terminal and `false` otherwise; `CI=true` gives one run. Always pass `--no-watch`: a watching run never ends.
- Reporters: `--reporters dots` gives the shortest output.

## 3. Configuration (Vitest builder)

- Test files: `**/*.spec.ts` and `**/*.test.ts`; `tsConfig` defaults to `tsconfig.spec.json`. [S42]
- Simulated DOM: `happy-dom` if installed, else `jsdom`. One of them must be installed. [S45]
- A Vitest configuration of the project goes through the `runnerConfig` option; `ng generate config vitest` writes it. The CLI overwrites `test.projects` and `test.include`. [S45]
- Vitest option against tests without assertions: `expect.requireAssertions: true`. [S68]

## 4. Lint rules for tests (ESLint)

- `@vitest/eslint-plugin`, preset `recommended`: `expect-expect`, `valid-expect`, `no-focused-tests`, `no-disabled-tests`, `no-conditional-expect`, `no-identical-title`. [S61]
- The page does not name ESLint 10: try the plugin in the dev container before you add it to `eslint.config.mjs`, and add it only with the user's agreement.

## Sources

- [S42] Angular, "Testing", v22.2.6, <https://angular.dev/guide/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S43] Angular, "ng test", CLI reference, v22, <https://angular.dev/cli/test>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S44] Angular, "Announcing Angular v21", Angular blog, 2025-11, <https://blog.angular.dev/announcing-angular-v21-57946c34f14b>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S45] Angular, "Migrating from Karma to Vitest", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/testing/migrating-to-vitest>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S46] angular/angular-cli, "Release 22.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular-cli/releases/tag/v22.0.0>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S47] angular/angular-cli, `packages/angular/build/package.json` at v22.0.0, <https://github.com/angular/angular-cli/blob/v22.0.0/packages/angular/build/package.json>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S61] vitest-dev, "eslint-plugin-vitest", README of the repository, <https://github.com/vitest-dev/eslint-plugin-vitest>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S68] Vitest, "expect" configuration reference, v5.1.0, <https://vitest.dev/config/expect>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.

# Test a TypeScript class that drives SVG

Contents: 1. Where to run the test · 2. Rules · 3. Example · Sources

## 1. Where to run the test (simulated DOM or real browser)

| Environment | `getBBox`, `getCTM`, `getScreenCTM` | Layout | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| jsdom | Not declared: calling one throws a `TypeError` | None | [S69] [S70] |
| happy-dom | Declared, but return an empty `DOMRect` and an identity `DOMMatrix` | Not computed | [S71] |
| Browser mode of Vitest (Playwright) | The real values | Real | [S66] |

- A test that reads attributes, classes, text or calls: simulated DOM.
- A test that needs geometry: pass the geometry to the class as a parameter, or run that test in the browser mode (`ng test --browsers chromium`) in a file of its own. [S42]
- Never assert a geometry in a simulated DOM: under happy-dom, the test passes on zeros.

## 2. Rules (SVG class tests)

- Keep the computation of the shown state a pure function, and test it without any DOM.
- Create the SVG nodes in the test as the code does: `document.createElementNS` with the SVG namespace.
- Time: `vi.useFakeTimers()`, then `vi.advanceTimersToNextFrame()` for one `requestAnimationFrame` step, or `vi.advanceTimersByTime(ms)` for a blink; `vi.useRealTimers()` in `afterEach`. [S65]
- Check the effect on the node (attribute, class, `data-*` value), not the calls inside the class.
- Call `dispose()` of the class in `afterEach`, and check that no timer is left: `vi.getTimerCount()` is 0.

## 3. Example (a valve symbol class)

```ts
describe('ValveSymbol', () => {
  afterEach(() => {
    vi.useRealTimers();
  });

  it('marks the symbol closed, then flashes an unacknowledged alarm at 1 Hz', () => {
    vi.useFakeTimers();
    const group = document.createElementNS('http://www.w3.org/2000/svg', 'g');
    const symbol = new ValveSymbol(group);

    symbol.update({ positionState: 'closed', alarmState: 'unacknowledged' });
    expect(group.dataset['position']).toBe('closed');
    expect(group.classList.contains('is-flashing-on')).toBe(true);

    vi.advanceTimersByTime(500);
    expect(group.classList.contains('is-flashing-on')).toBe(false);

    symbol.dispose();
    expect(vi.getTimerCount()).toBe(0);
  });
});
```

The expected values come from `docs/synoptics/synoptic-rules.md`: the state by a data attribute and a word, the flash at 1 Hz until acknowledged.

## Sources

- [S42] Angular, "Testing", v22.2.6, <https://angular.dev/guide/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S65] Vitest, "Vi" API reference, v5.1.0, <https://vitest.dev/api/vi>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S66] Vitest, "Browser Mode" guide, v5.1.0, <https://vitest.dev/guide/browser/>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S69] jsdom, README of the repository, <https://github.com/jsdom/jsdom>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S70] jsdom, `SVGGraphicsElement.webidl`, <https://github.com/jsdom/jsdom/blob/main/lib/jsdom/living/nodes/SVGGraphicsElement.webidl>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S71] happy-dom, `SVGGraphicsElement.ts`, <https://github.com/capricorn86/happy-dom/blob/master/packages/happy-dom/src/nodes/svg-graphics-element/SVGGraphicsElement.ts>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.

# Angular tests, old to new

Contents: 1. The rule · 2. Runner and Jasmine · 3. TestBed, HTTP and routing · Sources

## 1. The rule (old to new in Angular tests)

- Read `@angular/core` in `package.json`. Use the "New" column only at the "Since" version or later.
- A project still on Karma and Jasmine keeps them. The conversion to Vitest is the schematic `ng g @schematics/angular:refactor-jasmine-vitest`: propose it, never rewrite a test suite by hand. [S45]

## 2. Runner and Jasmine (old to new)

| Old | New | Since | Status of the old | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Karma and Jasmine in a new project | Vitest, builder `@angular/build:unit-test` | 21 | Still supported | [S42] |
| Jest or Web Test Runner builder | `@angular/build:unit-test` | 22 | Removed | [S46] |
| `ng test --watch=false --browsers=ChromeHeadless` | `ng test --no-watch --no-progress` | 21 | Karma only | [S43] |
| `jasmine.createSpy()`, `spyOn(o, 'm')` | `vi.fn()`, `vi.spyOn(o, 'm')` | 21 | Jasmine only | [S45] |
| `fit`, `fdescribe`, `xit` | `it.only`, `describe.only`, `it.skip` | 21 | Jasmine only | [S45] |
| `jasmine.any(T)`, `jasmine.objectContaining(o)` | `expect.any(T)`, `expect.objectContaining(o)` | 21 | Jasmine only | [S45] |
| `fakeAsync(() => { tick(1000); })` | `vi.useFakeTimers()`, `await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(1000)`, `vi.useRealTimers()` | 21 | No longer recommended; needs `zone.js` | [S45] [S48] |
| `waitForAsync(() => { ... })` | An `async` test and `await fixture.whenStable()` | 21 | Needs `zone.js` | [S48] |

## 3. TestBed, HTTP and routing (old to new)

| Old | New | Since | Status of the old | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `fixture.detectChanges()` after each change | `await fixture.whenStable()` | 21 | Avoid in new code; existing suites may keep it | [S49] |
| `component.value = x` on an input | `fixture.componentRef.setInput('value', x)` | 19 | Error under the OnPush rules of a zoneless `TestBed` | [S48] [S49] |
| `TestBed.flushEffects()` | `TestBed.tick()` | 20 | Deprecated | [S52] |
| `HttpClientTestingModule` | `provideHttpClient()`, then `provideHttpClientTesting()` | 18 | Replaced | [S50] |
| `RouterTestingModule` | `provideRouter(routes)`, `provideLocationMocks()` and `RouterTestingHarness` | 17 | Deprecated in 21 | [S55] |
| `NO_ERRORS_SCHEMA` everywhere | Stub children with `overrideComponent` | 17 | Hides template errors | [S48] |

## Sources

- [S42] Angular, "Testing", v22.2.6, <https://angular.dev/guide/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S43] Angular, "ng test", CLI reference, v22, <https://angular.dev/cli/test>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S45] Angular, "Migrating from Karma to Vitest", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/testing/migrating-to-vitest>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S46] angular/angular-cli, "Release 22.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular-cli/releases/tag/v22.0.0>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S48] Angular, "Component testing scenarios", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/testing/components-scenarios>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S49] Angular, "Zoneless", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/zoneless>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S50] Angular, "Test requests", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/http/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S52] Angular, "TestBed", API reference, v22, <https://angular.dev/api/core/testing/TestBed>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S55] Angular, "Testing routing and navigation", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/routing/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Test a component, a service and a route with the zoneless TestBed

Contents: 1. Rules · 2. A component · 3. HTTP · 4. A route · Sources

## 1. Rules (Angular tests)

- `TestBed` is zoneless by default, even with `zone.js` loaded, and applies the OnPush rules: change state through signals or `setInput`. [S49]
- Wait for the render with `await fixture.whenStable()`. Run effects with `TestBed.tick()`. [S48] [S52]
- Take the expected value from the request, never from the component code. Query the DOM as a user does: text, role, label.
- Mock only the boundaries: HTTP through `HttpTestingController`, never `HttpClient` by hand; never mock the router. [S50] [S55]
- Never assert the calls of a spy when the DOM or the result shows the behavior. [S82] Agents add mocks more often than people do. [S74]
- For time, use `vi.useFakeTimers()` and `await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(ms)`, then `vi.useRealTimers()`; never `fakeAsync` in new code. [S65]
- A test fails: fix the code under test. Never change, skip or delete the test to make it pass.

## 2. A component (inputs, outputs, signals)

Wrong: the input set as a field, a forced detection, the expected text copied from the template code.

```ts
it('works', () => {
  const fixture = TestBed.createComponent(ValveStatus);
  fixture.componentInstance.isOpen = true as never;
  fixture.detectChanges();
  expect(fixture.nativeElement.innerHTML).toContain('<span class="state">');
});
```

Right:

```ts
it('shows OPEN for an open valve', async () => {
  const fixture = TestBed.createComponent(ValveStatus);

  fixture.componentRef.setInput('isOpen', true);
  await fixture.whenStable();

  expect(fixture.nativeElement.textContent).toContain('OPEN');
});
```

- An output: subscribe to it, `fixture.componentInstance.closed.subscribe(...)`, then act and assert. [S48]
- A child component: stub it with `overrideComponent`; never `NO_ERRORS_SCHEMA` everywhere. [S48]

## 3. HTTP (HttpTestingController)

```ts
beforeEach(() => {
  TestBed.configureTestingModule({ providers: [provideHttpClient(), provideHttpClientTesting()] });
});

afterEach(() => {
  TestBed.inject(HttpTestingController).verify();
});

it('reads one valve', async () => {
  const pending = firstValueFrom(TestBed.inject(ValveApi).get('V-101'));
  const request = TestBed.inject(HttpTestingController).expectOne('/api/valves/V-101');

  request.flush({ tag: 'V-101', isOpen: true });

  expect(await pending).toEqual({ tag: 'V-101', isOpen: true });
});
```

- `provideHttpClient()` comes before `provideHttpClientTesting()`. [S50]
- `httpResource`: create it in an injection context, `TestBed.tick()` to send the request, `expectOne` and `flush`, then `await TestBed.inject(ApplicationRef).whenStable()`. [S50]

## 4. A route (RouterTestingHarness)

```ts
TestBed.configureTestingModule({ providers: [provideRouter([{ path: 'valves/:tag', component: ValveDetail }])] });
const harness = await RouterTestingHarness.create();
await harness.navigateByUrl('/valves/V-101', ValveDetail);

expect(harness.routeNativeElement?.textContent).toContain('V-101');
```

[S55]

## Sources

- [S48] Angular, "Component testing scenarios", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/testing/components-scenarios>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S49] Angular, "Zoneless", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/zoneless>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S50] Angular, "Test requests", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/http/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S52] Angular, "TestBed", API reference, v22, <https://angular.dev/api/core/testing/TestBed>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S55] Angular, "Testing routing and navigation", v22, <https://angular.dev/guide/routing/testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S65] Vitest, "Vi" API reference, v5.1.0, <https://vitest.dev/api/vi>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S74] A. Hora, R. Robbes, "Are Coding Agents Generating Over-Mocked Tests? An Empirical Study", MSR 2026, arXiv:2602.00409, <https://arxiv.org/html/2602.00409v1>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S82] A. Eagle, "Testing on the Toilet: Change-Detector Tests Considered Harmful", Google Testing Blog, 2015-01-27, <https://testing.googleblog.com/2015/01/testing-on-toilet-change-detector-tests.html>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.

# Angular à la version du projet

Ce qui a changé dans Angular de la version 17 à la 22, pour que l'IA écrive du code à la version
du projet, lue dans `package.json`, et non à celle de sa mémoire.

| Fichier | Ce qu'il contient |
| --- | --- |
| `old-to-new.md` | Le tableau « ancienne façon → nouvelle façon », avec la version à partir de laquelle chaque nouveauté vaut, et les commandes de migration. |
| `v22-changes.md` | Angular 22 : versions demandées, OnPush par défaut, ce qui est retiré, les API devenues stables (`@Service`, `resource`, Signal Forms). |

Quand Angular sort une nouvelle version majeure, la procédure `docs/maintenance/update-a-tool.md`
dit comment mettre ces documents à jour, et `node scripts/check-versions.ts <projet>` liste ceux
qui sont en retard sur un projet.

tool: angular

sources: [S1, S2, S3, S4, S5, S6, S7, S8, S9, S10, S11, S12, S13]

# Angular old patterns and their replacements

Contents: 1. Version rule · 2. Components and templates · 3. State, data and forms · 4. Services, HTTP and routing · 5. Tests and build · 6. Migration commands · Old patterns · Sources

## 1. Version rule (Angular old and new patterns)

Read the major version of `@angular/core` in `package.json`. Use a replacement only when that major version is equal to or higher than the `Since` column. Below it, write the old pattern. `17` in the column means every version this document covers. [S1]

Keep one style per file. In a file that already uses the old pattern, keep it, or migrate the whole file with a command of section 6.

Wrong, in a project with `"@angular/core": "^22.0.0"`:

```ts
@Component({
  selector: 'app-user-card',
  standalone: true,
  imports: [CommonModule],
  changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush,
  template: `<p *ngIf="user.active">{{ user.name }}</p>`,
})
export class UserCardComponent {
  @Input() user!: User;
  constructor(private readonly users: UserService) {}
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({
  selector: 'app-user-card',
  template: `
    @if (user().active) {
      <p>{{ user().name }}</p>
    }
  `,
})
export class UserCard {
  private readonly users = inject(UserService);
  public readonly user = input.required<User>();
}
```

## 2. Components and templates (Angular 17 to 22)

| Old pattern | Write instead | Since | Status of the old pattern |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `standalone: true` in the decorator | Nothing: standalone is the default | 19 | Redundant. A class declared in an NgModule needs `standalone: false`. [S5] |
| `@Input() name` | `readonly name = input<T>()` or `input.required<T>()` | 19 | Still works [S5] [S2] |
| `@Output() x = new EventEmitter<T>()` | `readonly x = output<T>()` | 19 | Still works [S5] [S2] |
| `@Input() v` with `@Output() vChange` | `readonly v = model<T>()` | 19 | Still works [S5] |
| `@ViewChild`, `@ContentChild` | `viewChild()`, `contentChild()` | 19 | Still works [S5] |
| `*ngIf`, `*ngFor`, `[ngSwitch]` | `@if`, `@for (item of items; track item.id)`, `@switch` | 18 | Deprecated in 20 [S6] |
| Template part loaded by hand | `@defer { … }` | 18 | — [S7] |
| `[ngClass]`, `[ngStyle]` | `[class.name]`, `[style.prop]`, `[class]`, `[style]` | 17 | Discouraged [S2] |
| `imports: [CommonModule]` | Import only the pipes and directives the template uses | 17 | Discouraged [S2] |
| `@HostBinding`, `@HostListener` | `host: { … }` in the decorator | 17 | Discouraged [S2] |
| `changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush` | Nothing: OnPush is the default | 22 | Redundant. `Default` is renamed `Eager`. [S4] |
| Constructor injection | `private readonly x = inject(X)` | 17 | Still works [S2] |
| `{{ this.item }}` to read a template variable | `{{ item }}` | 19 | Broken in 19 [S5] |

## 3. State, data and forms (Angular 17 to 22)

| Old pattern | Write instead | Since | Status of the old pattern |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Plain field or `BehaviorSubject` for component state | `signal()`, `computed()` | 17 | Still works [S2] |
| State derived from several sources, kept in sync by hand | `linkedSignal()` | 20 | — [S6] |
| `mySignal.mutate(…)` | `mySignal.update(…)` or `mySignal.set(…)` | 17 | Removed in 17 |
| `HttpClient` call, `subscribe`, loading flag | `resource({params, loader})` or `httpResource(() => url)` | 22 | Still works. Experimental before 22. [S3] [S10] |
| Reactive forms for a new form | Signal Forms: `form(model, schema)` and `[formField]` | 22 | Still supported. Experimental in 21. [S3] [S9] |
| `takeUntil(this.destroy$)` with `ngOnDestroy` | `takeUntilDestroyed()` | 19 | Still works [S5] |
| Manual `subscribe` to copy a value into a field | `toSignal(source$, {initialValue: …})` | 20 | The `async` pipe stays right in templates [S6] [S11] |
| `zone.js`, change detection driven by `NgZone` | `provideZonelessChangeDetection()` and signals | 21 | New applications have no `zone.js` from 21 [S7] |

Read a resource with `user.hasValue()`, `user.value()`, `user.isLoading()` and `user.error()`. [S10]

## 4. Services, HTTP and routing (Angular 17 to 22)

| Old pattern | Write instead | Since | Status of the old pattern |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `@Injectable({providedIn: 'root'})` | `@Service()` | 22 | Still works [S3] [S12] |
| `HttpClientModule` in `imports` | `provideHttpClient()` in `providers` | 18 | Deprecated in 18 |
| `provideHttpClient(withFetch())` | `provideHttpClient()` | 22 | `withFetch` deprecated in 22 [S4] |
| `ComponentFactoryResolver` | `viewContainerRef.createComponent(MyComponent)` | 17 | Removed in 22 [S4] |
| `provideRoutes(routes)` | `provideRouter(routes)` | 17 | Removed in 22 [S4] |
| `Router.errorHandler` | `withNavigationErrorHandler()` in `provideRouter()` | 19 | Removed in 19 [S5] |

## 5. Tests and build (Angular 17 to 22)

| Old pattern | Write instead | Since | Status of the old pattern |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `TestBed.get(X)` | `TestBed.inject(X)` | 17 | Removed in 20 [S6] |
| `async(() => …)` in a test | `waitForAsync(() => …)` | 17 | Removed in 18 |
| `TestBed.flushEffects()` | `TestBed.tick()` | 20 | Deprecated in 20, still in the API of 22 [S6] |
| `RouterTestingModule` | `provideRouter()` and `provideLocationMocks()` | 17 | Deprecated in 21 [S8] |
| Karma and Jasmine in a new project | Vitest, builder `@angular/build:unit-test` | 21 | Karma is still supported [S7] |
| Builders of `@angular-devkit/build-angular` (Webpack) | Builders of `@angular/build` | 18 | Deprecated in 22 |
| File `user-card.component.ts`, class `UserCardComponent` | File `user-card.ts`, class `UserCard` | 20 | Keep the naming the project already uses [S2] |

## 6. Migration commands (Angular schematics)

Do not rewrite an old pattern by hand across a project. Run the command from the project root. [S8] [S13]

| Change | Command |
| --- | --- |
| NgModules to standalone | `ng generate @angular/core:standalone-migration` |
| `*ngIf`, `*ngFor` to `@if`, `@for` | `ng generate @angular/core:control-flow-migration` |
| Constructor injection to `inject()` | `ng generate @angular/core:inject-migration` |
| `@Input` to `input()` | `ng generate @angular/core:signal-input-migration` |
| `@Output` to `output()` | `ng generate @angular/core:output-migration` |
| Query decorators to signal queries | `ng generate @angular/core:signal-queries-migration` |
| `ngClass` to `class` bindings | `ng generate @angular/core:ngclass-to-class-migration` |
| `CommonModule` to single imports | `ng generate @angular/core:common-to-standalone-migration` |
| `@Injectable` to `@Service`, from 22.1 | `ng generate @angular/core:service` |

- A command exists only from the version that introduced it. When it fails with an unknown schematic, show the error and stop. Do not rewrite the code by hand instead.
- Run the `inject-migration` before the `@Service` migration: it skips classes that inject through their constructor. [S12]
- To move to a new major version: `ng update @angular/core@<major> @angular/cli@<major>`, one major version at a time.

## Old patterns

- `standalone: true` (before 19), `*ngIf` and `*ngFor` (before 18), `changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush` (before 22): do not write them in new code of a project at these versions or later.
- `HttpClientModule` (before 18), `TestBed.get()` (before 20), `ComponentFactoryResolver` (before 22): do not use them.

## Sources

- [S1] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "LLM prompts and AI IDE setup" and "Angular coding style guide", v22, <https://angular.dev/ai/develop-with-ai>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Angular Team, "Announcing Angular v22", 2026-06-03, <https://blog.angular.dev/announcing-angular-v22-c52bb83a4664>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] angular/angular, "Release 22.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular/releases/tag/v22.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S5] angular/angular, "Release 19.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular/releases/tag/19.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S6] angular/angular, "Release 20.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular/releases/tag/20.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S7] Angular Team, "Announcing Angular v21", 2025-11-19, <https://blog.angular.dev/announcing-angular-v21-57946c34f14b>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S8] Angular Team, "Migrations", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/migrations>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S9] Angular Team, "Forms with signals", v22, <https://angular.dev/essentials/signal-forms>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

- [S11] Angular Team, "RxJS interop with Angular signals", v22, <https://angular.dev/ecosystem/rxjs-interop>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

- [S13] Types of `@angular/core` 22.2.6 installed in the example project of this base: `Service`, `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` and `injectAsync` checked on 2026-11-13, MIT.

# Angular 22: what changed

Contents: 1. Requirements · 2. OnPush by default · 3. Removed and changed · 4. New stable APIs · 5. Deprecated · 6. Migrations · Old patterns · Sources

Use this document only when `@angular/core` in `package.json` is 22 or higher.

## 1. Requirements (Angular 22 versions)

- Node.js: `^22.22.3 || ^24.15.0 || ^26.0.0`. Node.js 20 is not supported. [S2] [S5]
- TypeScript: `>=6.0.0 <6.1.0`. TypeScript 5.9 and TypeScript 7 are outside this range: never move `typescript` to 7. [S2]
- RxJS: `^6.5.3 || ^7.4.0`. [S2]
- Released on 2026-06-03. Active support ends in 2027-06, long-term support in 2028-06. [S1]

## 2. OnPush by default (Angular 22 change detection)

A component without `changeDetection` uses OnPush. `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Default` is renamed `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager`. [S4]

- Do not write `changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush`. [S3]
- Keep component state in signals, so that the view updates.
- Keep `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` where the update added it. Remove it only when the component reads its state from signals.

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-clock', template: '<time>{{ now }}</time>' })
export class Clock {
  public now = new Date();

  public constructor() {
    // With OnPush, the view does not update.
    setInterval(() => (this.now = new Date()), 1000);
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-clock', template: '<time>{{ now() }}</time>' })
export class Clock {
  protected readonly now = signal(new Date());

  public constructor() {
    const timer = setInterval(() => this.now.set(new Date()), 1000);

    inject(DestroyRef).onDestroy(() => clearInterval(timer));
  }
}
```

## 3. Removed and changed (Angular 22 breaking changes)

| Old pattern | Write instead |
| --- | --- |
| `ComponentFactoryResolver`, `ComponentFactory` | Pass the component class to `ViewContainerRef.createComponent()` |
| `createNgModuleRef()` | `createNgModule()` |
| `provideRoutes()` | `provideRouter()`, or the `ROUTES` multi token |
| `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Default` | `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` |
| Builders `@angular-devkit/build-angular:jest` and `:web-test-runner` | Builder `@angular/build:unit-test` |

Behaviour that changed: [S4] [S5]

- Router: `paramsInheritanceStrategy` defaults to `'always'`. `CanMatchFn` requires the `currentSnapshot` parameter.
- Forms: the `min` and `max` validation rules accept a number or `null`, not a string.
- Templates: an attribute with the `data-` prefix no longer binds an input or an output. Binding the same input or output twice is a compile error.
- HTTP: `HttpClient` uses the fetch backend by default. To report upload progress, keep the XHR backend with the `withXhr` feature of `provideHttpClient()`.
- Server rendering: a request with an unknown `Host` header gets a 400 response. List the valid hosts in `allowedHosts`.

## 4. New stable APIs (Angular 22: use these in new code)

| Need | Write | Import from |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Singleton service | `@Service()` | `@angular/core` |
| Asynchronous data as signals | `resource()` | `@angular/core` |
| HTTP data as signals | `httpResource()` | `@angular/common/http` |
| Form | `form()`, `FormField`, `[formField]` | `@angular/forms/signals` |
| Dependency loaded on demand | `injectAsync()` | `@angular/core` |

[S3] [S4] [S7] [S8]

Wrong:

```ts
@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })
export class UserStore {
  public constructor(private readonly http: HttpClient) {}
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Service()
export class UserStore {
  private readonly http = inject(HttpClient);
}
```

New template syntax in 22: comments inside an opening tag; spread in object and array literals; several `@case` for one block, and `@default never;` to make a `@switch` exhaustive; short arrow functions such as `(click)="count.update(n => n + 1)"`. [S3]

## 5. Deprecated (Angular 22: do not write)

- `withFetch()` in `provideHttpClient()`. Remove it. [S4]
- The `reportProgress` request option. Use `reportUploadProgress` and `reportDownloadProgress`. [S4]
- The Webpack builders: `@angular-devkit/build-angular`, `@angular-devkit/build-webpack`, `@ngtools/webpack`. Use the builders of `@angular/build`. [S5]
- `CommonEngine` for server rendering. Use `AngularNodeAppEngine` or `AngularAppEngine`. [S5]

## 6. Migrations (Angular 22 update)

Update from 21 with `ng update @angular/core@22 @angular/cli@22`. Do not skip a major version. The update does these changes; do not redo them by hand: [S4] [S5]

- It adds `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` to existing components where needed.
- It adds the `currentSnapshot` parameter for `CanMatchFn`, and `strictTemplates` to the TypeScript configuration.
- It keeps the XHR backend where the application reports upload progress.

Optional: `ng generate @angular/core:service`, from 22.1, turns `@Injectable` into `@Service`. [S6]

## Old patterns

- `changeDetection: ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush` (before 22): the opt-in to OnPush. Do not write it.
- `@Injectable({providedIn: 'root'})` (before 22): use `@Service()` in new code.
- `provideHttpClient(withFetch())` (before 22): do not write `withFetch()`.
- `import {form, Field} from '@angular/forms/signals'` with `[field]` (21.0): the experimental name. Write `FormField` and `[formField]`.

## Sources

- [S1] Angular Team, "Versioning and releases", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/releases>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Angular Team, "Announcing Angular v22", 2026-06-03, <https://blog.angular.dev/announcing-angular-v22-c52bb83a4664>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] angular/angular, "Release 22.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular/releases/tag/v22.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S5] angular/angular-cli, "Release 22.0.0", <https://github.com/angular/angular-cli/releases/tag/v22.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S6] Angular Team, "Injectable to Service migration", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/migrations/injectable-to-service>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S7] Angular Team, "Forms with signals", v22, <https://angular.dev/essentials/signal-forms>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S8] Types of `@angular/core` 22.2.6 installed in the example project of this base: `Service`, `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Eager` and `injectAsync` checked on 2026-11-13, MIT.

# Sujet « architecture » : applications .NET et Angular

Ce dossier aide l'IA à conseiller et à revoir l'architecture d'une application .NET et Angular
déployée sur Kubernetes, sans recommander par habitude une bibliothèque devenue payante ou un
patron retiré. Les deux agents `architecture-review` et `code-review` s'appuient dessus.

| Fichier | Ce qu'il contient |
| --- | --- |
| `choose-decomposition.md` | Monolithe modulaire d'abord, service séparé sur besoin constaté ; règles d'un module ; anti-patrons. |
| `structure-dotnet-solution.md` | Couches ou tranches verticales ; CQRS écrit à la main, sans médiateur ; tests d'architecture. |
| `api-contract-openapi.md` | Document OpenAPI généré au build, client Angular généré, versionnement, erreurs normalisées. |
| `real-time-push.md` | SignalR, SSE ou gRPC ; plusieurs réplicas ; format et réglages ; chemin d'une valeur jusqu'au synoptique. |
| `messaging.md` | Quand une file ; boîte d'envoi ; consommateurs idempotents ; licences des bibliothèques de bus. |
| `dotnet-service-patterns.md` | Injection, options, résilience, journaux, télémétrie, santé, données, Aspire. |
| `angular-app-structure.md` | Dossiers par fonctionnalité, composants autonomes, état par portée avec les signaux. |
| `angular-high-frequency-ui.md` | Afficher beaucoup de valeurs qui changent : signaux, OnPush, peu de nœuds, mesurer d'abord. |
| `outdated-libraries-and-patterns.md` | Bibliothèques devenues payantes, patrons .NET et Angular retirés, signes de sur-ingénierie. |

id: architecture-angular-app-structure
title: Angular application structure
topic: architecture
summary: Folders by feature, standalone components, state by scope with signals, server data and module boundaries. Angular 22.
tool: angular

# Angular application structure

Contents: 1. Organization · 2. State by scope · 3. Boundaries · Sources

## 1. Organization and code (Angular style guide, v22)

- Group by feature domain, never by type (`components/`, `services/`); one concept per file; tests next to the code. [S1] [S2]
- `inject()` rather than constructor injection. [S1] [S2]
- Components focused on presentation; complex logic out of the template, often in a `computed`. [S1]
- Template members `protected`; inputs, outputs and queries `readonly`. [S1]
- `class` and `style` bindings rather than `NgClass` and `NgStyle`. [S1] [S2]
- Standalone components without writing `standalone: true`; no explicit change detection strategy; lazy-loaded feature routes; `input()`, `output()`, `model()`; `@if`, `@for`, `@switch`. [S2]

## 2. State by scope (Angular signals)

| Scope | Tool |
| --- | --- |
| Local state of a component | `signal`, `computed`; `linkedSignal` for a dependent state [S2] [S3] |
| Shared state of a feature | A service that exposes signals, no library [S7] |
| Large application, large team | NgRx SignalStore (`signalStore`, `withState`, `withMethods`), MIT, version 22 [S5] [S7] |
| Server reads | `httpResource`, a reactive wrapper of `HttpClient`, for reads only; writes through `HttpClient` [S4] |
| Effects | Only for non-reactive APIs; to react to a state change, derive it [S3] |

A global Redux-like store is no longer the starting point; RxJS stays the tool for event streams, read in templates through the `async` pipe. [S7] [S2]

## 3. Boundaries (monorepo)

- Nx checks boundaries by a lint rule: each project carries tags (`scope:…`) and constraints say who may depend on whom; a project without a tag may depend on nothing. [S6]
- Useful when several applications share libraries; useless for one application. Single source, vendor of the tool.

## Sources

- [S1] Angular, "Angular coding style guide", v22.2.6, <https://angular.dev/style-guide>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular, "Best practices", context file for AI, <https://angular.dev/assets/context/best-practices.md>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Angular, "Signals overview", <https://angular.dev/guide/signals>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Angular, "Reactive data fetching with httpResource", <https://angular.dev/guide/http/http-resource>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Angular interface with many changing values

Contents: 1. What refreshes · 2. Measured case · 3. Rules for a synoptic · Sources

## 1. What refreshes the screen (zoneless and OnPush)

- Zoneless is the default since Angular 21. A refresh comes from a signal read by a template, `markForCheck()` (the `async` pipe calls it), `ComponentRef.setInput`, or a template event listener. [S1]
- OnPush is the default for new applications in v22; `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Default` becomes `Eager`. [S4]
- An OnPush component is checked again only on a new input reference, an event in its subtree, a mark, or a signal it reads; then only the components that read the signal refresh. Changing an object in place triggers nothing. [S2]
- In zoneless mode, a message from the server updates the screen only if it writes a signal or marks the component: every value shown on a synoptic goes through a signal.

## 2. A measured case (large table)

| Change | Effect measured by the author |
| --- | --- |
| OnPush, row and cell components, `@for` with `track` | Script of one click: 612 ms to 9 ms |
| Fewer DOM nodes (the cell becomes the component) | 513,000 to 221,000 nodes; one click rendered under 100 ms |
| Live updates every 50 ms, after these changes | Handled in about 7 ms |
| Heavy computation in a Web Worker, cache | First display from 3 s to 0.3 s |
| Virtual scrolling | Not used; kept as a last resort |

A table of about 73,000 cells, not a synoptic; Angular version not given; April 2025; single source. [S3]

## 3. Rules for a synoptic view (Angular)

- One component per symbol, one signal per shown value, the fewest DOM nodes per symbol.
- Batch the server messages and write the signals once per batch (`docs/architecture/real-time-push.md`).
- Measure before you optimize; virtual scrolling only after a measure shows the need.

## Sources

- [S1] Angular, "Angular without ZoneJS (Zoneless)", <https://angular.dev/guide/zoneless>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular, "Skipping component subtrees", <https://angular.dev/best-practices/skipping-subtrees>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Vincent Bourdeix, "Boost Angular Performance: Real-World Optimization Case Study", DEV Community, 2025-04-28, <https://dev.to/vbourdeix/boost-angular-performance-real-world-optimization-case-study-58c7>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] Angular, "Announcing Angular v22", 2026-06-03, <https://blog.angular.dev/announcing-angular-v22-c52bb83a4664>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# API contract with OpenAPI

Contents: 1. Rule · 2. Server side · 3. Angular client · 4. Versions and contracts · Sources

## 1. Rule of the API contract

The OpenAPI file generated at build is the contract. The Angular client is generated from it, never written by hand. A diff of the file shows every contract change in review.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  A["API code (.NET)"] --> B["Build: generated OpenAPI document"]
  B --> C["OpenAPI file versioned in the repository"]
  C --> D["TypeScript client generator"]
  D --> E["Angular client library"]
  E --> F["Angular build: a broken contract fails compilation"]
```

## 2. Server side (ASP.NET Core)

| Fact | Detail |
| --- | --- |
| Swashbuckle left the templates in .NET 9 | `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi` generates the document [S1] |
| Two calls | `AddOpenApi()` and `MapOpenApi()`; route `/openapi/{documentName}.json`; a test UI is added apart [S2] |
| Document version | 3.1 by default in .NET 10, 3.2 in .NET 11; set with `OpenApiVersion` [S2] [S3] |
| Build-time generation | Package `Microsoft.Extensions.ApiDescription.Server`; folder set with `OpenApiDocumentsDirectory`; it runs the entry point, so startup code runs [S2] |
| Endpoint not protected | No authorization by default; examples map it in development only [S2] |
| Minimal APIs | Recommended for new projects; controllers for extensible model binding, advanced validation or OData [S4] |
| Validation | `AddValidation()` checks query, headers and body and returns 400 (.NET 10) [S3] |
| Errors | `AddProblemDetails()`, `UseExceptionHandler()`, `UseStatusCodePages()`; `IExceptionHandler` since ASP.NET Core 8 [S5] |
| Cookie authentication | API endpoints get 401 or 403 instead of a redirect in .NET 10 [S3] |

## 3. Angular client (generated)

| Tool | State on its page | Known limits |
| --- | --- | --- |
| openapi-generator `typescript-angular` | Stable; Angular 9 to 22; option `withHttpResource` | `oneOf`, `anyOf`, `allOf` and unions listed as unsupported [S6] |
| NSwag | MIT; Angular template | Page names Swagger 2.0 and OpenAPI 3.0 only [S7] |
| Orval, Hey API, Kiota | Other generators | Not compared here |

.NET 10 emits OpenAPI 3.1 and .NET 11 describes C# 15 unions with `anyOf`, which the generator above does not list: set `OpenApiVersion` to 3.0, or keep unions out of API contracts until the generator handles them. [S2] [S3] [S6]

## 4. Versions and contracts (API)

- Version an API with `Asp.Versioning.Http` (minimal APIs) or `Asp.Versioning.Mvc`; MIT, .NET Foundation. [S8]
- A service update must not break its clients: design for compatibility. [S9]
- Pact tests a consumer-driven contract, born from the consumer tests; it fits between teams that deliver apart. In one repository, the generated client and the OpenAPI diff give the essential. [S10]

## Sources

- [S1] dotnet/aspnetcore, "Announcement: Swashbuckle.AspNetCore is being removed in .NET 9", issue 54599, <https://github.com/dotnet/aspnetcore/issues/54599>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "Generate OpenAPI documents", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/fundamentals/openapi/aspnetcore-openapi>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "What's new in ASP.NET Core in .NET 10", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/release-notes/aspnetcore-10.0>, and "Announcing .NET 11", 2026-11-10, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "APIs overview", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/fundamentals/apis>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Microsoft, "Handle errors in ASP.NET Core APIs", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/fundamentals/error-handling-api>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S6] OpenAPI Generator, "Documentation for the typescript-angular Generator", <https://openapi-generator.tech/docs/generators/typescript-angular>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S7] Rico Suter and contributors, "NSwag", README, <https://github.com/RicoSuter/NSwag>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S8] dotnet, "aspnet-api-versioning", README, <https://github.com/dotnet/aspnet-api-versioning>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S9] Microsoft, "Microservices architecture style", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/guide/architecture-styles/microservices>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S10] Pact Foundation, "Introduction", <https://docs.pact.io/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Modular monolith or microservices

Contents: 1. Rule · 2. Decision · 3. Module rules · 4. Anti-patterns · Sources

## 1. Rule of decomposition (start with a modular monolith)

- Start with a modular monolith. Split a service only on a need that is seen, never by anticipation.
- Successful microservices mostly start from a monolith grown too big; systems built as microservices from the start often had serious trouble. [S1]
- A monolith suits a small team; it gets in the way when many teams change one code base and deploy in a queue. [S3]
- Four documented moves back from microservices (Istio, Prime Video, Segment, InVision) cite cost in all four cases. [S4]
- Opposite view: for a large system whose domain is well known, independent subsystems from the start can be better. [S2]

## 2. Decision (split a service or not)

Read from top to bottom: each diamond is a question to ask before a separate service.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
  A["New application or rework"] --> B{"Several autonomous teams must deliver apart?"}
  B -- no --> C{"Must one part scale or fail alone?"}
  B -- yes --> D{"Are the domain boundaries known and stable?"}
  C -- no --> M["Modular monolith: one deployable, one module per domain"]
  C -- yes --> E["Extract that one module as a service"]
  D -- no --> M
  D -- yes --> S["Services per domain, private data, versioned contracts"]
  M --> R["Review at each need seen, never by anticipation"]
```

## 3. Module rules (modular monolith)

- Each module owns its data (its own schema or database), exposes an explicit public interface, and never reads the tables of another module; without these rules it is a plain monolith. [S7]
- When modules talk by events through an outbox, only the transport changes when one is extracted later. [S7] [S9]
- Service boundaries follow business capabilities, not technical layers; domain analysis and bounded contexts find them. [S6] [S5]
- Microservices cost complexity, testing, network latency, data integrity without transactions, operations, versioning and skills. [S5]
- The guide ".NET Microservices: Architecture for Containerized .NET Applications" stays at .NET 7 and its eShopOnContainers is no production model; the current reference app is `dotnet/eShop`, on .NET 10 with Aspire. [S8]

## 4. Anti-patterns of decomposition

- Splitting without knowing the domain; database entities used as events; shared common libraries between services; services exposed without a gateway. [S5]
- Microservices for one small team. [S1] [S3]

## Sources

- [S1] Martin Fowler, "Monolith First", 2015-06-03, <https://www.martinfowler.com/bliki/MonolithFirst.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Stefan Tilkov, "Don't start with a monolith", 2015-06-09, <https://martinfowler.com/articles/dont-start-monolith.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Chris Richardson, "When to use the microservice architecture: part 5", microservices.io, 2021, <https://microservices.io/post/microservices/2021/02/14/why-microservices-part-5-monolith.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] Su, Li, Taibi, "From Microservice to Monolith: A Multivocal Literature Review", Electronics 13(8), 2024, <https://mdpi.com/2079-9292/13/8/1452>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY, summarized.
- [S5] Microsoft, "Microservices architecture style", Azure Architecture Center, updated 2025-07-11, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/guide/architecture-styles/microservices>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S6] Microsoft, "Use domain analysis to model microservices", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/microservices/model/domain-analysis>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S7] Milan Jovanović, "Modular Monolith vs Microservices: How to Choose", 2026-07-14, <https://milanjovanovic.tech/blog/modular-monolith-vs-microservices.md>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S8] Microsoft, ".NET Microservices: Architecture for Containerized .NET Applications", v7.0, and dotnet, "eShop", <https://github.com/dotnet/eShop>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S9] Chris Richardson, "Pattern: Transactional outbox", <https://microservices.io/patterns/data/transactional-outbox.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
