# Code review checklist

Contents: A. Every change · B. C# and .NET · C. TypeScript and Angular · Sources

What the lint and the build check is not reviewed again: run the check commands of `AGENTS.md` first. A personal preference is marked as optional and never blocks. [S1]

## A. Every change (code review)

- Design: does the change belong here, and does it fit the rest of the system?
- Function: does it do what the author meant, also on edge cases and on errors?
- Complexity: can a reader understand it quickly? Is any code made general for a supposed future need?
- Tests: are there tests that would fail if the code were broken?
- Names: does every name say what it holds or does?
- Comments: do comments say why, not what?
- Consistency: does it follow the conventions of the project and of this base?
- Documentation: are the README, the API contract or the base updated when behavior changes?
- Every line: did you read every line you are asked to review, and the context around it?
- Good things: say what is well done. [S1]

## B. C# and .NET (code review)

- No singleton keeps a scoped service (captive dependency). [S2]
- Options are bound, validated and checked at start; no `IOptionsSnapshot<T>` in a singleton. [S2]
- One resilience handler per HTTP client; no retry of a POST without disabling unsafe methods. [S2]
- Log calls use message templates with named fields, never string interpolation. [S2]
- One short `DbContext` per unit of work; never two operations at once on one context; every async call awaited. [S2]
- No library of `docs/dotnet/paid-packages.md` added without the user's agreement.
- No pattern of `docs/architecture/outdated-libraries-and-patterns.md` for the target framework.

## C. TypeScript and Angular (code review)

- Every value shown comes from a signal; no object changed in place under OnPush. [S2]
- No effect used to copy a state into another: derive it with `computed`. [S2]
- Server reads through `httpResource` or a service; writes through `HttpClient`. [S2]
- No subscription left open after the component ends.
- Folders by feature; no new NgModule; no old template syntax.
- Symbols of a synoptic follow `docs/synoptics/`; property names follow the naming document.

## Sources

- [S1] Google, "What to look for in a code review", Google Engineering Practices, <https://google.github.io/eng-practices/review/reviewer/looking-for.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] The documents of this topic (`dotnet-service-patterns.md`, `angular-app-structure.md`, `angular-high-frequency-ui.md`) and their sources, 2026-11-13.

tool: dotnet
version: "10"

# .NET service patterns

Contents: 1. Do and do not · 2. Resilience and Polly · 3. Aspire · Sources

## 1. Do and do not (.NET service)

| Subject | Do | Do not |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dependency injection | Thread-safe singletons; scope validation on; fast synchronous factories | Captive dependency (a singleton that keeps a scoped service); service locator; `BuildServiceProvider` while configuring; `Task.Result` in a factory [S1] |
| Options | `AddOptions<T>().Bind(...)`, `ValidateDataAnnotations()`, `ValidateOnStart()`; `IOptionsMonitor<T>` in a singleton | `IOptionsSnapshot<T>` in a singleton [S2] |
| HTTP resilience | `AddStandardResilienceHandler()`: rate limiter, 30 s total timeout, 3 retries, circuit breaker, 10 s per attempt | Several handlers stacked; retrying a POST without `DisableForUnsafeHttpMethods()` [S3] |
| Logging | Message templates with named fields; `[LoggerMessage]` on hot paths | String interpolation in a log call [S5] |
| Telemetry | `ILogger` for logs, `Meter` for metrics, `ActivitySource` for traces; OTLP export | — [S4] |
| Health | Readiness probe with the tagged checks; liveness probe without dependency checks | A heavy query in a probe [S6] |
| Data access | One short `DbContext` per unit of work; `IDbContextFactory<T>` outside an HTTP request; await every async call | Two operations in parallel on one context; the in-memory provider for tests [S7] [S8] |
| Data tests | Test against the real engine (a container started by the tests); a repository as test double, knowing its cost | Mocking `DbSet` for queries [S7] |

- A `DbContext` is not thread-safe; the typical error starts with "A second operation started on this context". [S8]
- The EF Core in-memory provider is "strongly discouraged" as a fake engine. [S7]

## 2. Resilience and Polly (.NET)

- `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience` builds on Polly. [S3]
- The old package `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Polly` is deprecated by the `Polly.Extensions.Http` repository, archived on 2023-11-13. [S9]
- Polly adopts a maintenance fee for the next major version after 2026-11-16, on direct references only; going through `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience` is not subject to it. [S10]
- Rule: resilience through the `Microsoft.Extensions.*` packages; no direct reference to Polly without a recorded decision.

## 3. Aspire (local orchestration)

- Aspire (MIT) describes, runs, debugs and deploys a distributed application; its core, the AppHost, describes the architecture in code. [S11]
- It replaces neither Kubernetes nor a runtime: the AppHost runs at local start and in the deployment pipeline, then stops; never deploy it as a service. [S11]
- `aspire publish` writes Helm charts and `aspire deploy` installs them in the current `kubectl` context (Helm 4.2.0 or later). [S11]
- The "ServiceDefaults" project of the templates sets OpenTelemetry, health endpoints and HTTP resilience; it works without the Aspire orchestration. [S4]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Dependency injection guidelines", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/extensions/dependency-injection-guidelines>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "Options pattern in .NET", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/extensions/options>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "Build resilient HTTP apps: Key development patterns", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/resilience/http-resilience>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, ".NET observability with OpenTelemetry", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/diagnostics/observability-with-otel>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Microsoft, "Compile-time logging source generation", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/extensions/logger-message-generator>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S6] Microsoft, "Health checks in ASP.NET Core", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/host-and-deploy/health-checks>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S7] Microsoft, "Choosing a testing strategy" (EF Core), <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/testing/choosing-a-testing-strategy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S8] Microsoft, "DbContext Lifetime, Configuration, and Initialization", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/dbcontext-configuration/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S9] App-vNext, "Polly.Extensions.Http", archived repository, <https://github.com/App-vNext/Polly.Extensions.Http>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S10] The Polly Project, "Introducing the Open Source Maintenance Fee for Polly", 2026-07-14, updated 2026-10-12, <https://thepollyproject.org/2026/07/14/polly-osmf-announcement.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S11] Aspire, "Frequently asked questions" and "Deploy to Kubernetes", <https://aspire.dev/get-started/faq/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Messaging between services

Contents: 1. When · 2. Outbox and idempotence · 3. Broker · 4. Libraries and licenses · Sources

## 1. When to use a queue (messaging)

- Between services, asynchronous messaging avoids coupling and chatty call chains. [S1]
- Inside a modular monolith, a direct call is enough until you need to decouple in time: long work, recovery after a failure, several subscribers. A queue never replaces a method call.
- The base sets no bus library by default: the choice is made per project, with its license.

## 2. Transactional outbox and idempotent consumers

- Updating the database and publishing a message atomically needs a transactional outbox: the message is written to a table in the same transaction, and a relay publishes it later. [S2]
- The relay can publish twice: every consumer must be idempotent (store the ids of the messages already handled). [S2]
- CQRS with two databases syncs the read side by events, with the same care. [S3]

## 3. Broker (RabbitMQ or Kafka)

The RabbitMQ team writes that "RabbitMQ for queues, Kafka for streams" is outdated: RabbitMQ has replayable streams since 3.9 (2021), Kafka has queues since 4.2 (2026); its advice is to start with RabbitMQ and add Kafka for what only Kafka does (log compaction, tiered storage, Kafka Streams). [S4] Interested single source.

## 4. .NET bus libraries and their licenses (checked 2026-11-12)

| Library | Model | Free use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MassTransit 9 and later | Commercial license, key required | 100 % discount under one million dollars of revenue and for non-profits; v8 keeps its open license, support until 2026-12-31 [S5] |
| NServiceBus | Commercial: free in development, paid in production | Community tier: 3 endpoints, 10,000 messages per day [S6] |
| Wolverine | MIT; durable inbox and outbox | Paid support offers apart [S7] |
| Rebus | MIT | Commercial module apart (Rebus Pro) [S8] |

Never propose a library of this table without its license and version; if the project already uses it, report it as a risk to decide. MediatR is an in-memory mediator, not a bus: see `docs/dotnet/paid-packages.md`.

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Microservices architecture style", Azure Architecture Center, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/guide/architecture-styles/microservices>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Chris Richardson, "Pattern: Transactional outbox", <https://microservices.io/patterns/data/transactional-outbox.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "CQRS pattern", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/cqrs>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] RabbitMQ, "RabbitMQ vs. Apache Kafka", documentation 4.3, <https://rabbitmq.com/docs/compare/kafka>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] Massient, "Massient", MassTransit licenses, <https://massient.com/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S6] Particular Software, "Licensing", <https://particular.net/licensing>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Outdated libraries and patterns

Contents: 1. Rule · 2. Retired .NET patterns · 3. Retired Angular patterns · 4. Over-engineering · Sources

## 1. Rule for outdated libraries

Never propose a library that changed license (MediatR, AutoMapper, MassTransit, FluentAssertions, Verify, Polly as a direct reference) without its license status and the version concerned; if the project already uses it, report it as a risk to decide. The table and the free replacements are in `docs/dotnet/paid-packages.md`. A tracking site lists more packages (IdentityServer, ImageSharp, QuestPDF, EPPlus and others); single secondary source. [S1] It also lists Moq for its SponsorLink episode of 2023; Moq stayed BSD-3-Clause and dropped SponsorLink in 4.20.2 (`docs/dotnet-testing/test-old-to-new.md`).

## 2. Retired .NET patterns

| Old habit | State | Write instead |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `AddSwaggerGen()` and Swashbuckle by default | Out of the templates in .NET 9 | `AddOpenApi()`, `MapOpenApi()` [S2] [S3] |
| `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Polly`, `AddPolicyHandler` | Deprecated by the Polly project | `AddStandardResilienceHandler()` [S4] |
| EF Core in-memory provider for tests | Strongly discouraged | Real engine in a container, or a repository double [S5] |
| Controllers by default for a new API | Minimal APIs recommended | `MapGet`, `TypedResults` [S3] |
| eShopOnContainers as a model | Guide stuck at .NET 7, app replaced | `dotnet/eShop` as a reference to read [S3] |

## 3. Retired Angular patterns

| Old habit | State | Write instead |
| --- | --- | --- |
| NgModules, `standalone: true` by hand | Standalone by default | A standalone component without the flag [S6] |
| `*ngIf`, `*ngFor`, `*ngSwitch` | Deprecated in v20 | `@if`, `@for` with `track`, `@switch` [S7] [S6] |
| Constructor injection, `@Input()`, `@Output()` | Replaced in the guide | `inject()`, `input()`, `output()`, `model()` [S6] |
| `ngClass`, `ngStyle`, `@HostBinding`, `@HostListener` | Discouraged | `class` and `style` bindings, the `host` object [S6] |
| zone.js, `NgZone.onStable` | Zoneless by default in v21 | Signals, `afterNextRender` [S8] |
| `changeDetection: OnPush` written everywhere | OnPush by default in v22 | Nothing to write [S9] |
| Karma and Jasmine for a new project | Vitest by default in v21 | Vitest [S7] |
| `@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })` | Replaceable in most cases in v22 | `@Service` [S9] |
| Reactive forms for a new form | Signal Forms stable in v22 | Signal Forms [S9] |
| Suffixes `.component.ts`, `.service.ts` | Dropped by the 2025 style guide for new projects | `user-profile.ts` [S6] |

## 4. Signs of over-engineering

| Sign | Why it is a signal |
| --- | --- |
| Microservices for one small team | The premium only pays back on a complex system |
| Four projects, CQRS pipeline and domain events for five CRUD endpoints | Main risk of clean architecture |
| A service and a repository that only pass calls through | Abstractions that should not exist |
| A message bus between modules of one deployable without a need to decouple | Operations cost and duplicate messages |
| A global store for a local state | Signals and a service are enough |
| Virtual scrolling before any measure | Last resort |
| Code made general for a supposed future need | A classic point of code review |

These signs come from `choose-decomposition.md`, `structure-dotnet-solution.md`, `messaging.md` and `angular-high-frequency-ui.md` of this topic.

## Sources

- [S1] Dariusz Woźniak, "FOSSed", <https://dariusz-wozniak.github.io/fossed/>, read 2026-11-12, secondary source, summarized.
- [S2] dotnet/aspnetcore, "Announcement: Swashbuckle.AspNetCore is being removed in .NET 9", issue 54599, <https://github.com/dotnet/aspnetcore/issues/54599>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "Generate OpenAPI documents", "APIs overview" and dotnet "eShop", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/fundamentals/openapi/aspnetcore-openapi>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] App-vNext, "Polly.Extensions.Http", archived repository, <https://github.com/App-vNext/Polly.Extensions.Http>, and Microsoft, "Build resilient HTTP apps", read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] Microsoft, "Choosing a testing strategy" (EF Core), <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/testing/choosing-a-testing-strategy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S6] Angular, "Angular coding style guide" and "Best practices", <https://angular.dev/style-guide>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Real-time push to the browser

Contents: 1. Choice · 2. SignalR on several replicas · 3. Format and defaults · 4. Path of a value · Sources

## 1. Choice of transport (SignalR, SSE, gRPC)

| Criterion | SignalR | Server-Sent Events |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Direction | Both ways, hub method calls [S1] | Server to client only [S2] |
| Transport | WebSocket, then SSE, then long polling [S1] | HTTP, UTF-8 text [S2] |
| Broadcast and groups | Built in [S1] | To write |
| Format | JSON or MessagePack [S1] [S4] | Text |
| Reconnect | Stateful reconnect option since .NET 8 [S6] | Automatic in the browser [S2] |
| Limit | Session affinity on several servers [S3] | Without HTTP/2, 6 connections per browser and domain, all tabs together [S2] |
| .NET | `AddSignalR()`, `MapHub<T>()` | `TypedResults.ServerSentEvents(...)` since .NET 10 [S8] |

- Dashboards, monitoring and high-frequency updates are good candidates for SignalR. [S1]
- A browser cannot call gRPC directly: gRPC-Web or JSON transcoding; gRPC-Web supports only unary calls and server streaming from a browser. Keep gRPC between services. [S7]

## 2. SignalR on several replicas (scale out)

- Every request of one connection must reach the same server: session affinity is required. [S3]
- Exception: all clients use WebSocket only, with `skipNegotiation` on the client. [S3]
- The Redis backplane is the advised way on your own infrastructure; it keeps the affinity requirement, except for the case above. [S3]
- On Kubernetes, set affinity at the cluster entry, or use WebSocket only (`docs/kubernetes/ingress-gateway.md`).

## 3. Format and defaults (SignalR)

- MessagePack makes smaller messages than JSON, with unreadable traces; pitfalls: C# casing of property names on the client, `DateTime.Kind` not kept, strict types, `UntrustedData` option advised. [S4]
- For a continuous flow, a hub method that returns `IAsyncEnumerable<T>`; the client cancels by disposing its subscription. [S5]
- Defaults: incoming message limited to 32 KB; ping every 15 s; client lost after 30 s; 64 KB buffers before backpressure; detailed errors off. [S6]

## 4. Path of a value to a synoptic (SignalR and Angular)

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  S["Simulation service"] --> H["SignalR hub: one group per synoptic view"]
  H --> W["WebSocket"]
  W --> C["Angular service: batches the messages received"]
  C --> G["Signals: one per shown value"]
  G --> V["OnPush components: only those that read the signal refresh"]
```

No public source gives a measured rate for synoptics. Send batches of changes at a fixed rate rather than one message per variable; subscribe a client only to the views it shows; switch to MessagePack if message size matters; measure.

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Overview of ASP.NET Core SignalR", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/signalr/introduction>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Mozilla, "Using server-sent events", MDN, <https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Server-sent_events/Using_server-sent_events>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized in own words.
- [S3] Microsoft, "ASP.NET Core SignalR hosting and scaling", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/signalr/scale>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "Use MessagePack Hub Protocol in SignalR for ASP.NET Core", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/signalr/messagepackhubprotocol>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Microsoft, "Use streaming in ASP.NET Core SignalR", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/signalr/streaming>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S6] Microsoft, "ASP.NET Core SignalR configuration", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/signalr/configuration>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S7] Microsoft, "Compare gRPC services with HTTP APIs" and "gRPC-Web in ASP.NET Core gRPC apps", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/grpc/comparison>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S8] Microsoft, "What's new in ASP.NET Core in .NET 10", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/release-notes/aspnetcore-10.0>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

title: Architecture review checklist
topic: architecture
summary: Closed questions of an architecture review, by theme, each answered yes, no or unknown, with the themes to keep.

# Architecture review checklist

Contents: A. Goals · B. Decomposition · C. .NET structure · D. API contract · E. Real-time · F. Messaging · G. .NET services · H. Data · I. Angular · J. Live display · K. Libraries · Sources
Answer each question with yes, no or unknown. A "no" is a finding. An "unknown" is a question for the user.

## A. Goals and decisions (always)

- Are the quality goals written and ranked?
- Is there a record of the main decisions, each with its reason and the rejected alternative?
- Does each deployable unit, layer and library have a stated reason?

## B. Decomposition (when there is more than one deployable unit, or a split is proposed)

- Does each service exist for a stated need: a team that releases alone, a load to scale alone, a failure to isolate?
- Are the boundaries drawn around business capabilities, not technical layers?
- Does each service or module own its data? No table is read by two of them.
- Can each service be released without releasing another one the same day?
- Is one business operation done without a long chain of calls between services?
- If the team is small and the services are released together: is a modular monolith ruled out for a stated reason?

## C. .NET solution structure (when there is .NET code)

- Is the code organized by feature or module, with a public interface per module?
- Does each layer do work? No service or repository only forwards calls.
- Is CQRS used only where reads and writes differ? Not on simple CRUD.
- Are commands and queries plain classes called directly, with no mediator library required?
- Is there a test that enforces the dependency rules between modules or layers?

## D. API contract (when Angular calls a .NET API)

- Is the OpenAPI document generated by the built-in ASP.NET Core package, at build time?
- Is the generated document committed, so that a contract change shows in a diff?
- Is the Angular client generated from that document, not written by hand?
- Does the client generator support every schema the API emits? Check the OpenAPI version, `oneOf` and `anyOf`.
- Is the API version explicit, and are breaking changes shipped as a new version?
- Do errors use the problem details format, and is request validation enabled?
- Is the OpenAPI endpoint closed or protected outside development?

## E. Real-time (when the server pushes data to the browser)

- Is the transport chosen for the need: SignalR for two-way or groups, server-sent events for a simple one-way stream? gRPC is not called from the browser.
- With more than one replica: is session affinity set, or do all clients use WebSockets only with negotiation skipped?
- With more than one replica: is there a backplane?
- Are clients subscribed only to the views they display, through groups?
- Are changes sent in batches at a fixed rate, not one message per value?
- Is each message under the receive size limit? Is MessagePack considered when size matters?
- Are detailed errors off in production?
- Was the message rate measured against the goal?

## F. Messaging (when a queue or a bus is used or proposed)

- Is there a stated need to decouple in time: long work, retry after failure, several subscribers?
- Is a message written in the same transaction as the data, through an outbox?
- Are consumers idempotent?
- Is the license of the messaging library checked for its exact version?

## G. .NET service patterns (when there is .NET code)

- Does no singleton hold a scoped service?
- Are options bound, validated and checked at start?
- Is there exactly one resilience handler per HTTP client, with retries off for unsafe methods?
- Do log messages use named placeholders?
- Are logs, metrics and traces exported with OpenTelemetry?
- Are readiness and liveness checks separate? The liveness check calls no dependency.

## H. Data access (when a database is used)

- Is each database context short-lived, one per unit of work?
- Is no context used by two operations at the same time?
- Do data tests run against the real database engine?

## I. Angular structure and state (when there is Angular code)

- Are folders organized by feature, not by type?
- Are components standalone, with lazy-loaded feature routes?
- Is local state in signals, and derived state in `computed`?
- Is shared state in a service with signals? A store library is used only for a stated need.
- Are server reads done with `httpResource` or a service, and writes with `HttpClient`?
- With several apps or libraries: is there a lint rule that enforces module boundaries?

## J. Angular live display (when the screen shows values that change often)

- Does each displayed value come from a signal?
- Is each symbol a small component, with as few DOM nodes as possible?
- Are lists rendered with `@for` and `track`?
- Are incoming messages grouped before they update signals?
- Is heavy computation moved off the main thread?
- Was the display measured before any further optimization?

## K. Libraries and licenses (always)

- Is each library checked in `outdated-libraries-and-patterns.md`?
- For a library with a paid or restricted license: is the version pinned and the decision recorded?
- Does the code use a pattern listed as outdated for its framework version?

## Sources

- [S1] Research report "Software architecture, .NET and Angular" of this project, checklist annex, 2026-11-12, built from the documents of this topic and their sources.

title: Structure of a .NET solution

summary: Layers or vertical slices, module rules, CQRS written by hand without a mediator library, and when each fits. .NET 10.

# Structure of a .NET solution

Contents: 1. Styles · 2. CQRS without a library · 3. Architecture tests · Sources

## 1. Styles inside one application (clean architecture or vertical slices)

| Style | Idea | Fits when | Risk |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Clean architecture | Domain, Application, Infrastructure, Presentation layers; dependencies point inward | Rich domain, rules shared by features, long project | Over-engineering simple features; one feature touches several projects [S1] |
| Vertical slices | Code grouped by request or feature, from interface to data | Features of uneven complexity, small team, mostly CRUD API | Duplication; less consistency; needs a team that refactors [S1] [S2] |
| Mixed | Vertical slices inside a layered solution | The most common case in practice | — [S1] |

Rule of slices: little coupling between slices, much inside a slice. [S2] Remove every layer that only passes a call through. [S2]

## 2. CQRS without a library

- CQRS separates the write model from the read model; its simple form keeps one database; it does not fit a simple domain or a CRUD interface. [S3]
- The pattern needs no mediator: one class per command or query, called directly by the API endpoint. [S3]

```csharp
public sealed class CloseValveHandler(AppDbContext db)
{
    public async Task<Results<NoContent, NotFound>> HandleAsync(string tagName, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        Valve? valve = await db.Valves.SingleOrDefaultAsync(v => v.TagName == tagName, cancellationToken);
        if (valve is null)
        {
            return TypedResults.NotFound();
        }
        valve.Close();
        await db.SaveChangesAsync(cancellationToken);
        return TypedResults.NoContent();
    }
}

app.MapPost("/valves/{tagName}/close", (string tagName, CloseValveHandler handler, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    => handler.HandleAsync(tagName, cancellationToken));
```

MediatR 13 and later is no longer free for every company: see `docs/dotnet/paid-packages.md`.

## 3. Architecture tests (.NET)

- Check architecture rules at each build with a test: NetArchTest (MIT) states that "types of this namespace do not depend on that one". [S4]
- Write one test per module rule: no module reads the namespace of the data of another module.

## Sources

- [S1] Milan Jovanović, "Vertical Slice Architecture vs Clean Architecture", 2026-08-13, <https://milanjovanovic.tech/blog/vertical-slice-vs-clean-architecture.md>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Jimmy Bogard, "Vertical Slice Architecture", 2018-04-19, <https://www.jimmybogard.com/vertical-slice-architecture/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "CQRS pattern", Azure Architecture Center, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/architecture/patterns/cqrs>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] Ben Morris, "NetArchTest", README, <https://github.com/BenMorris/NetArchTest>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.

id: containers-angular-image

# Angular container image

Contents: 1. Build output · 2. Dockerfile · 3. Server · 4. Old patterns · Sources

## 1. Angular build output (ng build and dist)

- `ng build` writes to `dist/<app>/` and uses the production configuration by default. [S1]
- For a static server, copy the subfolder `dist/<app>/browser/`, not `dist/<app>/`. [S1]
- Every route the server does not know must return `index.html`. With static prerendering, the fallback file is `index.csr.html`. [S1]
- With server-side rendering, copy `browser/` and `server/`, then run `node dist/<app>/server/server.mjs`. [S1]

## 2. Angular Dockerfile (build stage and NGINX stage)

```dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
ARG NODE_VERSION=24
ARG NGINX_VERSION=1.29
FROM docker.io/library/node:${NODE_VERSION} AS build
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
RUN npm ci
COPY . .
RUN npm run build

FROM docker.io/nginx/nginx-unprivileged:${NGINX_VERSION} AS final
COPY nginx.conf /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf
COPY --from=build /app/dist/web/browser /usr/share/nginx/html
USER 101
EXPOSE 8080
```

- Replace `web` with the project name from `angular.json`.
- Take the Node.js version from the Angular version: Angular 22 needs Node.js 22.22, 24.15 or 26 (see `docs/typescript/nodejs.md`).
- Take the versions from the project; the values above are examples. The full template is `templates/containers/angular.Dockerfile`.

## 3. Unprivileged NGINX server (nginx.conf for an Angular app)

- The image `nginx/nginx-unprivileged` listens on 8080, has no `user` directive and writes its temporary files under `/tmp`. [S2]
- The old name `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged` does not receive the recent versions. Use `nginx/nginx-unprivileged`. [S2]

```nginx
server {
  listen 8080;
  root /usr/share/nginx/html;
  index index.html;

  location / {
    try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;
  }
}
```

The `try_files` line returns `index.html` for every unknown route, as Angular routing needs. This server block is a common pattern; check it once by starting the image.

## 4. Old patterns (Angular images)

| Old | Now |
| --- | --- |
| `COPY --from=build /app/dist/<app> /usr/share/nginx/html` | Copy `dist/<app>/browser` |
| `FROM nginx` and `listen 80` | `nginx/nginx-unprivileged`, `listen 8080` |
| `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged` | `nginx/nginx-unprivileged` |
| `ng build --prod` | `ng build` (production by default) |

## Sources

- [S1] Angular, "Deployment", v22, <https://angular.dev/tools/cli/deployment>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] NGINX, F5, "docker-nginx-unprivileged", README, <https://github.com/nginx/docker-nginx-unprivileged>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.

tool: docker, podman

# Container problems and their causes

Contents: 1. How to explain a problem · 2. Symptoms and causes · Sources

## 1. How to explain a container problem (method)

1. Ask for the exact command and its full output. Never guess from a summary.
2. Read the image name, the ports, the user and the engine (Docker or Podman) in the command or the file.
3. Match the output with the table below. Give the cause, then the smallest fix.
4. Say what you could not check: the image, the host and the network are not in the output.

## 2. Symptoms and causes (Docker and Podman)

| Symptom | Likely cause | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| A .NET app answers nothing on port 80 | Since .NET 8 the app listens on 8080 [S3] | Publish `-p <host port>:8080` |
| `permission denied` on bind to port 80 or 443 | Rootless container: ports under 1024 are refused [S1] | Listen on 8080 |
| Podman refuses an image name without a terminal | Short image name in `enforcing` mode [S2] | Write the full name with its registry |
| `failed to connect to the docker API` | No Docker daemon answers | Start the daemon, or skip the checks that need it |
| `MSB4018` in `CreateNewImage` | `dotnet publish /t:PublishContainer` without a daemon [S4] | Publish to an archive with `ContainerArchiveOutputPath` |
| `docker exec <container> sh` fails: no shell found | Chiseled .NET image: no shell [S3] | Use a full image to debug |
| Container cannot reach a service on the host | Rootless network "pasta": the host main address is not reachable [S1] | Use a service name or a published port, never the host address |
| The app fails when it writes a file | Root filesystem read-only, or a non-root user without rights on the folder | Write to a mounted volume or to `/tmp` |
| Exit code 137 | The process was killed, often for memory (`OOMKilled` in Kubernetes) | Raise the memory limit or find the leak |

## Sources

- [S1] Podman, "podman-rootless(7)", package 6.2.0, <https://man.archlinux.org/man/podman-rootless.7.en>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Red Hat, "Working with container registries", RHEL 10, <https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/10/html/building_running_and_managing_containers/working-with-container-registries>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, ".NET containers overview" and "What's new in containers for .NET 8", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/docker/introduction>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "Containerize a .NET app with dotnet publish", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/containers/sdk-publish>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# devcontainer.json properties and lifecycle

Contents: 1. Location · 2. Properties · 3. Lifecycle · 4. Example · Sources

## 1. Where the file lives (devcontainer.json location)

In this order of priority: `.devcontainer/devcontainer.json`, `.devcontainer.json`, `.devcontainer/<folder>/devcontainer.json`. [S2]

The file is JSON with comments. Paths are relative to the folder of `devcontainer.json`. [S1]

## 2. Useful properties (devcontainer.json)

| Group | Properties | To know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Origin | `image`; or `build.dockerfile` with `context`, `args`, `target`; or `dockerComposeFile` with `service` | One of the three |
| Users | `containerUser`, `remoteUser`, `updateRemoteUserUID` | `remoteUser` runs the scripts and the tools; on Linux its UID follows the host by default |
| Variables | `containerEnv`, `remoteEnv` | `containerEnv` is fixed at creation (rebuild to change it); `remoteEnv` applies to the processes the tool starts |
| Ports | `forwardPorts`, `portsAttributes` | Prefer `forwardPorts` to `appPort` |
| Mounts and rights | `mounts`, `runArgs`, `capAdd`, `securityOpt`, `privileged`, `init` | `runArgs` takes an array only |
| Tools | `features`, `customizations` | `customizations.vscode.extensions` for one editor |
| Substitutions | `${localWorkspaceFolder}`, `${containerWorkspaceFolder}`, `${localEnv:VAR}`, `${containerEnv:VAR}` | Default value: `${localEnv:VAR:default}` |

Sources: [S1] [S2].

## 3. Lifecycle commands, in order (devcontainer.json)

| Command | Where | When |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `initializeCommand` | Host | At initialization, at each start too |
| `onCreateCommand` | Container | After creation, once |
| `updateContentCommand` | Container | Then, when new content is there, at least once |
| `postCreateCommand` | Container | Then, once |
| `postStartCommand` | Container | At each start |
| `postAttachCommand` | Container | At each attach of a tool |

- When a command fails, the next ones are skipped. [S1]
- A string runs through `/bin/sh`; an array runs without a shell; an object runs its entries in parallel, and all must succeed. [S1] [S2]
- The tool waits for `updateContentCommand` by default (`waitFor`); `postCreateCommand` runs in the background. [S2]
- Put `dotnet restore` and `npm ci` in `updateContentCommand`. In `postCreateCommand`, a lint started right after the container starts can run before they end.

## 4. Example (.NET and Angular dev container)

```jsonc
{
  "name": "dotnet-angular",
  "build": { "dockerfile": "Dockerfile", "context": ".." },
  "remoteUser": "dev",
  "forwardPorts": [8080, 4200],
  "updateContentCommand": {
    "api": "dotnet restore",
    "ui": "npm ci"
  },
  "postStartCommand": "dotnet --version && node --version"
}
```

The coding assistant and the check tools are installed in the image of `.devcontainer/Dockerfile`; the full template is the folder `templates/containers/devcontainer/`.

The template also installs Poppler (`pdftotext`) and Tesseract (`tesseract`, English and French) for the skill `read-heavy-file`. Set the build argument `EXTRACT_TOOLS` to `"false"` when apt packages cannot be installed; then PDF text and OCR are not available.

## Sources

- [S1] Dev Containers, "Dev Container metadata reference", <https://containers.dev/implementors/json_reference/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Dev Containers, "Development Container Specification", <https://containers.dev/implementors/spec/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

topic: containers
summary: How the coding assistant runs commands in the dev container, the devcontainer CLI, and features without a registry.

# Run commands in a dev container

Contents: 1. The way of this base · 2. Other ways · 3. Features · Sources

## 1. The assistant runs inside the container (way of this base)

The coding assistant is installed in the image of the dev container and runs there. The check commands of `AGENTS.md` are then plain terminal commands: no prefix, no container name.

- Run every check command from the project root, inside the container.
- `remoteUser` and `remoteEnv` of `devcontainer.json` apply, because the assistant is started by the dev container tool.
- If a command is missing, it is missing from the image: say so. Never install it.

## 2. Other ways, from the host (devcontainer CLI and docker exec)

| Way | Command | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dev Container CLI | `devcontainer up --workspace-folder .` then `devcontainer exec --workspace-folder . <command>` | The CLI must be on the host; `stop` and `down` are not implemented [S2] |
| Container engine | `docker exec <container> <command>` | Does not apply `remoteUser`, `remoteEnv` nor the user environment, which `devcontainer exec` applies [S2] |

- `devcontainer read-configuration --workspace-folder .` prints the configuration of the project: it is the check for `devcontainer.json`. [S2] It first runs `docker ps`: without a container engine that answers, it fails (seen with CLI 0.90.0). Inside the dev container, say that the check needs a rebuild of the container by the user.
- The CLI writes a lock file `.devcontainer-lock.json`; `--frozen-lockfile` enforces it. [S2]
- Tools that read the specification: VS Code, GitHub Codespaces, IntelliJ IDEA (early support), DevPod and the reference CLI. [S3]

## 3. Features, with or without a registry (dev container features)

- A feature is referenced by an OCI registry (`<registry>/<namespace>/<id>[:version]`, for example `ghcr.io/devcontainers/features/go:1`), by an archive address, or by a local folder. [S1]
- A remote feature is downloaded at each build. When the registry cannot be reached, use a `build.dockerfile`, a prebuilt image, or a local feature. [S1]
- A local feature lives in a subfolder of `.devcontainer/` named after its `id`, with `devcontainer-feature.json` and `install.sh`. [S1]

## Sources

- [S1] Dev Containers, "Dev Container Features distribution", <https://containers.dev/implementors/features-distribution/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] devcontainers, "Dev Container CLI", README, <https://github.com/devcontainers/cli>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S3] Dev Containers, "Supporting tools and services", <https://containers.dev/supporting.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Dockerfile checks

Contents: 1. Two tools · 2. Commands · 3. Messages and fixes · 4. Settings · Sources

## 1. Two tools that complete each other (hadolint and docker build --check)

|  | hadolint | `docker build --check` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What it reads | The Dockerfile and the shell of each `RUN` (ShellCheck rules) | The Dockerfile, with the builder, without building |
| What it needs | Its binary, or its image | Dockerfile syntax 1.8, Buildx 0.15.0, a Docker daemon that answers |
| Sees alone | Missing tag or `latest`, last `USER` root, `ADD`, `pipefail`, package versions | Instruction case, undefined `ARG` and variables, ignored file copied, fixed platform |
| Exit code | Not zero from its failure threshold (default `info`) | Not zero with `--check` |

Both see the exec form, relative `WORKDIR`, secrets in `ARG` or `ENV`, a reserved stage name and a fixed platform. Run hadolint first: it works everywhere. Add the Docker checks when a daemon answers. [S1] [S2] [S3]

Neither tool builds the image. A Dockerfile that passes both can still fail to build. Say so when no build ran. [S1]

## 2. Commands (Dockerfile check commands)

```sh
hadolint Dockerfile
docker info >/dev/null && docker build --check -f Dockerfile .
```

- Without the binary, hadolint runs from its image: `docker run --rm -i hadolint/hadolint < Dockerfile`, or with Podman `podman run --rm -i docker.io/hadolint/hadolint < Dockerfile`. [S3]
- `docker build --check` prints `failed to connect to the docker API` when no daemon answers: skip it and say so. [S1]
- Output formats of hadolint: `-f tty` (default), `json`, `sarif`, `junit` and others. [S3]

## 3. Messages and fixes (hadolint and build check codes)

| Code | Meaning | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DL3006 | Image without a tag | Add a version tag |
| DL3007 | Image tag `latest` | Write a version tag |
| DL3002 | Last user is root | Add `USER` with a non-root account |
| DL3000, WorkdirRelativePath | Relative `WORKDIR` | Write an absolute path |
| DL3020 | `ADD` for local files | Use `COPY` |
| DL3025, JSONArgsRecommended | `CMD` or `ENTRYPOINT` in shell form | Write the JSON array form |
| DL3008 | System package without a version | Warning only: pin when you know the version |
| DL3009 | Package lists kept | `rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*` in the same `RUN` |
| DL3015 | Recommended packages installed | Add `--no-install-recommends` |
| DL4006 | Pipe without `pipefail` | `SHELL ["/bin/bash", "-o", "pipefail", "-c"]` |
| SecretsUsedInArgOrEnv | A secret name in `ARG` or `ENV` | Remove it from the image |
| CopyIgnoredFile | `COPY` of a file that `.dockerignore` excludes | Fix the copy or the ignore list |
| UndefinedVar, UndefinedArgInFrom | A variable never defined | Declare the `ARG` before it is used |

Sources: [S2] [S3]. Fix only the lines the tool names, then run the same commands again.

## 4. Settings (.hadolint.yaml and check comments)

- `.hadolint.yaml` next to the Dockerfile sets the failure threshold and the level of each rule. Keep DL3008 as a warning. [S3]
- `# hadolint ignore=DL3008` on the line above an instruction skips one rule for that line. Use it only with a reason in the same comment. [S3]
- `# check=skip=<codes>` at the top of the Dockerfile skips build checks; `# check=error=true` makes them fail the build. [S1] [S2]

## Sources

- [S1] Docker, "Checking your build configuration", <https://docs.docker.com/build/checks/>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized; and a test of the research without a daemon, Docker CLI 29.9.3, Buildx 0.38.1.
- [S2] Docker, "Build checks", <https://docs.docker.com/reference/build-checks/>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S3] hadolint, README, <https://github.com/hadolint/hadolint>, read 2026-11-12, GPL-3.0, rules described in own words.

title: Dockerfile rules
topic: containers
summary: Rules with wrong and right examples for a Dockerfile that passes hadolint and the Docker build checks.
tool: docker
version: "1.8"

# Dockerfile rules

Contents: 1. Build and run stages · 2. Base image and user · 3. Instructions · 4. Packages and shell · 5. Context and secrets · Sources

The same rules apply to a `Containerfile` (the name Podman also uses). Check every rule with the tools of `docs/containers/dockerfile-checks.md`.

## 1. Build in one stage, run in another (Dockerfile stages)

Build with the SDK image in a first stage. Copy only the output into a small runtime image. [S1]

Copy the dependency files first and restore them, then copy the rest of the source. The restore layer stays in the cache while the dependency files do not change. [S1]

Wrong:

```dockerfile
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0
COPY . /src
RUN dotnet publish /src/Api/Api.csproj -c Release -o /app
CMD dotnet /app/Api.dll
```

Right:

```dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:10.0 AS build
WORKDIR /source
COPY Api/Api.csproj Api/
RUN dotnet restore Api/Api.csproj
COPY Api/ Api/
RUN dotnet publish Api/Api.csproj -c Release -o /app --no-restore

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:10.0 AS final
WORKDIR /app
COPY --from=build /app ./
USER $APP_UID
EXPOSE 8080
ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "Api.dll"]
```

`# check=error=true` turns every warning of the Docker build checks into an error. [S2]

## 2. Base image tag and non-root user (Dockerfile FROM and USER)

| Rule | Wrong | Right | Check |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pin the base image | `FROM ubuntu`, `FROM node:latest` | `FROM node:24` ; a tag and a `@sha256:` digest for a frozen image | hadolint DL3006, DL3007 |
| Run as a non-root user | No `USER`, or `USER root` last | `USER` with a non-root account, by UID when Kubernetes checks `runAsNonRoot` | hadolint DL3002 |
| Write the full image name for Podman | `FROM nginx` | `FROM docker.io/library/nginx:<version>` | Podman refuses a short name without a terminal |

Sources: [S1] [S3]. With Podman, a short name resolves through a list of registries; in `enforcing` mode it fails without a terminal. [S1]

## 3. Instructions (Dockerfile WORKDIR, COPY, CMD and ENTRYPOINT)

| Rule | Wrong | Right | Check |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Exec form for the start command | `CMD dotnet App.dll` | `ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "App.dll"]` | JSONArgsRecommended, DL3025 |
| Absolute working folder | `WORKDIR app` | `WORKDIR /app` | WorkdirRelativePath, DL3000 |
| `COPY` rather than `ADD` | `ADD src/ /app` | `COPY src/ /app` | DL3020 |

The exec form passes signals to the process: the container stops cleanly. The shell form starts `/bin/sh` first. [S1] [S2] [S3]

## 4. Packages and shell (Dockerfile RUN)

- Run `apt-get update` and `apt-get install` in the same `RUN`, with `--no-install-recommends`, and remove the package lists in the same `RUN` (DL3015, DL3009). [S1] [S3]
- Put `set -o pipefail &&` before a command with a pipe; otherwise the step hides a failure (DL4006). [S3]
- Version pinning of system packages (DL3008) stays a warning in `.hadolint.yaml`. Never invent a package version number: write the version only when you know it.

Wrong:

```dockerfile
RUN apt-get update
RUN apt-get install -y curl
RUN curl -fsSL https://example.org/tool.tar.gz | tar -xz
```

Right:

```dockerfile
RUN apt-get update \
 && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends curl \
 && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*
SHELL ["/bin/bash", "-o", "pipefail", "-c"]
```

## 5. Build context and secrets (Dockerfile .dockerignore, ARG and ENV)

- Keep a `.dockerignore` next to the Dockerfile: `.git`, `**/bin`, `**/obj`, `**/node_modules`, `**/dist`, `.angular`. A file that is ignored but copied triggers the check `CopyIgnoredFile`. [S1] [S2]
- Never put a password, a token or a key in `ARG` or `ENV`: it stays in the image history (SecretsUsedInArgOrEnv). [S2]
- Use `--pull` to refresh the base image and `--no-cache` to rebuild every layer. [S1]

## Sources

- [S1] Docker, "Building best practices", <https://docs.docker.com/build/building/best-practices/>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized; Red Hat, "Working with container registries", RHEL 10, for short names.
- [S2] Docker, "Build checks" and "Checking your build configuration", <https://docs.docker.com/reference/build-checks/>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S3] hadolint, README, <https://github.com/hadolint/hadolint>, read 2026-11-12, GPL-3.0, rules described in own words.

---
id: containers-dotnet-images
title: .NET container images
topic: containers
summary: Image names, tags, non-root user, port 8080 and image publishing without a Dockerfile for .NET 10.
tool: dotnet
version: "10"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2, S3, S4, S5]

---

# .NET container images

Contents: 1. Images · 2. Port and user · 3. Dockerfile · 4. Without a Dockerfile · 5. Old patterns · Sources

## 1. Images and base system (.NET images)

- Build with `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:<version>`. Run an ASP.NET Core app with `mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:<version>`. [S1] [S4]
- From .NET 10, the default tags are on Ubuntu 24.04. There are no Debian images for .NET 10. [S5]
- "Chiseled" images are a reduced Ubuntu: no shell, no package manager, a non-root user already active, about 100 MB smaller. Without the `extra` variant they have no culture data. [S1] [S3]
- A chiseled image has no shell: `docker exec <container> sh` fails. Debug with a full image, or read logs from outside.

## 2. Port and user (.NET images since .NET 8)

| Point | Value | Source |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Port | 8080 by default since .NET 8 (80 before) | [S1] [S3] |
| Variable for the port | `ASPNETCORE_HTTP_PORTS` | [S3] |
| User | Non-root account `app`, UID 1654, in every image since .NET 8 | [S1] [S3] |
| Variable for the user | `APP_UID` | [S2] [S3] |

- Going back to port 80 forbids the non-root user: ports under 1024 need root. Keep 8080. [S3]
- Kubernetes checks `runAsNonRoot` with a UID, not a name: write `USER $APP_UID`, not `USER app`. [S3]

## 3. A .NET Dockerfile (.NET image build)

```dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
ARG DOTNET_VERSION=10.0
FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:${DOTNET_VERSION} AS build
WORKDIR /source
COPY Api/Api.csproj Api/
RUN dotnet restore Api/Api.csproj
COPY Api/ Api/
RUN dotnet publish Api/Api.csproj -c Release -o /app --no-restore

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:${DOTNET_VERSION} AS final
WORKDIR /app
COPY --from=build /app ./
USER $APP_UID
EXPOSE 8080
ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "Api.dll"]
```

Read the .NET version of the project in `TargetFramework` (`net10.0` gives `10.0`) and set `DOTNET_VERSION`. The full template is `templates/containers/dotnet.Dockerfile`. [S4]

## 4. Publish an image without a Dockerfile (dotnet publish PublishContainer)

```sh
dotnet publish --os linux --arch x64 /t:PublishContainer -p ContainerImageTag=1.4.0
dotnet publish --os linux --arch x64 /t:PublishContainer -p ContainerArchiveOutputPath=./images/api.tar.gz
```

- The first form needs a container daemon that answers; without one it fails with MSB4018 (task `CreateNewImage`). The second writes an archive and needs no daemon. Load it with `docker load -i` or `podman load -i`. [S2]
- Push to a registry with `-p ContainerRegistry=<registry>`. [S2]
- The default tag is `latest`. Always set `ContainerImageTag` to a version. [S2]
- Other properties: `ContainerRepository` (default: assembly name), `ContainerFamily`, `ContainerBaseImage`, `ContainerUser`, `ContainerPort`, `ContainerEnvironmentVariable`. `ContainerEntrypoint` is deprecated since .NET 8: use `ContainerAppCommand`. [S2]

## 5. Old patterns (.NET images before .NET 8 and 10)

| Old | Now | Since |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `EXPOSE 80`, `ASPNETCORE_URLS` set to port 80 | `EXPOSE 8080`, `ASPNETCORE_HTTP_PORTS` | .NET 8 |
| Image runs as root | `USER $APP_UID` | .NET 8 |
| Tags `-bookworm-slim` (Debian) | Default tags (Ubuntu 24.04) | .NET 10 |
| `ContainerEntrypoint` | `ContainerAppCommand` | .NET 8 |

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, ".NET containers overview", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/docker/introduction>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "Containerize a .NET app with dotnet publish" and "Containerize a .NET app reference", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/containers/sdk-publish>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "What's new in containers for .NET 8", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/whats-new/dotnet-8/containers>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "Building .NET Docker images", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/docker/building-net-docker-images>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, adapted.

# Podman Quadlet and kube play

Contents: 1. Quadlet · 2. kube play · 3. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. A container as a service (Podman Quadlet)

- A file `.container` (or `.volume`, `.network`, `.kube`, `.image`, `.build`, `.pod`) becomes a systemd service after `systemctl daemon-reload`. [S1]
- Folders: `/etc/containers/systemd/` for root, `~/.config/containers/systemd/` for a user. [S1]
- `api.container` gives the service `api.service`; the container is named `systemd-api`. [S1]
- The generated services are transient: `systemctl enable` does not apply. Start at boot with an `[Install]` section and `WantedBy=default.target`. [S1]
- Dry run of the generator: `/usr/lib/systemd/system-generators/podman-system-generator --user --dryrun`. [S1]
- Pulling an image can take longer than the 90 seconds systemd allows: set `TimeoutStartSec` or pull the image first. [S1]

```ini
# ~/.config/containers/systemd/api.container
[Container]
Image=registry.example.internal/api:1.4.0
PublishPort=8080:8080

[Service]
TimeoutStartSec=300

[Install]
WantedBy=default.target
```

## 2. A Kubernetes YAML on one machine (podman kube play)

- `podman kube play app.yaml` creates the containers, pods and volumes of the file; `--down` removes them; `--replace` creates them again. [S2]
- Kinds it handles: Pod, Deployment, PersistentVolumeClaim, ConfigMap, Secret, DaemonSet, Job. Service and Ingress are not handled. [S2]
- Fields it does not handle, among others: `imagePullSecrets`, `readinessProbe`, `startupProbe`. [S2]
- With the tag `:latest`, Podman tries to pull the image. [S2]
- It expects best a file made by `podman kube generate`. [S2]

`podman kube play` runs a Pod locally. It does not validate a manifest for a cluster: use `docs/kubernetes/validate-offline.md` for that.

## 3. Pitfalls (Quadlet and kube play)

- `systemctl enable api.service` for a Quadlet unit: it fails. Use the `[Install]` section.
- A short image name in `Image=`: write the full name with its registry.
- A Service or an Ingress in the file for `kube play`: Podman ignores them; publish ports in the Pod instead.

## Sources

- [S1] Podman, "podman-systemd.unit(5)", v6.1.0, <https://docs.podman.io/en/v6.1.0/markdown/podman-systemd.unit.5.html>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Podman, "podman-kube-play(1)", <https://docs.podman.io/en/stable/markdown/podman-kube-play.1.html>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.

# Podman compared with Docker

Contents: 1. What differs · 2. Same commands · 3. Registries and mirrors · 4. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. What differs with Podman (defaults that make a command fail)

| Point | Podman behavior | What to write |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Image names | A short name resolves through a list of registries. In `enforcing` mode, without a terminal, the command fails. | Always the full name: `docker.io/library/nginx:<version>`, never `nginx`. [S3] |
| Rootless ports | Ports under 1024 are refused (kernel setting `net.ipv4.ip_unprivileged_port_start`). | Publish a high port: `-p 8080:8080`. [S1] |
| Rootless limits | 65,536 user ids only; no device creation; home folder on NFS not handled. | Do not assume root in the container. [S1] |
| Rootless network | "pasta" by default since Podman 5.0: the container does not reach the main address of the host; the default port forwarder hides the client address. | Never write the host address in a configuration. [S1] |
| Compose | `podman compose` passes its arguments to an external provider: `docker-compose` if installed, else `podman-compose`. Set it with `compose_providers` or `PODMAN_COMPOSE_PROVIDER`. | Install a provider next to Podman. [S2] |
| Build file | Podman also reads a file named `Containerfile`. | Treat both names the same. |

## 2. Same commands (Docker and Podman equivalents)

| Need | Docker | Podman |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Run a tool in a container | `docker run --rm -i hadolint/hadolint < Dockerfile` | `podman run --rm -i docker.io/hadolint/hadolint < Dockerfile` |
| Load an image archive | `docker load -i image.tar.gz` | `podman load -i image.tar.gz` |
| Publish a .NET image with the SDK | The daemon is found | The same, no extra setting |

Only these three lines were checked in primary sources. For other commands, run `podman <command> --help` before you write an option. [S1] [S2]

## 3. Registries and mirrors (Podman registries.conf)

- Registries and mirrors are set in `registries.conf`: one `[[registry]]` entry, then its `[[registry.mirror]]` entries, tried in order. `insecure=true` allows a local registry without TLS. [S3]
- Example:

```toml
[[registry]]
prefix = "docker.io"
location = "docker.io"

[[registry.mirror]]
location = "registry.example.internal:5000"
insecure = true
```

Write the real mirror of the team in the `AGENTS.md` of the project, not in this base.

## 4. Pitfalls of a model with Podman

- A short image name in a `podman` command, a Quadlet file or a `FROM`.
- Port 80 or 443 published from a rootless container.
- `systemctl enable` for a Quadlet service (see `docs/containers/podman-quadlet-kube.md`).
- `podman compose` or `docker build --check` used as if nothing else were needed.
- A success of `podman kube play` taken as proof that a manifest is right for a cluster.

## Sources

- [S1] Podman, "podman-rootless(7)", package 6.2.0, <https://man.archlinux.org/man/podman-rootless.7.en>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Podman, "podman-compose(1)", <https://docs.podman.io/en/stable/markdown/podman-compose.1.html>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S3] Red Hat, "Working with container registries", RHEL 10, <https://docs.redhat.com/en/documentation/red_hat_enterprise_linux/10/html/building_running_and_managing_containers/working-with-container-registries>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Analyse C# et .NET

Les règles d'analyse que le build fait échouer, chacune avec un exemple faux et un exemple juste,

| `nullability-rules.md` | Valeurs qui peuvent être nulles, propriétés `required`, aides `ThrowIfNull`. |

| `style-rules.md` | Noms d'interfaces, modificateurs d'accès, accolades, `readonly` ; ce que le formatage corrige seul. |

Les deux commandes : `bash scripts/format-csharp.sh`, puis `dotnet build`. La ligne

# C# analysis setup

Contents: 1. Files · 2. Commands · 3. Strict formatting · 4. Reading the errors · Sources

## 1. The configuration files (.NET analyzers)

Three files of `templates/csharp/` go to the root of the .NET project or solution:

- `Directory.Build.props`: nullable reference types on, every warning is an error, `AnalysisLevel` set to the release of the project (never `latest`), code style checked during the build, and the `StrictFormatting` switch. [S1]
- `.editorconfig`: the closed list of rules that fail the build, one line per rule ID, with the reason in a comment. [S1]
- `format-csharp.sh`, in the `scripts/` folder of the project: the format command.

Never change these files to remove an error. Fix the code.

## 2. The commands (.NET SDK)

Run them from the folder that holds `Directory.Build.props`, in this order:

1. Format: `bash scripts/format-csharp.sh`
2. Build and analyze: `dotnet build --no-restore -tl:off -nologo -v:q > /tmp/build.log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"`
3. List the errors: `grep -E ": (error|warning) " /tmp/build.log | sort -u | head -n 30`
4. Check async void, which must print nothing: `grep -rn --include=*.cs "async void" . | grep -v EventArgs`
5. Check the format: `bash scripts/format-csharp.sh --check`

Run `dotnet restore` once before, and again after a package changes. [S2]

## 3. The strict formatting switch (StrictFormatting)

The line `<StrictFormatting>false</StrictFormatting>` of `Directory.Build.props` selects the formatter:

- `false`: `dotnet format` applies spaces, style and analyzer fixes. It never wraps a long line. [S2]
- `true`: `dotnet format style` and `dotnet format analyzers`, then CSharpier, which also wraps lines longer than 100 characters. [S3]

The strict mode needs CSharpier once per project: `dotnet new tool-manifest`, then `dotnet tool install csharpier`.

## 4. Reading the errors (MSBuild output)

Each error line gives the file, the line, the rule ID and a message: `Valve.cs(12,20): error CA2016: …`. Fix the first one, then build again: later errors often come from the first. Find the rule ID in the index of this base, open that document, and read only its section.

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Code analysis in .NET: configuration options", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/configuration-options>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "dotnet format", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/tools/dotnet-format>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] CSharpier, "CSharpier documentation", version 1.4, <https://csharpier.com/docs/About>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.

# C# async rules

Contents: 1. CA2016 · 2. CA1849 · 3. CS4014 · 4. async void · Sources

## 1. Forward the CancellationToken (CA2016)

Pass the `CancellationToken` parameter of your method to every call that accepts one. [S1]

Why: without the token, the called method keeps running after the caller cancels.

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class FileLoader
{
    public async Task<string> LoadAsync(string path, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        return await File.ReadAllTextAsync(path);
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class FileLoader
{
    public async Task<string> LoadAsync(string path, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        return await File.ReadAllTextAsync(path, cancellationToken);
    }
}
```

## 2. Call the async API in an async method (CA1849)

In an async method, call and await the async version of an API. Never block with `.Result`, `.Wait()` or a synchronous call. [S1] [S3]

Why: a blocking call keeps a thread busy for nothing and can deadlock.

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class LineCounter
{
    public async Task<int> CountAsync(string path, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        await Task.Delay(10, cancellationToken);
        string text = File.ReadAllText(path);
        return text.Split('\n').Length;
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class LineCounter
{
    public async Task<int> CountAsync(string path, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        string text = await File.ReadAllTextAsync(path, cancellationToken);
        return text.Split('\n').Length;
    }
}
```

## 3. Await every task (CS4014)

Await every call that returns a `Task`. [S2]

Why: a task that nobody awaits runs on its own; its error is lost.

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class ValveOpener(IValveRepository repository)
{
    public async Task OpenAsync(Valve valve, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        valve.IsOpen = true;
        repository.SaveAsync(valve, cancellationToken);
        await Task.Delay(10, cancellationToken);
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class ValveOpener(IValveRepository repository)
{
    public async Task OpenAsync(Valve valve, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        valve.IsOpen = true;
        await repository.SaveAsync(valve, cancellationToken);
    }
}
```

## 4. Return Task, never async void (project check async-void)

Return `Task` or `Task<T>` from an async method. Write `async void` only for an event handler, whose second parameter is an `EventArgs`. [S3]

Why: the caller cannot await an `async void` method, and its exception stops the process.

No rule of the SDK reports it. Check it with: `grep -rn --include=*.cs "async void" . | grep -v EventArgs`. The command must print nothing.

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class ValveSaver(IValveRepository repository)
{
    public async void Save(Valve valve)
    {
        await repository.SaveAsync(valve, CancellationToken.None);
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class ValveSaver(IValveRepository repository)
{
    public async Task SaveAsync(Valve valve, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
    {
        await repository.SaveAsync(valve, cancellationToken);
    }
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Code quality rules: CA2016, CA1849", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/quality-rules/>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "Compiler Warning (level 1) CS4014", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/compiler-messages/async-await-errors>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "Asynchronous programming scenarios", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/asynchronous-programming/async-scenarios>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

---
id: csharp-analysis-collection-disposal-rules
title: C# collection and disposal rules
topic: csharp-analysis
summary: CA1827, CA1860, CA1854, CA1869 and CA1001, with wrong and right examples. .NET 10.
tool: dotnet

# C# collection and disposal rules

Contents: 1. Any, Count, Length · 2. TryGetValue · 3. JsonSerializerOptions · 4. Disposable fields · Sources

## 1. Any for a sequence, Count or Length for a list (CA1827, CA1829, CA1860)

Test a sequence (`IEnumerable<T>`) with `Any()`. Test a list or an array with its `Count` or `Length` property. Never call the `Count()` method when the property exists. [S1]

Why: `Count()` walks the whole sequence, and `Any()` on a list is slower than reading `Count`.

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class Work
{
    public static bool HasWork(IEnumerable<Valve> pending, List<Valve> done)
    {
        return pending.Count() > 0 || done.Any();
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class Work
{
    public static bool HasWork(IEnumerable<Valve> pending, List<Valve> done)
    {
        return pending.Any() || done.Count > 0;
    }
}
```

## 2. Read a dictionary with TryGetValue (CA1854)

Read a dictionary with `TryGetValue`, never with `ContainsKey` followed by the indexer. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class Levels
{
    public static double Read(Dictionary<string, double> levels, string tank)
    {
        if (levels.ContainsKey(tank))
        {
            return levels[tank];
        }

        return 0;
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class Levels
{
    public static double Read(Dictionary<string, double> levels, string tank)
    {
        return levels.TryGetValue(tank, out double level) ? level : 0;
    }
}
```

## 3. Create JsonSerializerOptions once (CA1869)

Create `JsonSerializerOptions` once, in a `static readonly` field. [S1]

Why: each new options object rebuilds its cache, which is slow.

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class ValveJson
{
    public static string Write(Valve valve)
    {
        return JsonSerializer.Serialize(valve, new JsonSerializerOptions { WriteIndented = true });
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class ValveJson
{
    private static readonly JsonSerializerOptions Options = new() { WriteIndented = true };

    public static string Write(Valve valve)
    {
        return JsonSerializer.Serialize(valve, Options);
    }
}
```

## 4. Dispose the disposable fields you own (CA1001, CA2213)

A class that owns a disposable field implements `IDisposable` and disposes that field. Create a local disposable object with a `using` declaration. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class LogWriter
{
    private readonly StreamWriter writer = new("valves.log");

    public void Write(string line) => writer.WriteLine(line);
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class LogWriter : IDisposable
{
    private readonly StreamWriter writer = new("valves.log");

    public void Write(string line) => writer.WriteLine(line);

    public void Dispose() => writer.Dispose();
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Code quality rules: performance (CA1827, CA1829, CA1854, CA1860, CA1869) and design (CA1001), usage (CA2213)", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/quality-rules/performance-warnings>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# C# logging and exception rules

Contents: 1. CA2254 · 2. CA1727 · 3. CA1031 · 4. CA2200 · Sources

## 1. Log with a constant template (CA2254)

Write the log message as a constant template with named placeholders. Pass the values as arguments. Never use string interpolation in a log call. [S1]

Why: a constant template keeps each value as a separate field that a log tool can search.

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class ValveLog(ILogger<ValveLog> logger)
{
    public void Opened(string tag)
    {
        logger.LogInformation($"Valve {tag} opened");
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class ValveLog(ILogger<ValveLog> logger)
{
    public void Opened(string tag)
    {
        logger.LogInformation("Valve {Tag} opened", tag);
    }
}
```

## 2. Name placeholders in PascalCase (CA1727)

Write `{Tag}`, never `{tag}`, in a log template. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class PumpLog(ILogger<PumpLog> logger)
{
    public void Started(string tag)
    {
        logger.LogInformation("Pump {tag} started", tag);
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class PumpLog(ILogger<PumpLog> logger)
{
    public void Started(string tag)
    {
        logger.LogInformation("Pump {Tag} started", tag);
    }
}
```

## 3. Catch the specific exception (CA1031)

Catch the exception types you can handle, such as `IOException`. Never catch `Exception`, and never leave a `catch` block empty. [S2]

Why: `catch (Exception)` also hides the errors you cannot handle.

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class Importer(ILogger<Importer> logger)
{
    public void Remove(string path)
    {
        try
        {
            File.Delete(path);
        }
        catch (Exception exception)
        {
            logger.LogError(exception, "Remove failed for {Path}", path);
        }
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class Importer(ILogger<Importer> logger)
{
    public void Remove(string path)
    {
        try
        {
            File.Delete(path);
        }
        catch (IOException exception)
        {
            logger.LogError(exception, "Remove failed for {Path}", path);
        }
    }
}
```

## 4. Rethrow with throw; (CA2200)

To rethrow the caught exception, write `throw;`. Never write `throw exception;`. [S2]

Why: `throw exception;` starts a new stack trace and loses where the error came from.

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class Copier(ILogger<Copier> logger)
{
    public void Copy(string source, string target)
    {
        try
        {
            File.Copy(source, target);
        }
        catch (IOException exception)
        {
            logger.LogError(exception, "Copy failed for {Source}", source);
            throw exception;
        }
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class Copier(ILogger<Copier> logger)
{
    public void Copy(string source, string target)
    {
        try
        {
            File.Copy(source, target);
        }
        catch (IOException exception)
        {
            logger.LogError(exception, "Copy failed for {Source}", source);
            throw;
        }
    }
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "CA2254: Template should be a static expression" and "CA1727: Use PascalCase for named placeholders", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/quality-rules/ca2254>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "CA1031: Do not catch general exception types" and "CA2200: Rethrow to preserve stack details", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/quality-rules/ca1031>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

tool: dotnet

# C# nullability rules

Contents: 1. CS8602 · 2. CS8603 · 3. CS8618 · 4. CA1510 · Sources

Nullable reference types are on: `string` can never be null, `string?` can. Never write `!` after a
value to silence a warning: check the value. [S1]

## 1. Check a value that can be null before you use it (CS8602)

Read a member of a nullable value only after a null check, or with `?.` and `??`. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class SensorUnits
{
    public static string UnitOf(Dictionary<string, Sensor> sensors, string name)
    {
        Sensor? sensor = sensors.GetValueOrDefault(name);
        return sensor.Unit;
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class SensorUnits
{
    public static string UnitOf(Dictionary<string, Sensor> sensors, string name)
    {
        Sensor? sensor = sensors.GetValueOrDefault(name);
        return sensor?.Unit ?? "unknown";
    }
}
```

## 2. Say when a method can return null (CS8603)

When a method can return null, add `?` to its return type. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class ValveFinder
{
    public static Valve Find(IEnumerable<Valve> valves, string tag)
    {
        return valves.FirstOrDefault(valve => valve.Tag == tag);
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class ValveFinder
{
    public static Valve? Find(IEnumerable<Valve> valves, string tag)
    {
        return valves.FirstOrDefault(valve => valve.Tag == tag);
    }
}
```

## 3. Give every non-nullable property a value (CS8618)

Mark a property that the caller must set `required`, or give it a value. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class Pump
{
    public string Tag { get; set; }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class Pump
{
    public required string Tag { get; set; }
}
```

## 4. Check arguments with the throw helpers (CA1510)

Write `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(value)`. Also: `ArgumentException.ThrowIfNullOrEmpty` (CA1511), `ArgumentOutOfRangeException.ThrowIfNegative` (CA1512), `ObjectDisposedException.ThrowIf` (CA1513). [S2]

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class ValveGuard
{
    public static void Open(Valve valve)
    {
        if (valve is null)
        {
            throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(valve));
        }

        valve.IsOpen = true;
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class ValveGuard
{
    public static void Open(Valve valve)
    {
        ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(valve);
        valve.IsOpen = true;
    }
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Resolve nullable warnings", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/compiler-messages/nullable-warnings>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "CA1510: Use ArgumentNullException throw helper", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/quality-rules/ca1510>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

---
id: csharp-analysis-string-culture-rules
title: C# string and culture rules
topic: csharp-analysis
summary: CA1305, CA1310 and CA1862, with wrong and right examples. .NET 10.
tool: dotnet
version: "10.0"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1]
license: MIT, examples written for this base; rule IDs from the .NET documentation, CC BY 4.0

# C# string and culture rules

Contents: 1. CA1305 · 2. CA1310 · 3. CA1862 · Sources

## 1. Parse and format with an explicit culture (CA1305)

Pass `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` when you parse or format numbers and dates for files and data. Pass `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture` only for text shown to the user. [S1]

Why: the default culture changes with the machine. A machine set to French writes `3,14` and can fail to read `3.14`.

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class PressureText
{
    public static string Format(string text)
    {
        double pressure = double.Parse(text);
        return pressure.ToString("F2");
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class PressureText
{
    public static string Format(string text)
    {
        double pressure = double.Parse(text, CultureInfo.InvariantCulture);
        return pressure.ToString("F2", CultureInfo.InvariantCulture);
    }
}
```

## 2. Compare strings with a StringComparison (CA1310)

Pass a `StringComparison` to `StartsWith`, `EndsWith`, `IndexOf` and `Compare`. Use `Ordinal` for identifiers, tags, keys and paths. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class Tags
{
    public static bool IsValve(string tag) => tag.StartsWith("V-");
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class Tags
{
    public static bool IsValve(string tag) => tag.StartsWith("V-", StringComparison.Ordinal);
}
```

## 3. Compare without case, never with ToLower (CA1862)

Write `string.Equals(a, b, StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase)`. Never call `ToLower()` or `ToUpper()` to compare. [S1]

Why: `ToLower()` creates a new string, and it depends on the culture (CA1311).

Wrong:

```csharp
public static class Kinds
{
    public static bool IsPump(string kind) => kind.ToLower() == "pump";
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class Kinds
{
    public static bool IsPump(string kind) => string.Equals(kind, "pump", StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase);
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Code quality rules: globalization (CA1304, CA1305, CA1309, CA1310, CA1311) and performance (CA1862)", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/quality-rules/globalization-warnings>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

title: C# style rules
topic: csharp-analysis
summary: IDE1006, IDE0040, IDE0011 and IDE0044 with examples; the style rules the format command fixes. .NET 10.
tool: dotnet

# C# style rules

Contents: 1. IDE1006 · 2. IDE0040 · 3. IDE0011 · 4. IDE0044 · 5. Fixed by the format command · Sources

## 1. Start an interface name with I (IDE1006)

Name an interface `IValveStore`, never `ValveStore`. The format command cannot rename it for you. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public interface ValveStore
{
    Valve? Find(string tag);
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public interface IValveStore
{
    Valve? Find(string tag);
}
```

## 2. Write an access modifier on every type and member (IDE0040)

Write `public`, `internal` or `private` on every type and member. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
sealed class Counter
{
    public int Count { get; private set; }

    public void Add() => Count++;
}
```

Right:

```csharp
internal sealed class Counter
{
    public int Count { get; private set; }

    public void Add() => Count++;
}
```

## 3. Put braces around every body (IDE0011)

Put braces around the body of every `if`, `else`, `for`, `foreach` and `while`. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class Gauge
{
    public int Level { get; private set; }

    public void Raise()
    {
        if (Level < 10) Level++;
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class Gauge
{
    public int Level { get; private set; }

    public void Raise()
    {
        if (Level < 10)
        {
            Level++;
        }
    }
}
```

## 4. Mark a field readonly when nothing changes it (IDE0044)

Mark a private field `readonly` when only its initializer or the constructor sets it. [S1]

Wrong:

```csharp
public sealed class Tank
{
    private int capacity = 40;

    public double Percent(int level) => level * 100.0 / capacity;
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public sealed class Tank
{
    private readonly int capacity = 40;

    public double Percent(int level) => level * 100.0 / capacity;
}
```

## 5. Rules that the format command fixes (C# code style)

Never fix these by hand: run the format command. It applies IDE0005 (unused `using`), IDE0161 (file-scoped namespace), IDE0065 (`using` outside the namespace), IDE0036 (order of modifiers), IDE0051 and IDE0052 (unused private members) and IDE0059 (unused values), and the spaces and line breaks.

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Code-style rules", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/fundamentals/code-analysis/style-rules/>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
