# .NET integration tests

Contents: 1. An API · 2. A database · 3. The clock · 4. HttpClient and SignalR · Sources

## 1. An API (WebApplicationFactory)

- Package `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc.Testing`; test project in `Sdk="Microsoft.NET.Sdk.Web"`. [S26]
- `WebApplicationFactory<Program>` as a class fixture; `CreateClient()` gives an `HttpClient` on the in-memory server.
- Replace a service for one test: `WithWebHostBuilder`, then `ConfigureTestServices`.
- The default environment is `Development`.

```csharp
public sealed class ValvesApiTests(WebApplicationFactory<Program> factory) : IClassFixture<WebApplicationFactory<Program>>
{
    [Fact]
    public async Task Get_returns_the_valve()
    {
        var client = factory.CreateClient();

        var response = await client.GetAsync("/valves/V-101", TestContext.Current.CancellationToken);

        Assert.Equal(HttpStatusCode.OK, response.StatusCode);
    }
}
```

## 2. A database (EF Core in tests)

| Option | Use it |
| --- | --- |
| The real database engine, in a container | First choice |
| A repository interface with a fake | When a double is needed |
| SQLite in memory, connection kept open | When neither is possible |
| EF Core in-memory provider | Never: fewer queries, no transactions, no raw SQL |
| A mocked `DbSet` | Never |

[S27]

- SQLite in memory: open one `SqliteConnection("DataSource=:memory:")`, keep it open for the test, and register the context with `UseSqlite(connection)`. In `WebApplicationFactory`, remove the existing context options first. [S26]
- A container (Testcontainers) needs a container engine and the database image already on the workstation: ask before you add it. [S29]

## 3. The clock (FakeTimeProvider)

- Package `Microsoft.Extensions.TimeProvider.Testing`, namespace `Microsoft.Extensions.Time.Testing`. [S28]
- `new FakeTimeProvider()` starts at 2000-01-01 UTC; `Advance(TimeSpan)` moves time without waiting; `SetUtcNow` never goes back.
- `Task.Delay(delay, timeProvider)` ends only after an `Advance` that covers the delay.
- In dependency injection: `services.AddSingleton<TimeProvider>(fakeTime)`. Never mix `TimeProvider.System` and the fake in one test.

```csharp
var time = new FakeTimeProvider(new DateTimeOffset(2026, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, TimeSpan.Zero));
var valve = new MotorValve(time, travelTime: TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10));

valve.Open();
time.Advance(TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10));

Assert.True(valve.IsOpen);
```

## 4. HttpClient and SignalR

- `HttpClient`: give it a handler written for the test, with no package.

```csharp
sealed class StubHandler(HttpStatusCode status, string body) : HttpMessageHandler
{
    protected override Task<HttpResponseMessage> SendAsync(HttpRequestMessage request, CancellationToken cancellationToken) =>
        Task.FromResult(new HttpResponseMessage(status) { Content = new StringContent(body) });
}

var client = new HttpClient(new StubHandler(HttpStatusCode.OK, "{\"isOpen\":true}")) { BaseAddress = new Uri("http://test") };
```

- SignalR: connect a `HubConnection` to the in-memory server with `options.HttpMessageHandlerFactory = _ => server.CreateHandler()`; for WebSockets, also `options.WebSocketFactory` with `server.CreateWebSocketClient()`. [S32]

## Sources

- [S26] Microsoft, "Integration tests in ASP.NET Core", Microsoft Learn, 2026-10-23, <https://learn.microsoft.com/aspnet/core/test/integration-tests>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S27] Microsoft, "Choosing a testing strategy", EF Core docs, updated 2023-01-30, <https://learn.microsoft.com/ef/core/testing/choosing-a-testing-strategy>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S28] Microsoft, "Testing with FakeTimeProvider", Microsoft Learn, updated 2026-03-02, <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/extensions/timeprovider-testing>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S29] Testcontainers, "Testcontainers for .NET", <https://dotnet.testcontainers.org/>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S32] dotnet/aspnetcore, SignalR functional tests of the repository, <https://github.com/dotnet/aspnetcore>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.

# Run .NET tests with dotnet test

Contents: 1. Read the runner first · 2. Commands · 3. Exit codes · 4. Frameworks on the platform · Sources

## 1. Read the runner first (global.json)

`dotnet test` has two modes. The project chooses; you never change it. [S1] [S2]

| Point | VSTest mode | Microsoft.Testing.Platform (MTP) mode |
| --- | --- | --- |
| When | Default, every SDK | SDK .NET 10 and later, when `global.json` holds `"test": { "runner": "Microsoft.Testing.Platform" }` |
| Projects | VSTest projects; MTP projects through a legacy bridge | MTP projects only; a VSTest project is an error |
| Framework options | After `--` | Directly |
| TRX report | `--logger trx` | `--report-trx`, with `Microsoft.Testing.Extensions.TrxReport` |
| Coverage | `--collect:"XPlat Code Coverage"` | `--coverage` or `--coverlet`, through an extension of the project |

1. Read `global.json`. Look for `"runner"`.
2. Read the test project: the framework package (`xunit.v3`, `MSTest.Sdk`, `NUnit`, `TUnit`) and `OutputType`.
3. In MTP mode, the options depend on the extensions the test project references. An unknown option exits with code 5. [S2] [S3]

## 2. Commands (short output)

Run only the tests you wrote, then the whole test project. Keep the output in a file and read its end.

| Case | Command |
| --- | --- |
| MTP, one xUnit v3 class | `dotnet test --project <tests.csproj> --no-restore --no-progress --no-ansi --filter-class "<Namespace.Class>" > /tmp/test.log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"` |
| MTP, one MSTest or NUnit class | Same, with `--filter "FullyQualifiedName~<Class>"` |
| MTP 2.4 and later | Add `--output Minimal`: failures only |
| VSTest | `dotnet test <tests.csproj> --no-restore --filter "FullyQualifiedName~<Class>" -v q > /tmp/test.log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"` |
| Read the result | `tail -n 40 /tmp/test.log` |
| List the tests without running them | `--list-tests` |
| Stop after N failures (MTP) | `--maximum-failed-tests <N>` |

- xUnit v3 filters in MTP mode: `--filter-class`, `--filter-method`, `--filter-namespace`, `--filter-trait`. With SDK .NET 8 or 9, put them after `--`: `dotnet test -- --filter-class <Class>`. [S7]
- Since MTP 2.3, when the platform detects an AI tool, it drops the banner and shows the standard output of failed tests only. [S4]
- `--no-progress` is deprecated in MTP 2.3 for `--progress off`; both still work. [S3]

## 3. Exit codes (MTP mode)

| Code | Meaning | What to do |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | Every test passed | Run the whole test project once |
| 2 | At least one test failed | Read the failure, fix the code under test |
| 3 | Timeout or interruption | Run fewer tests |
| 5 | Unknown option | Remove the option; the project lacks its extension |
| 8 | No test ran | Check the filter |
| 9 | Fewer tests than `--minimum-expected-tests` | Check the filter |
| 13 | `--maximum-failed-tests` reached | Read the first failures |

[S2]

## 4. Frameworks on the platform (2026-11)

| Framework | State | Project |
| --- | --- | --- |
| xUnit v3 | 4.0 (2026-08-14): MTP v2 by default | Package `xunit.v3` (or `xunit.v3.mtp-v2`), `OutputType` `Exe`; template `dotnet new xunit3` after `dotnet new install xunit.v3.templates` |
| MSTest 4 | MTP by default with `MSTest.Sdk` | `<Project Sdk="MSTest.Sdk/<version>">`; `<UseVSTest>true</UseVSTest>` goes back to VSTest |
| NUnit 4 and 5 | 5.0 (2026-09-27) | `NUnit3TestAdapter` 5.0 or later with `EnableNUnitRunner` for MTP |
| TUnit | MTP only | One package; assertions are all `await Assert.That(...)` |

[S5] [S6] [S14]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Testing with 'dotnet test'", Microsoft Learn, updated 2026-09-12, <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/testing/unit-testing-with-dotnet-test>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "dotnet test with Microsoft.Testing.Platform (MTP)", Microsoft Learn, updated 2026-09-21, <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/tools/dotnet-test-mtp>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "Microsoft.Testing.Platform CLI options reference", Microsoft Learn, updated 2026-11-06, <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/testing/microsoft-testing-platform-cli-options>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "Microsoft.Testing.Platform terminal output", Microsoft Learn, updated 2026-10-28, <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/testing/microsoft-testing-platform-terminal-output>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] xUnit.net, "Core Framework v3 4.0.0", release notes, 2026-08-14, <https://xunit.net/releases/v3/4.0.0>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S6] xUnit.net, "Getting Started with xUnit.net v3", <https://xunit.net/docs/getting-started/v3/getting-started>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S7] xUnit.net, "Microsoft Testing Platform support in xUnit.net v3", <https://xunit.net/docs/getting-started/v3/microsoft-testing-platform>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.

# .NET tests, old to new

Contents: 1. The rule · 2. Frameworks · 3. Packages and tools · Sources

## 1. The rule (old to new in .NET tests)

- Keep the framework, the runner and the libraries the project already has. Use the "New" column only in a project at the "Since" version or later.
- Never add FluentAssertions 8 or later, or Verify 33 or later, without the user's agreement. Say their license first. In a new project, use the `Assert` of the framework.
- Never add an assertion or mocking library the project does not reference.

## 2. Frameworks (old to new)

| Old | New | Since | Status of the old | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Packages `xunit` 2.x, `xunit.runner.visualstudio` | `xunit.v3` and `OutputType` `Exe` | xUnit v3 | Keep them in a project still on v2 | [S8] |
| `using Xunit.Abstractions;` | `using Xunit;` | xUnit v3 | Does not compile | [S8] |
| `IAsyncLifetime` with `Task` | `ValueTask` | xUnit v3 | Does not compile | [S8] |
| `async void` test | `async Task` test | xUnit v3 | Fails at run time | [S8] |
| Async call without a token | `TestContext.Current.CancellationToken` | xUnit v3 | Warning xUnit1051 | [S8] |
| `[ExpectedException(typeof(T))]` | `Assert.ThrowsExactly<T>(() => ...)` | MSTest 4 | Removed | [S10] |
| `Assert.ThrowsException<T>(...)` | `Assert.ThrowsExactly<T>(...)` | MSTest 4 | Removed | [S10] |
| `[TestMethod("name")]` | `[TestMethod(DisplayName = "name")]` | MSTest 4 | Does not compile | [S10] |
| `[ClassCleanup(ClassCleanupBehavior.EndOfClass)]` | `[ClassCleanup]` | MSTest 4 | Removed | [S10] |
| NUnit `Assert.AreEqual(expected, actual)` | `Assert.That(actual, Is.EqualTo(expected))` | NUnit 4 | Moved to `ClassicAssert` in `NUnit.Framework.Legacy` | [S13] |
| `Assert.ThrowsAsync(...)` not awaited | `await Assert.ThrowsAsync(...)` | NUnit 5 | Returns a task | [S12] |
| `--logger trx --collect:"XPlat Code Coverage"` | `--report-trx`, `--coverage` | SDK .NET 10, MTP mode | Still right in VSTest mode | [S1] |

## 3. Packages and tools (old to new)

| Old | New | Since | Status of the old | Sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FluentAssertions, latest version | `Assert` of the framework; else AwesomeAssertions or Shouldly | FluentAssertions 8 | Paid for commercial use; 7.x stays free | [S18] [S19] [S20] |
| Verify, latest version | Ask first; stay below 33 | Verify 33 | Sponsorship checked at build time; fee for versions after 2026-09-01 | [S33] |
| Moq "became paid" | Moq 4.21, BSD-3-Clause | Moq 4.20.2 | The SponsorLink episode ended in 4.20.2 | [S21] |
| `UseInMemoryDatabase("...")` | SQLite in memory with an open connection, or the real database | EF Core 7 (docs of 2023) | Discouraged: fewer queries, no transactions | [S27] |
| `DateTime.UtcNow` or a real wait in a test | `TimeProvider` injected, `FakeTimeProvider.Advance(...)` | .NET 8 | Slow or flaky test | [S28] |
| `coverlet.collector` | `coverlet.MTP` or `Microsoft.Testing.Extensions.CodeCoverage` | SDK .NET 10, MTP mode | Does not work with MTP | [S34] [S35] |

- [S13] NUnit, "Breaking changes", <https://docs.nunit.org/articles/nunit/release-notes/breaking-changes.html>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S18] Fluent Assertions, README of the repository, <https://github.com/fluentassertions/fluentassertions>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.

# Write a C# unit test that checks something

Contents: 1. Rules · 2. Wrong and right · 3. Analyzers · 4. Mutation testing · Sources

## 1. Rules (C# unit tests)

Models write tests that pass but check little: too many mocks, prints instead of assertions, tests that copy the code, and tests changed to pass. [S74] [S76] [S77]

1. Take the expected value from the request or the specification, never from the code under test.
2. One behavior per test: one act, then the assertions on its result. Name the test after that behavior.
3. Assert on the result. Never print a value instead of asserting it.
4. Mock only the boundaries: network, clock, file system, database. Never mock the class under test, a value object or a `DbSet`.
5. Never assert the order or the count of calls on a mock when the result shows the behavior: such a test breaks on every refactoring. [S82]
6. Inject `TimeProvider`; never read `DateTime.Now` in the code under test. Use `FakeTimeProvider` in the test.
7. Never rely on the order of a `Dictionary`, a `HashSet` or a query without `OrderBy`.
8. A test fails: fix the code under test. Never change, skip or delete the test to make it pass. Stop after 3 failed attempts and show the failure.
9. For what to test in a simulation model, read `docs/simulation/model-testing.md`.

## 2. Wrong and right (C# tests)

Wrong: the expected value copies the code, the mock checks calls, nothing is asserted on the result.

```csharp
[Fact]
public void Test1()
{
    var repository = new Mock<IValveRepository>();
    var service = new ValveService(repository.Object);
    service.Close("V-101");
    repository.Verify(r => r.Get("V-101"), Times.Once());
    Console.WriteLine(service.Status("V-101"));
}
```

Right: one behavior, a fake at the boundary, the expected value from the request.

```csharp
[Fact]
public void Close_sets_an_open_valve_to_closed()
{
    var repository = new InMemoryValveRepository([new Valve("V-101", isOpen: true)]);
    var service = new ValveService(repository);

    service.Close("V-101");

    Assert.False(repository.Get("V-101").IsOpen);
}
```

Wrong: `async void` and an assertion not awaited.

```csharp
[Fact]
public async void Opens_the_valve() => Assert.ThrowsAsync<InvalidOperationException>(() => service.OpenAsync("X"));
```

Right:

```csharp
[Fact]
public async Task Open_refuses_an_unknown_valve() =>
    await Assert.ThrowsAsync<InvalidOperationException>(() => service.OpenAsync("X", TestContext.Current.CancellationToken));
```

## 3. Analyzers (C# test mistakes)

| Mistake | xUnit | MSTest |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Async assertion not awaited | xUnit2021, xUnit2014 | MSTEST0040 |
| `async void` test | xUnit1048, xUnit1049 | MSTEST0003 |
| Blocking call on a task | xUnit1031 | — |
| Cancellation token not passed | xUnit1051 | MSTEST0049 |
| Expected and actual swapped | xUnit2000 | MSTEST0017 |
| Weak or always-true assertion | xUnit2002, xUnit2003, xUnit2004, xUnit2013 | MSTEST0032, MSTEST0025, MSTEST0037 |

- The xUnit analyzers come with `xunit.v3`; the MSTest analyzers with `MSTest.TestFramework`, set by `MSTestAnalysisMode` (`Recommended` turns information into warnings). [S36] [S37]
- No analyzer flags a test without any assertion: review it.

## 4. Mutation testing (a check run by a person)

- Stryker.NET changes the code and checks that a test fails: `dotnet tool install -g dotnet-stryker`, then `dotnet stryker` from the test project folder; `--since` limits it to changed code.
- It is slow, and its MTP runner is in preview: never run it in the write, run, fix loop. Propose it to the user for one module.

## Sources

- [S36] xUnit.net, "xUnit.net Analyzer Rules", <https://xunit.net/xunit.analyzers/rules/>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S37] Microsoft, "MSTest analyzers", Microsoft Learn, updated 2026-11-05, <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/testing/mstest-analyzers/overview>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S74] A. Hora, R. Robbes, "Are Coding Agents Generating Over-Mocked Tests? An Empirical Study", MSR 2026, arXiv:2602.00409, <https://arxiv.org/html/2602.00409v1>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S76] Z. Chen and others, "Rethinking the Value of Agent-Generated Tests for LLM-Based Software Engineering Agents", arXiv:2602.07900 (preprint), <https://arxiv.org/html/2602.07900v2>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S77] Z. Zhong, A. Raghunathan, N. Carlini, "ImpossibleBench: Measuring LLMs' Propensity of Exploiting Test Cases", arXiv:2510.20270 (preprint), <https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.20270>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.
- [S82] A. Eagle, "Testing on the Toilet: Change-Detector Tests Considered Harmful", Google Testing Blog, 2015-01-27, <https://testing.googleblog.com/2015/01/testing-on-toilet-change-detector-tests.html>, read 2026-11-13, summarized.

# ASP.NET Core 6 to 11, what changed

Contents: 1. Old and new patterns · 2. Example · 3. Authentication and SignalR · 4. Obsolete in .NET 10 · Sources

## 1. Old and new patterns (ASP.NET Core 6 to 11)

Use a new pattern only when the target framework is equal to or higher than the "Since" column. `Startup.cs` still works: keep it in a project that has it.

| Old | New | Since |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Startup.cs` and `Program.CreateHostBuilder` | One `Program.cs`: `WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args)`, `builder.Services…`, `app.Map…`, `app.Run()` | 6 |
| `app.UseRouting()` then `app.UseEndpoints(…)` | `app.MapControllers()`, `app.MapGet(…)` directly | 6 |
| `Results.Ok(x)` | `TypedResults.Ok(x)` and `Results<Ok<T>, NotFound>` | 7 |
| Route prefix repeated on each endpoint | `app.MapGroup("/orders")` | 7 |
| Exception middleware written by hand | `IExceptionHandler`, `AddExceptionHandler<T>()`, Problem Details | 8 |
| `AddSwaggerGen()`, `UseSwagger()` | `AddOpenApi()`, `MapOpenApi()`; a visual UI comes from a separate package | 9 |
| `.WithOpenApi()` | Operation transformers | 10 |
| Validation by hand in a minimal API | `builder.Services.AddValidation()` | 10 |
| Event stream written by hand | `TypedResults.ServerSentEvents` | 10 |
| Redirect to a login page for an API call | 401 or 403 for known API endpoints | 10 |

[S1] [S2] [S3] [S4]

## 2. Example of a minimal API (ASP.NET Core 10)

Wrong, in a new `net10.0` project:

```csharp
builder.Services.AddSwaggerGen();
app.MapGet("/valves/{id}", (int id, ValveStore store) =>
{
    var valve = store.Find(id);
    return valve is null ? Results.NotFound() : Results.Ok(valve);
});
```

Right:

```csharp
builder.Services.AddOpenApi();
builder.Services.AddValidation();
app.MapOpenApi();
app.MapGet("/valves/{id}", Results<Ok<Valve>, NotFound> (int id, ValveStore store) =>
    store.Find(id) is { } valve ? TypedResults.Ok(valve) : TypedResults.NotFound());
```

## 3. Authentication and SignalR (ASP.NET Core 7 to 11)

- Authentication: a single registered scheme is the default (7); Identity API endpoints with `MapIdentityApi<TUser>()` (8); passkeys in Identity (10). [S2] [S3]
- SignalR: stateful reconnect with `WithStatefulReconnect` (8); polymorphic types in hubs, native AOT with the JSON protocol only (9). [S2] [S3]

## 4. Obsolete in .NET 10 (ASP.NET Core 10)

`WebHostBuilder`, `IWebHost` and `WebHost`; `IActionContextAccessor`; Razor runtime compilation; `IPNetwork` and `ForwardedHeadersOptions.KnownNetworks`. The build shows them as warnings, so `dotnet build` fails on them in this base. [S5]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Migrate from ASP.NET Core 5.0 to 6.0", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/migration/50-to-60>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "What's new in ASP.NET Core 7.0" and "8.0", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/release-notes/aspnetcore-7.0>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "What's new in ASP.NET Core 9.0", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/release-notes/aspnetcore-9.0>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "What's new in ASP.NET Core in .NET 10", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/release-notes/aspnetcore-10.0>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Microsoft, "Breaking changes in ASP.NET Core 10", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/aspnet/core/breaking-changes/10/overview>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

summary: Find the project's C# version from its target framework, then pick old or new syntax. C# 10 to 15.
tool: C#
version: "15"

# C# 10 to 15, what changed

Contents: 1. Find the C# version · 2. Rules · 3. Syntax C# 10 to 12 · 4. Syntax C# 13 to 15 · 5. Examples · 6. Pitfalls · Old patterns · Sources

## 1. Find the C# version of the project (.NET TargetFramework)

Read `TargetFramework` in the `*.csproj` file, or in `Directory.Build.props`. The target framework sets the C# version. [S1]

| `TargetFramework` | C# version |
| --- | --- |
| `net6.0` | 10 |
| `net7.0` | 11 |
| `net8.0` | 12 |
| `net9.0` | 13 |
| `net10.0` | 14 |
| `net11.0` | 15 |
| `netstandard2.1` | 8 |
| `netstandard2.0`, .NET Framework | 7.3 |

- If the project file sets `LangVersion`, use that version. Never add `LangVersion`, never raise it: a C# version newer than the default of the target framework is not supported. [S1]
- The SDK version in `global.json` does not change the C# version. A `net8.0` project built with a newer SDK still uses C# 12. [S1]
- If `TargetFrameworks` lists several targets, write code for the lowest one. [S1]

## 2. Rules for choosing C# syntax (C# 10 to 15)

1. Use a syntax of sections 3 and 4 only when its "Since" value is lower than or equal to the C# version of the project.
2. In a new file, use the newest syntax the project allows.
3. In a file that exists, keep its style. Change old syntax only when the task asks for it.
4. Never use a preview feature.
5. The compiler is the check: `dotnet build` must end with zero errors.

## 3. Old and new syntax, C# 10 to 12 (.NET 6 to 8)

| Since | Old | New | Feature |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 10 | `namespace App { ... }` | `namespace App;` | File-scoped namespace |
| 10 | `using System.Linq;` in every file | `global using System.Linq;` in one file | Global using directive |
| 10 | `struct` with hand-written equality | `public readonly record struct Point(int X, int Y);` | Record struct |
| 11 | String with escaped quotes | `"""` raw text `"""` | Raw string literal |
| 11 | Constructor that forces every value | `public required string Name { get; init; }` | Required member |
| 11 | `a.Length > 0 && a[0] == 1` | `a is [1, ..]` | List pattern |
| 12 | Private field and constructor that copies the parameter | `public class OrderService(IOrderRepository repository)` | Primary constructor |
| 12 | `new List<int> { 1, 2 }`, `Array.Empty<int>()` | `List<int> a = [1, 2];`, `int[] b = [];` | Collection expression |
| 12 | `a.Concat(b).ToArray()` | `int[] all = [.. a, .. b];` | Spread element |

[S2] [S3]

## 4. Old and new syntax, C# 13 to 15 (.NET 9 to 11)

| Since | Old | New | Feature |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 13 | `params string[] parts` | `params ReadOnlySpan<string> parts` | `params` collection |
| 13 | `private readonly object gate = new();` | `private readonly Lock gate = new();` | `System.Threading.Lock` |
| 13 | Partial methods only | `public partial string Name { get; set; }` | Partial property |
| 14 | Private field and property that validates | `set => field = Math.Clamp(value, 0, 100);` | `field` keyword |
| 14 | `if (c != null) c.Order = o;` | `c?.Order = o;` | Null-conditional assignment |
| 14 | Static method `IsBlank(this string text)` | `extension(string text) { ... }` | Extension members |
| 15 | Base class and `switch` with a default arm | `public union Pet(Cat, Dog, Bird);` | Union type |
| 15 | `new List<string>(6)` then `AddRange(values)` | `List<string> names = [with(capacity: 6), .. values];` | Collection expression arguments |
| 15 | Flag or `goto` to leave two loops | `outer: for (...) { ... break outer; }` | Labeled `break` and `continue` |

[S2] [S4] [S5] [S6]

## 5. Examples of new C# syntax (C# 12 and 14)

Primary constructor, C# 12. The parameter is in scope in the whole class body. [S3]

Wrong, in a new file of a C# 12 project:

```csharp
public class OrderService
{
    private readonly IOrderRepository repository;

    public OrderService(IOrderRepository repository)
    {
        this.repository = repository;
    }
}
```

Right:

```csharp
public class OrderService(IOrderRepository repository)
{
    public Task<Order?> FindAsync(int id) => repository.FindAsync(id);
}
```

`field` keyword, C# 14: the compiler creates the backing field. [S5]

```csharp
public int Volume
{
    get;
    set => field = Math.Clamp(value, 0, 100);
}
```

## 6. Pitfalls (C# 14 and 15 keywords)

- Error CS9272 or warning CS9258: in a C# 14 property accessor, `field` means the backing field. A local named `field` conflicts. Rename it, or write `@field`. [S5] [S7]
- A type named `extension` does not compile in C# 14. Rename it, or write `@extension`. [S7]
- `x.Reverse()` on an array, in a C# 14 project whose target is older than `net10.0`: it reverses in place and returns `void`. Write `Enumerable.Reverse(x)`. [S7]
- A collection expression needs a target type: an array, a span, or a type such as `List<T>`. [S3]
- Interceptors (C# 12) and the memory safety syntax of C# 15 are previews. Do not use them. [S3] [S6]

## Old patterns

- Block-scoped namespace (before C# 10). Do not use it in a new file.
- Constructor that only copies its parameters into fields (before C# 12); `new List<T> { ... }` (before C# 12). Do not use them in a new file of a C# 12 project or later.
- Lock on a private `object` (before C# 13). Do not use it in a new file of a C# 13 project or later.

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "C# language versioning", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/language-versioning>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, adapted.

# .NET 6 to 11, what changed in the libraries and the SDK

Contents: 1. Support · 2. Built-in APIs that replace packages · 3. Breaking changes to know · 4. File-based apps · 5. Native AOT · Sources

## 1. Support (.NET versions)

.NET 11 (STS) came out on 2026-11-10. Only .NET 10 (LTS) and .NET 11 are supported, both until 2028-11. .NET 6 to 9 are out of support. A new major version comes out each November. [S1]

## 2. Built-in APIs that replace packages (.NET libraries)

Use the built-in API when the target framework is equal to or higher than the "Since" column. Add a third-party package only when the user asks for it.

| Need | Old habit | Built-in | Since |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| JSON | `Newtonsoft.Json` | `System.Text.Json` (case sensitive by default) | .NET Core 3.1; snake_case in 8 |
| Testable clock | `DateTime.UtcNow`, own clock interface | `TimeProvider`, `ITimer` | 8 |
| Fast logging | Interpolated strings in `LogInformation` | `[LoggerMessage]` on a `partial` method | — |
| HTTP resilience | Polly policies written by hand | `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience`: `AddStandardResilienceHandler()` | package |
| OpenAPI document | Swashbuckle `AddSwaggerGen` | `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi`: `AddOpenApi()`, `MapOpenApi()` | 9 |
| Two-level cache | `IMemoryCache` and `IDistributedCache` by hand | `HybridCache`: `AddHybridCache()`, `GetOrCreateAsync` | 9 |
| Named services | Factories, third-party container | Keyed services: `AddKeyedSingleton`, `[FromKeyedServices]` | 8 |
| Read-only lookups | Static `Dictionary` | `FrozenDictionary`, `FrozenSet`, `SearchValues` | 8 |
| Counting by key | `GroupBy` then `Count` | `CountBy`, `AggregateBy`, `Index` | 9 |
| Time-ordered ids | Own generator | `Guid.CreateVersion7()` | 9 |
| Async LINQ | Package `System.Linq.Async` | `System.Linq.AsyncEnumerable` | 10 |

[S2] [S3] [S4] [S6]

## 3. Breaking changes to know (.NET 8 to 11)

| .NET | Change |
| --- | --- |
| 8 | Container images listen on port 8080 and run as the non-root user `app`; `dotnet publish` builds in Release; `BinaryFormatter` disabled |
| 9 | `BinaryFormatter` always throws; span-based `params` overloads are preferred; Terminal Logger by default |
| 10 | Container images based on Ubuntu; `dotnet new sln` creates a `.slnx` file; `dotnet restore` audits transitive packages; `PackageReference` without a version is an error; `System.Linq.AsyncEnumerable` may conflict with the `System.Linq.Async` package |
| 11 | `IHost.RunAsync` throws when a `BackgroundService` fails; restore no longer looks for a higher version in the global folder |

[S5] [S7]

## 4. File-based apps (.NET SDK 10)

Run a single C# file without a project: `dotnet run app.cs`. Directives at the top of the file add packages and settings: `#:package Serilog@3.1.1`, `#:sdk Microsoft.NET.Sdk.Web`, `#:property TargetFramework=net10.0`. `dotnet project convert app.cs` turns it into a project. [S8]

The `Directory.Build.props`, `Directory.Packages.props` and `global.json` files of the parent folders apply to it.

## 5. Native AOT (.NET publishing)

Set `<PublishAot>true</PublishAot>`, then run `dotnet publish -r linux-x64 -c Release`. Console apps from .NET 7, ASP.NET Core from .NET 8. No dynamic loading of assemblies, no `System.Reflection.Emit`; trimming is required. [S6]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, ".NET support policy", <https://dotnet.microsoft.com/platform/support/policy/dotnet-core>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "What's new in the .NET 8 runtime", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/whats-new/dotnet-8/runtime>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "What's new in .NET libraries for .NET 9", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/whats-new/dotnet-9/libraries>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "Build resilient HTTP apps", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/resilience/http-resilience>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Microsoft, "Breaking changes in .NET 10", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/compatibility/10>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized; .NET 8 and 9 from the same series of pages.
- [S6] Microsoft, "Native AOT deployment", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/deploying/native-aot/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S7] Microsoft, "Breaking changes in .NET 11", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/compatibility/11>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized; the page says it is not complete yet.
- [S8] Microsoft, "File-based apps", .NET 10, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/core/sdk/file-based-apps>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# EF Core 7 to 11, what changed

Contents: 1. Find the version · 2. New features · 3. Breaking changes and their fix · Sources

## 1. Find the EF Core version (EF Core package version)

Read the version of the package `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore` in `Directory.Packages.props` or in the `PackageReference` of the project. It is not the target framework: EF Core 9 runs on .NET 8. [S1]

## 2. New features by version (EF Core 7 to 11)

| EF Core | Write |
| --- | --- |
| 7 | `ExecuteUpdateAsync`, `ExecuteDeleteAsync` for bulk changes; JSON columns with `ToJson()`; TPC inheritance with `UseTpcMappingStrategy()` |
| 8 | Collections of primitive types; complex types; `DateOnly` and `TimeOnly` |
| 9 | `UseSeeding`, `UseAsyncSeeding` for seed data; a lock against concurrent migrations |
| 10 | `LeftJoin`, `RightJoin`; named query filters; optional complex types and complex types in JSON (prefer them to owned entities) |
| 11 | `FullJoin`; `MaxBy`, `MinBy`; `dotnet ef database update --add` |

[S2] [S3] [S4] [S5]

## 3. Breaking changes and their fix (EF Core 7 to 11)

| EF Core | Breaking change | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 7 | SQL Server connections use `Encrypt=true` by default | A valid server certificate; else `TrustServerCertificate=True`, at a cost in security |
| 8 | `Contains` on a list is translated with `OPENJSON` | SQL Server compatibility level 130 or more, else `UseCompatibilityLevel(120)` |
| 8 | Enums in JSON are stored as numbers | `HasConversion<string>()` |
| 9 | An exception when the model has changes without a migration | Add a migration; in `HasData`, static values instead of `DateTime.Now` or `Guid.NewGuid()` |
| 9 | An exception when a migration runs inside an explicit transaction | Call `MigrateAsync` alone |
| 10 | Parameterized collections become several parameters | `UseParameterizedCollectionMode(ParameterTranslationMode.Constant)` where needed |
| 10 | SQLite dates without an offset are read as UTC | Adapt the code to UTC |
| 11 | SQL Server compatibility level 160 by default | `UseCompatibilityLevel(<level>)` for an older server |

[S2] [S3] [S4] [S5]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "EF Core releases and planning", <https://learn.microsoft.com/ef/core/what-is-new>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "Breaking changes in EF Core 7.0" and "8", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/what-is-new/ef-core-7.0/breaking-changes> and <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/what-is-new/ef-core-8.0/breaking-changes>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "Breaking changes in EF Core 9", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/what-is-new/ef-core-9.0/breaking-changes>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Microsoft, "What's new in EF Core 10" and its breaking changes, <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/what-is-new/ef-core-10.0/whatsnew>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Microsoft, "What's new in EF Core 11", <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/ef/core/what-is-new/ef-core-11.0/whatsnew>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized; the page says EF Core 11 is in development.

# .NET packages that became paid

Contents: 1. The rule · 2. The packages · 3. Example · Sources

## 1. The rule (.NET package licenses)

- Never add AutoMapper, MediatR, MassTransit, FluentAssertions, Verify or Polly to a project without the user's agreement. Say their license first.
- In a project that already has one of them, never raise it to the version of the table below or later.
- Read the versions in `Directory.Packages.props` or in the `PackageReference` of the project.
- Licenses change often: when in doubt, say that the user must check the license of the version. This is not legal advice.

## 2. The packages (licenses checked 2026-11)

| Package | What changed | Last free version | Free replacements |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| AutoMapper | Version 15.0 (2025-07-02): RPL 1.5 or commercial license; free community edition under 5 million dollars of yearly revenue, with a license key | 14.x | Mapperly, Mapster, mapping written by hand |
| MediatR | Version 13.0 (2025-07-02): same terms as AutoMapper | 12.x | Mediator, Brighter, a handler interface written by hand |
| MassTransit | Version 9: commercial product, license required | 8.x | Rebus, Brighter, Wolverine |
| FluentAssertions | Version 8 (2025-01): proprietary license, paid for commercial use | 7.x (Apache 2.0) | AwesomeAssertions, Shouldly, plain `Assert` |
| Verify | Code still MIT; a maintenance fee for the versions released after 2026-09-01, for organizations with revenue; version 33 checks the sponsorship at build time | versions below 33 | `Assert` of the test framework, expected values written in the test [S6] |
| Polly | Open Source Maintenance Fee for companies that earn from a product using it, from 2026-11-16; code still BSD-3-Clause | versions before that date | `Microsoft.Extensions.Http.Resilience`, which uses Polly underneath |
| Swashbuckle | No license change: removed from the project templates of .NET 9 | — | `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi` |

[S1] [S2] [S3] [S4] [S5]

## 3. Example (mapping without a paid package)

Wrong, in a new project:

```csharp
builder.Services.AddAutoMapper(typeof(Program));
var dto = mapper.Map<ValveDto>(valve);
```

Right:

```csharp
public static class ValveMapping
{
    public static ValveDto ToDto(this Valve valve) => new(valve.Tag, valve.IsOpen);
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] J. Bogard, "AutoMapper and MediatR Commercial Editions Launch Today", 2025-07-02, <https://www.jimmybogard.com/automapper-and-mediatr-commercial-editions-launch-today/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] NuGet Gallery, "MassTransit 9.1.1", <https://www.nuget.org/packages/MassTransit/9.1.1>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] InfoQ, article on the license of Fluent Assertions 8, 2025-01-30, <https://www.infoq.com/news/2025/01/fluent-assertions-v8-license>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] App-vNext, "Polly", README of the repository, <https://github.com/App-vNext/Polly>, read 2026-11-12, BSD-3-Clause, summarized.
- [S5] ASP.NET Core team, "Announcement: Swashbuckle.AspNetCore is being removed in .NET 9", issue 54599, <https://github.com/dotnet/aspnetcore/issues/54599>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S6] VerifyTests, "Verify", README of the repository, <https://github.com/VerifyTests/Verify>, read 2026-11-13, MIT with a maintenance fee, summarized.

# Kubernetes API versions

Contents: 1. Versions to write · 2. Removed versions · 3. Converting · 4. Kubernetes releases · Sources

## 1. apiVersion to write (Kubernetes 1.37)

| Kind | apiVersion |
| --- | --- |
| Deployment, DaemonSet, ReplicaSet, StatefulSet | `apps/v1` |
| Service, ConfigMap, Secret, Pod, PersistentVolumeClaim | `v1` |
| Job, CronJob | `batch/v1` |
| Ingress | `networking.k8s.io/v1` |
| HTTPRoute, Gateway, GatewayClass | `gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1` |
| HorizontalPodAutoscaler | `autoscaling/v2` |
| PodDisruptionBudget | `policy/v1` |
| CustomResourceDefinition | `apiextensions.k8s.io/v1` |
| Role, RoleBinding, ClusterRole | `rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1` |

The cluster tells the versions it serves: `kubectl api-resources`. [S1]

## 2. Removed versions (Kubernetes deprecation guide)

| Removed in | Old version | Write instead |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1.16 | Deployment, DaemonSet, ReplicaSet in `extensions/v1beta1`, `apps/v1beta1`, `apps/v1beta2` | `apps/v1` |
| 1.22 | Ingress in `extensions/v1beta1` and `networking.k8s.io/v1beta1` | `networking.k8s.io/v1` |
| 1.22 | CustomResourceDefinition and RBAC in `v1beta1` | `v1` |
| 1.25 | CronJob in `batch/v1beta1` | `batch/v1` |
| 1.25 | PodDisruptionBudget in `policy/v1beta1` | `policy/v1` |
| 1.25 | PodSecurityPolicy | Pod Security Admission |
| 1.25, 1.26 | HorizontalPodAutoscaler in `autoscaling/v2beta1`, `v2beta2` | `autoscaling/v2` |
| 1.32 | FlowSchema in `flowcontrol.apiserver.k8s.io/v1beta3` | `v1` |

Source: [S1]. A cluster refuses an object whose `apiVersion` was removed.

Wrong (Ingress before 1.22):

```yaml
apiVersion: extensions/v1beta1
kind: Ingress
spec:
  rules:
    - http:
        paths:
          - path: /
            backend:
              serviceName: api
              servicePort: 80
```

Right:

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: Ingress
metadata:
  name: api
spec:
  rules:
    - http:
        paths:
          - path: /
            pathType: Prefix
            backend:
              service:
                name: api
                port:
                  number: 80
```

## 3. Converting a file (kubectl convert)

`kubectl convert -f <file> --output-version <group>/<version>` converts a file. It is a plugin to install, and its result must be read again. [S1]

Without the plugin, rewrite the fields by hand with the right example above, then validate with kubeconform (`docs/kubernetes/validate-offline.md`).

## 4. Kubernetes releases

- Current minor version on 2026-11-12: 1.37. Maintained branches: 1.37, 1.36, 1.35, about one year of patches each. [S2]
- The cluster version is not in the repository. Read it in the `AGENTS.md` of the project, or ask the user.

## Sources

- [S1] Kubernetes, "Deprecated API Migration Guide", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/using-api/deprecation-guide/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Kubernetes, "Releases", <https://kubernetes.io/releases/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

id: kubernetes-debug-pods
title: Debug a Pod that does not run

summary: Read the state and events of a Pod, then match the symptom with its cause and its fix. Kubernetes 1.37.
tool: kubernetes

# Debug a Pod that does not run

Contents: 1. Read first · 2. Symptoms · Sources

## 1. Read the Pod first (kubectl describe)

1. Ask for the output of `kubectl describe pod <name>`, or run it if you have access. These commands only read.
2. Read the state, the last termination reason and exit code, and the events at the end. [S1] [S2]
3. Match them with the table below. Give the cause, then the smallest fix.
4. Never change the cluster yourself: give the user the command or the manifest change.

## 2. Symptoms, causes and fixes (Kubernetes Pods)

| Symptom | Likely cause | Command or fix |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Pending`, event `FailedScheduling` | Requests larger than the free room | Lower the requests [S1] [S2] |
| Waiting, image not pulled | Wrong image name, image not pushed, pull Secret not found (`FailedToRetrieveImagePullSecret`) | Check the name and the namespace of the Secret [S2] [S3] |
| `OOMKilled`, exit code 137, restarts | Memory limit passed | Find a leak, or raise the limit [S1] |
| Slow, never stopped | CPU limit reached: throttled, not killed | Raise the CPU limit [S1] |
| Service without endpoints | Wrong selector or port | `kubectl get endpointslices -l kubernetes.io/service-name=<service>` [S2] |
| A field ignored in silence | Misspelled key | `kubectl apply --validate --dry-run=server -f <file>`; kubeconform `-strict` [S2] |
| Rollout does not move | New Pods never ready | Condition `Progressing` `False` after 600 s; `kubectl describe deployment <name>` [S4] |
| Object refused: unknown version | `apiVersion` removed | `docs/kubernetes/api-versions.md` |

## Sources

- [S1] Kubernetes, "Resource Management for Pods and Containers", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Kubernetes, "Debug Pods", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/debug/debug-application/debug-pods/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Kubernetes, "Pull an Image from a Private Registry", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/configure-pod-container/pull-image-private-registry>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Kubernetes, "Update a Deployment Without Downtime", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/run-application/update-deployment-rolling/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

variant: files
---

# ASP.NET Core health endpoints and probes

Contents: 1. Probes · 2. Endpoints · 3. Probe settings · Sources

## 1. Three probes (Kubernetes startup, liveness and readiness)

- Until the startup probe succeeds, the two others do not run. [S2]
- A failed readiness probe removes the Pod from the endpoints of the Service, at any time of its life. [S2]
- A liveness probe that is too strict restarts Pods under load, then overloads the Pods left. [S2]
- An `exec` probe creates processes at each pass: prefer `httpGet`. [S2]

## 2. Health endpoints (ASP.NET Core health checks)

- Register checks with `AddHealthChecks` and map endpoints with `MapHealthChecks`. [S1]
- Answer 200 when healthy or degraded, 503 otherwise. The checks run at each call. [S1]
- Keep `/healthz/live` without checks: it tells that the process answers. Filter the checks of `/healthz/ready` by tag. [S1]

```csharp
builder.Services.AddHealthChecks()
    .AddCheck("database", () => HealthCheckResult.Healthy(), tags: ["ready"]);

WebApplication app = builder.Build();
app.MapHealthChecks("/healthz/live", new HealthCheckOptions { Predicate = _ => false });
app.MapHealthChecks("/healthz/ready", new HealthCheckOptions { Predicate = check => check.Tags.Contains("ready") });
```

## 3. Probe settings (Deployment)

```yaml
startupProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /healthz/live
    port: 8080
  periodSeconds: 5
  failureThreshold: 30
livenessProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /healthz/live
    port: 8080
  periodSeconds: 10
  failureThreshold: 3
readinessProbe:
  httpGet:
    path: /healthz/ready
    port: 8080
  periodSeconds: 5
  failureThreshold: 3
```

- The startup probe above gives 150 seconds to start (30 tries of 5 seconds). Set it from the real start time.
- Never put a database check in the liveness probe: a slow database would restart every Pod.

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "Health checks in ASP.NET Core", <https://learn.microsoft.com/aspnet/core/host-and-deploy/health-checks>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, adapted.
- [S2] Kubernetes, "Liveness, Readiness, and Startup Probes", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/probes/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Helm charts and Kustomize overlays

Contents: 1. Which one · 2. Helm · 3. Kustomize · Sources

## 1. Chart or overlay (Helm or Kustomize)

- Kustomize changes plain YAML files without a text template: a base, overlays that depend on it, and patches (strategic merge or JSON). [S2]
- Helm packages templates and values into a chart. [S1]
- Use what the project already uses. Read it in the `AGENTS.md` of the project, or look for `Chart.yaml` and `kustomization.yaml`. Never mix both for the same objects.

## 2. Helm chart commands

| Need | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Check the chart | `helm lint --strict <chart folder>` |
| Check for a Kubernetes version | `helm lint --strict --kube-version <version> <chart folder>` |
| Render the objects | `helm template <chart folder>` |
| Validate the rendered objects | `helm template <chart folder> \| kubeconform -strict -summary` |

- `helm lint` prints `[ERROR]` for what will fail the install and `[WARNING]` for a gap with the conventions; `--strict` makes a warning fail. [S1]
- Never run `helm install` or `helm upgrade` yourself: they change the cluster.
- Check `helm template --help` before you use an option of it.

## 3. Kustomize overlay commands

```text
deploy/
  base/
    kustomization.yaml
    deployment.yaml
    service.yaml
  overlays/
    test/kustomization.yaml
    production/kustomization.yaml
```

```yaml
# deploy/overlays/production/kustomization.yaml
apiVersion: kustomize.config.k8s.io/v1beta1
kind: Kustomization
resources:
  - ../../base
images:
  - name: registry.example.internal/api
    newTag: 1.4.0
```

- Render: `kustomize build deploy/overlays/production`. [S2]
- Validate: `kustomize build deploy/overlays/production | kubeconform -strict -summary`.

## Sources

- [S1] Helm, "helm lint", v4.3.1, <https://helm.sh/docs/helm/helm_lint>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S2] Kustomize, "Glossary", <https://kubectl.docs.kubernetes.io/references/kustomize/glossary>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Ingress and Gateway API

Contents: 1. Which one · 2. Ingress v1 · 3. Gateway API · Sources

## 1. Which one to write (Ingress or Gateway API)

- Ingress NGINX, the controller, gets no release, no fix and no security update after March 2026. Existing installs keep running. [S2]
- Gateway API is the successor of Ingress. It has no Ingress kind: an Ingress is converted once. Its definitions and a controller are installed in the cluster in addition. [S3]
- The right choice depends on what the cluster has installed. Read it in the `AGENTS.md` of the project. If it is not there, ask the user before you write the file.

## 2. Ingress v1 (networking.k8s.io/v1)

- `pathType` is required: `Prefix`, `Exact` or `ImplementationSpecific`. [S1]
- The backend is `service.name` and `service.port.number`. The old `serviceName` and `servicePort` were removed in 1.22. [S1]

```yaml
apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: Ingress
metadata:
  name: api
spec:
  rules:
    - host: api.example.internal
      http:
        paths:
          - path: /
            pathType: Prefix
            backend:
              service:
                name: api
                port:
                  number: 80
```

## 3. Gateway API HTTPRoute (gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1)

The kinds: GatewayClass, Gateway (`gatewayClassName`, `listeners`), HTTPRoute (`parentRefs`, `hostnames`, `rules`, `backendRefs`). [S3]

```yaml
apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
kind: HTTPRoute
metadata:
  name: api
spec:
  parentRefs:
    - name: main-gateway
  hostnames:
    - "api.example.internal"
  rules:
    - matches:
        - path:
            type: PathPrefix
            value: /
      backendRefs:
        - name: api
          port: 80
```

- `parentRefs` names a Gateway that the cluster team created. Ask for its name.
- An HTTPRoute does nothing in a cluster without the Gateway API definitions and a controller. [S3]
- kubeconform needs the schemas of these custom kinds in its local schema folder (`docs/kubernetes/validate-offline.md`).

## Sources

- [S1] Kubernetes, "Deprecated API Migration Guide", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/using-api/deprecation-guide/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Kubernetes, "Ingress NGINX Retirement: What You Need to Know", 2025-11-11, <https://www.kubernetes.io/blog/2025/11/11/ingress-nginx-retirement/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Kubernetes, "Gateway API", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/services-networking/gateway/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, adapted.

# Private registry and pull secret

Contents: 1. The Secret · 2. Using it · 3. Images without a registry · Sources

## 1. The pull Secret (kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson)

- The Secret has the type `kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson`. [S1]
- It is created by `kubectl create secret docker-registry <name> --docker-server=<registry> --docker-username=<user> --docker-password=<password>`, or from an existing configuration file of the engine. [S1]
- A password typed on the command line stays in the shell history. Prefer the file form, and never write a password in a manifest or in the repository.
- Never run these commands yourself: they change the cluster. Give them to the user.

## 2. Using the Secret in a Pod (imagePullSecrets)

```yaml
spec:
  template:
    spec:
      imagePullSecrets:
        - name: registry-pull
      containers:
        - name: api
          image: registry.example.internal/api:1.4.0
```

- The Secret must be in the same namespace as the Pod. Otherwise the Pod waits and its events show `FailedToRetrieveImagePullSecret`. [S1]
- Write the full image name with the registry, and a version tag.

## 3. Images without a registry (archive)

- The .NET SDK can write an image archive without a daemon (`docs/containers/dotnet-images.md`); load it with `docker load -i` or `podman load -i`. [S2]
- Put configuration values in a ConfigMap or in environment variables of the Deployment, never in the image. Keep one file per environment or one Kustomize overlay (`docs/kubernetes/helm-kustomize.md`).

## Sources

- [S1] Kubernetes, "Pull an Image from a Private Registry", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/configure-pod-container/pull-image-private-registry>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Microsoft, "Containerize a .NET app with dotnet publish", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/containers/sdk-publish>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Resources, exclusive cores and .NET memory

Contents: 1. Requests and limits · 2. When latency matters · 3. .NET memory · Sources

## 1. Requests and limits (Kubernetes resources)

| Point | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| CPU limit | A hard limit applied by the kernel: the container is throttled, never killed. [S1] |
| Memory limit | Over the limit, the container is killed (`OOMKilled`, code 137) and restarted if it can be. [S1] |
| Limit without request | The request is set to the limit. [S1] |
| Units | CPU in cores or millicores (`500m`); memory in `Mi` or `Gi`. `400m` of memory is 0.4 byte. [S1] |

## 2. When latency matters (exclusive CPU cores)

- The CPU manager policy is `none` by default. The `static` policy is set on the kubelet of each node (`cpuManagerPolicy: static`), with a CPU reserve that is not zero. [S2]
- Only the containers of a "Guaranteed" Pod with a whole number of CPUs get exclusive cores. A fractional request stays in the shared pool. [S2]
- Changing the policy means draining the node and restarting the kubelet: it is the work of the cluster administrator. [S2]

For a latency-sensitive process, write requests equal to limits, CPU as a whole number, memory with a margin above the .NET heap:

```yaml
resources:
  requests:
    cpu: "2"
    memory: "1Gi"
  limits:
    cpu: "2"
    memory: "1Gi"
```

Then ask the cluster administrator whether the `static` policy is active. Without it, the manifest alone reserves no core. Keep a tolerant liveness probe and a separate startup probe (`docs/kubernetes/health-endpoints.md`).

## 3. The .NET garbage collector in a container

- The hard heap limit is 75 % of the container memory limit by default, 20 MB at least. [S3]
- Settings: `DOTNET_gcServer`, `DOTNET_GCHeapCount`, `DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit`, `DOTNET_GCConserveMemory`. [S3]
- From .NET 9, dynamic adaptation to the size of the application is on by default. [S3]

```yaml
env:
  - name: DOTNET_GCHeapHardLimit
    value: "0x30000000"
```

Set a heap limit only with a measure of the real use; the default of 75 % fits most services.

## Sources

- [S1] Kubernetes, "Resource Management for Pods and Containers", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Kubernetes, "Control CPU Management Policies on the Node", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/administer-cluster/cpu-management-policies>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Microsoft, "Runtime configuration options for garbage collection", <https://learn.microsoft.com/dotnet/core/runtime-config/garbage-collector>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Rolling update and rollback

Contents: 1. Strategy · 2. Commands · 3. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Rollout strategy (Deployment strategy)

- `RollingUpdate` is the default. `maxUnavailable` and `maxSurge` take a number or a percentage, 25 % each by default. [S1]
- `Recreate` stops every Pod before it creates the new ones: there is a downtime. [S1]
- A Pod whose readiness probe fails gets no traffic: a rolling update waits for the new Pods to be ready. [S2]

For a service with few replicas, keep every old Pod until a new one is ready:

```yaml
spec:
  strategy:
    type: RollingUpdate
    rollingUpdate:
      maxUnavailable: 0
      maxSurge: 1
```

## 2. Rollout commands (kubectl rollout)

| Need | Command |
| --- | --- |
| Follow the rollout | `kubectl rollout status deployment/<name>` |
| Pause, then resume | `kubectl rollout pause deployment/<name>`, `kubectl rollout resume deployment/<name>` |
| See the revisions | `kubectl rollout history deployment/<name>` |
| Go back one revision | `kubectl rollout undo deployment/<name>` |
| Go back to a revision | `kubectl rollout undo deployment/<name> --to-revision=<n>` |

Source: [S1]. These commands change the cluster, except `status` and `history`: give them to the user; never run them yourself.

## 3. Pitfalls (rolling update)

- The new Pods never get ready: the condition `Progressing` turns `False` after 600 seconds. Read `kubectl describe deployment <name>`. [S1]
- No readiness probe: Pods get traffic before the app is ready, and a rolling update cuts requests. [S2]
- The image tag does not change (`latest`): nothing rolls out. Always change the version tag.

## Sources

- [S1] Kubernetes, "Update a Deployment Without Downtime", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/run-application/update-deployment-rolling/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Kubernetes, "Liveness, Readiness, and Startup Probes", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/probes/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# StatefulSet

Contents: 1. When · 2. Manifest · 3. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. When to write a StatefulSet

- Write a StatefulSet when each Pod needs a stable identity and its own stable storage: a database, a broker, a node of a cluster. [S1]
- For a stateless API or a front end, write a Deployment (`docs/kubernetes/workload.md`).

## 2. StatefulSet manifest (apps/v1)

- `serviceName` names a headless Service (`clusterIP: None`) that you create yourself. [S1]
- `volumeClaimTemplates` gives each Pod its own PersistentVolumeClaim. [S1]

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: store
spec:
  clusterIP: None
  selector:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: store
  ports:
    - port: 5432
---
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: StatefulSet
metadata:
  name: store
spec:
  serviceName: store
  replicas: 1
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app.kubernetes.io/name: store
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app.kubernetes.io/name: store
    spec:
      containers:
        - name: store
          image: registry.example.internal/store:16.4
          volumeMounts:
            - name: data
              mountPath: /var/lib/data
  volumeClaimTemplates:
    - metadata:
        name: data
      spec:
        accessModes: ["ReadWriteOnce"]
        resources:
          requests:
            storage: 10Gi
```

## 3. Pitfalls (StatefulSet)

- The volumes are not deleted with the StatefulSet: delete the claims on purpose, never by accident. [S1]
- An ordered rolling update can block on a Pod that never gets ready. [S1]
- To stop the Pods in order, scale to zero before you delete the object. [S1]
- Jobs and CronJobs (`batch/v1`) are for work that ends; their page was not read for this base: run `kubectl explain job.spec` on a cluster before you write one.

## Sources

- [S1] Kubernetes, "StatefulSets", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/controllers/statefulset>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

tool: kubeconform, kube-linter, helm
version: "4"

# Validate manifests without a cluster

Contents: 1. The tools · 2. Commands · 3. Local schemas · 4. What they miss · Sources

## 1. The tools (Kubernetes validation without a cluster)

| Tool | What it checks | What it needs | Limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| kubeconform | The schema of each object; `-strict` refuses unknown fields and duplicate keys; `-kubernetes-version` | Schemas, downloaded by default; a local folder with `-schema-location` | Not the validation done by the server [S1] |
| KubeLinter | Security and production good practices, on YAML, Helm charts and Kustomize | Its binary and local files | Its options and configuration can change [S3] |
| `helm lint` | The chart: `[ERROR]` for what fails the install, `[WARNING]` for a convention; `--strict` fails on a warning | Helm | Not the result once deployed [S4] |
| `kustomize build` | That the overlay builds; prints the final objects | Kustomize | Their validity: pass them to kubeconform [S5] |
| kubectl-validate | The rules of the server, with the same code | Go to install it | No official image [S2] |

Run kubeconform first, then KubeLinter.

## 2. Commands (validate a manifest)

```sh
kubeconform -strict -summary <files>
kube-linter lint <files>
helm lint --strict <chart folder>
helm template <chart folder> | kubeconform -strict -summary
kustomize build <folder> | kubeconform -strict -summary
```

The `AGENTS.md` of the project can give the cluster version and the local schema folder. Use its commands first.

## 3. Local schemas for kubeconform

- kubeconform downloads its schemas by default. When the network is closed, it fails to download a schema. [S1]
- Point it to a local folder, filled once for the cluster version:

```sh
kubeconform -strict -summary -kubernetes-version 1.37.0 \
  -schema-location '<schema folder>/{{ .ResourceKind }}{{ .KindSuffix }}.json' <files>
```

- Custom kinds (Gateway API, operators) need their own schemas in the same folder. [S1]
- `-cache <folder>` keeps the downloaded schemas. [S1]

## 4. What validation misses

- A manifest that passes kubeconform can still be refused by the server, or fail at run time: links between objects (selector and labels, Secret in the right namespace) are not checked. [S1]
- `kubectl apply --validate -f <file>` and `kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f <file>` ask a real server; they need a cluster. Never run `kubectl apply` without a dry run.
- Say in your answer which checks ran and which did not.

## Sources

- [S1] yannh, "kubeconform", README, <https://github.com/yannh/kubeconform>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Kubernetes SIG CLI, "kubectl-validate", README, <https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kubectl-validate>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] StackRox, Red Hat, "KubeLinter", README, <https://github.com/stackrox/kube-linter>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S4] Helm, "helm lint", v4.3.1, <https://helm.sh/docs/helm/helm_lint>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] Kustomize, "Glossary", <https://kubectl.docs.kubernetes.io/references/kustomize/glossary>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

sources: [S1, S2, S3, S4, S5, S6]
license: MIT, written for this base from CC BY 4.0 and Apache 2.0 sources
variant: files
---

# Deployment and Service manifest

Contents: 1. Good practices · 2. Deployment · 3. Service · 4. Security context · 5. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Good practices of a Kubernetes manifest

- Write the latest stable `apiVersion` of each kind (`docs/kubernetes/api-versions.md`). [S1]
- Write YAML, not JSON. Keep related objects in one file, separated by `---`. [S2]
- Label every object with `app.kubernetes.io/name` and the other `app.kubernetes.io/*` labels. [S2]
- Never run a bare Pod: a Pod without a controller is not rescheduled when its node fails. Use a Deployment. [S2]
- No `hostPort` and no `hostNetwork` without a real need. Write booleans as `true` or `false` only. [S2]
- Never use the image tag `latest`: write a version (KubeLinter `latest-tag`). [S6]

## 2. Deployment (apps/v1)

The selector is required and must equal the labels of the Pod template. [S1]

```yaml
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: api
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
spec:
  replicas: 2
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app.kubernetes.io/name: api
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app.kubernetes.io/name: api
    spec:
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      containers:
        - name: api
          image: registry.example.internal/api:1.4.0
          ports:
            - containerPort: 8080
          readinessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /healthz/ready
              port: 8080
          resources:
            requests:
              cpu: "500m"
              memory: "256Mi"
            limits:
              cpu: "1"
              memory: "256Mi"
          securityContext:
            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
            readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
            capabilities:
              drop: ["ALL"]
```

The full template, with the three probes and the rollout strategy, is `templates/containers/deployment.yaml`. Probes: `docs/kubernetes/health-endpoints.md`. Resources: `docs/kubernetes/resources-realtime.md`.

## 3. Service (v1)

```yaml
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: api
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
spec:
  selector:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
  ports:
    - port: 80
      targetPort: 8080
```

- The `selector` must equal the labels of the Pods. The `targetPort` must equal the `containerPort`. Otherwise the Service has no endpoints. [S4]
- Check: `kubectl get endpointslices -l kubernetes.io/service-name=api`. [S4]

## 4. Restricted security context (Pod Security Standards)

The "Restricted" profile asks for `runAsNonRoot: true`, `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`, `seccompProfile` `RuntimeDefault` or `Localhost`, and every capability dropped. A `hostPath` volume is forbidden from the "Baseline" profile. [S5]

`runAsNonRoot` checks a numeric user: the image must set `USER` with a UID (`docs/containers/dotnet-images.md`).

`readOnlyRootFilesystem: true` answers the KubeLinter check `no-read-only-root-fs` [S6]. If the app writes files, mount an `emptyDir` volume on the folder it writes.

## 5. Pitfalls (Deployment and Service)

- Selector of the Deployment different from the Pod labels: the API refuses the object.
- Memory written `400m`: that is 0.4 byte. Write `400Mi`. [S3]
- A limit without a request: the request is set to the limit. [S3]
- `targetPort: 80` for a .NET 8 or later image: the app listens on 8080.

## Sources

- [S1] Kubernetes, "Deprecated API Migration Guide", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/using-api/deprecation-guide/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Kubernetes, "Kubernetes Configuration Good Practices", 2025-11-25, <https://kubernetes.io/blog/2025/11/25/configuration-good-practices/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Kubernetes, "Resource Management for Pods and Containers", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S4] Kubernetes, "Debug Pods", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/debug/debug-application/debug-pods/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S5] Kubernetes, "Pod Security Standards", <https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/security/pod-security-standards/>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S6] StackRox, Red Hat, "KubeLinter", README, <https://github.com/stackrox/kube-linter>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.

# Update the documents of a tool to a new version

Contents: 1. When · 2. Angular sources · 3. .NET sources · 4. Steps · 5. Played on Angular 21 to 22 · Sources

## 1. When to run this procedure (version check)

- A new major version of a tool comes out: Angular in May or June, .NET in November.
- Or `node scripts/check-versions.ts <project>` lists documents that are behind a project.

Update one tool at a time. A person with web access gathers the sources; the AI writes from the texts they give. [S1]

## 2. Sources to read, in this order (Angular)

1. The release notes `<N>.0.0` of the `angular/angular` repository: "Breaking Changes", "Deprecations", the list of features.
2. The release notes `<N>.0.0` of the `angular/angular-cli` repository: Node.js, builders, tests, migrations.
3. The announcement "Announcing Angular vN", N being the new version: what became stable, new syntax.
4. The "Version compatibility" page: Node.js, TypeScript, RxJS ranges.
5. The "Versioning and releases" page: dates of support.
6. The rules for models of the new version: `@angular/core/resources/best-practices.md` in the installed package.
7. The "Migrations" page, and the release notes of the minor versions since `.0`.

The announcement alone is not enough: it lists neither the breaking changes nor the migrations. [S1]

## 3. Sources to read each November (.NET)

1. The .NET support policy: release date, LTS or STS, end of support.
2. "C# language versioning": the new line "target framework → C# version".
3. "What's new in C# N" and the compiler breaking changes: rows "old → new", keyword pitfalls.
4. "Breaking changes in .NET N", then "What's new in .NET N" (libraries, SDK).
5. "What's new" and "Breaking changes" of ASP.NET Core N and of EF Core N.
6. `docs/dotnet/paid-packages.md`: check each license again; licenses change outside November too.

Update `docs/dotnet/` and `docs/csharp-analysis/`, then pass again three months later: the breaking change pages of .NET are often incomplete at release. [S1]

## 4. Steps (any tool)

1. Sort each point of the sources into one of five boxes: breaks; changes a default; deprecated; new stable API that replaces an old one; automatic migration. Drop the rest.
2. Write the document `v<N>-changes.md` of the topic with the `write-base-document` skill: one section per box, a wrong and a right example for the riskiest points.
3. Update the cumulative table (`old-to-new.md` for Angular): add the new rows; change the state of old patterns ("Still works", then "Deprecated in N", then "Removed in N").
4. Search the whole base for each API that was removed or deprecated: `grep -rn "<name>" docs templates .agents`. Rewrite only the sections found. Move the old form to "Old patterns".
5. In every document changed: set `version` to the new major version and `checked` to the date of the review. Update the line of each document in `docs/<topic>/index.md` and the French summary of the topic.
6. Run `node scripts/check-base.ts`, then `node scripts/check-versions.ts <project at version N>`: it must list no document of this tool.
7. Compile and lint the examples in a project at version N. Commit one tool per commit.

## 5. Played on Angular 21 to 22 (Angular 22 update)

- Trigger: Angular 22 came out on 2026-06-03.
- Sorting gave about fifteen "breaks", three "defaults", four "deprecated", five "new stable APIs" and eight "migrations".
- Written: `docs/angular/v22-changes.md`. In `docs/angular/old-to-new.md`: rows for OnPush by default, `@Service()`, `resource`, Signal Forms and `withFetch`; `ComponentFactoryResolver` and `provideRoutes()` moved to "Removed in 22"; `version` from 21 to 22.
- Searched in the base: `ChangeDetectionStrategy.OnPush`, `ChangeDetectionStrategy.Default`, `providedIn: 'root'`, `withFetch`, `ComponentFactoryResolver`, `provideRoutes`, `[field]`. Found in `docs/typescript-lint/angular-components-v22.md`: the service example now uses `@Service()`.
- Checked: the new APIs exist in the types of `@angular/core` 22.2.6; the version check lists no Angular document for a project in Angular 22. [S2]

## Sources

- [S1] Research reports "What changed in Angular and TypeScript" and "What changed in C# and .NET" of this project, sections on the update procedure, 2026-11-12.
- [S2] Types of `@angular/core` 22.2.6 and the tests of this base (`scripts/check-versions.test.ts`), 2026-11-13.

# Sujet « reading-code » : lire du C, du C++, du Java et du Rust

Ce dossier aide l'IA à expliquer un extrait de C, C++, Java ou Rust à un développeur C# et
TypeScript. Chaque guide de langage classe chaque construction en « même sens », « faux ami » ou
« sans équivalent », les faux amis d'abord. Ces langages se lisent, ils ne s'écrivent pas.

| Fichier | Ce qu'il contient |
| --- | --- |
| `c-for-csharp-readers.md` | Le C pour un lecteur C# : pointeurs, tableaux, mémoire manuelle, en-têtes, macros, comportement indéfini. |
| `c-embedded-idioms.md` | Les idiomes du code embarqué : champs de bits, `volatile`, virgule fixe, registres, interruptions. |
| `cpp-for-csharp-readers.md` | Le C++ : RAII, pointeurs intelligents, déplacement, modèles, surcharges. |
| `cpp-version-markers.md` | Les marqueurs de syntaxe qui datent un extrait C++, de C++11 à C++23. |
| `java-for-csharp-readers.md` | Le Java : génériques effacés, exceptions vérifiées, records, types scellés, flux, annotations. |
| `java-framework-idioms.md` | Ce que font sans se voir les annotations de Spring et de Lombok. |
| `rust-for-csharp-readers.md` | Le Rust : possession, emprunt, durées de vie, traits, `Option`, `Result` et `?`. |
| `rust-macros-unsafe-async.md` | Les macros, `unsafe`, l'asynchrone et les registres en Rust embarqué. |
| `build-cmake-make.md` | Ce que disent un `CMakeLists.txt` et un `Makefile`. |
| `build-maven-gradle-cargo.md` | Ce que disent un `pom.xml`, un `build.gradle` et un `Cargo.toml`. |

Le skill `explain-code` suit une méthode : repérer le langage et sa version, lister ce que l'extrait
utilise sans le définir, développer macros et code généré, lire le code avant les noms, et vérifier
que chaque nom cité existe dans l'extrait.

# Reading CMakeLists.txt and Makefile

Contents: 1. CMakeLists.txt · 2. Makefile · 3. What to take before explaining · Sources

## 1. CMakeLists.txt (CMake build)

| You read | What it says about the project | C# landmark |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `add_executable`, `add_library` (`STATIC`, `SHARED`, `MODULE`, `OBJECT`, `INTERFACE`) | The targets: programs and libraries, and their kind [S1] | `OutputType` of a `.csproj` |
| `target_link_libraries(target PRIVATE or PUBLIC or INTERFACE dependency)` | The dependencies; `PUBLIC` and `INTERFACE` pass on to users of the target [S1] | `ProjectReference`, `PackageReference` |
| `target_compile_definitions` | Macros given to the compiler (`-D`): they decide the `#ifdef` branches [S1] | `DefineConstants` |
| `target_compile_features`, `target_include_directories` | The minimum language standard; where headers are found [S1] | `LangVersion` |
| Target `Namespace::name`, `$<…>` expression, `CMAKE_BUILD_TYPE` | Imported target or alias; value computed at generation; configuration (`Debug`, `Release`, `RelWithDebInfo`, `MinSizeRel`) [S1] | Build configuration |

## 2. Makefile (GNU Make)

| You read | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `$@`, `$<`, `$^`, `$?`, `$*` | Target, first prerequisite, all prerequisites, newer prerequisites, pattern stem [S2] |
| `include`, `ifdef`, `ifeq`, `define`, `export` | Another file read; conditions; multi-line variable; variable passed to sub-processes [S2] |
| `$(wildcard …)`, `$(patsubst …)`, `$(shell …)`, `$(foreach …)`, `$(call …)` | Computed file lists and commands [S2] |

The GNU Make manual is under GFDL: described here in own words.

## 3. What to take before explaining (C and C++ build)

- The language standard: `target_compile_features` or the standard property.
- The defined macros: they say which `#ifdef` branch is compiled.
- The dependencies: they explain an unknown name (library, generated code).

## Sources

- [S1] Kitware, "cmake-buildsystem(7)", CMake 4.5.0, <https://cmake.org/cmake/help/latest/manual/cmake-buildsystem.7.html>, read 2026-11-12, BSD 3-Clause, summarized.
- [S2] Free Software Foundation, "Quick Reference", GNU make 4.4.1, <https://www.gnu.org/software/make/manual/html_node/Quick-Reference.html>, read 2026-11-12, GFDL 1.3, summarized in own words.

# Reading pom.xml, build.gradle and Cargo.toml

Contents: 1. pom.xml · 2. build.gradle · 3. Cargo.toml · Sources

## 1. pom.xml (Maven)

| You read | What it says | C# landmark |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `groupId`, `artifactId`, `version`, `packaging` | Identity and output kind (`jar` by default) [S1] | NuGet package identity |
| `parent`; `modules` | Inherited settings; sub-projects built together [S1] | `Directory.Build.props`; solution |
| `properties`, for example `maven.compiler.release` | The Java version targeted [S1] | `TargetFramework` |
| `dependencies` and `scope` (`compile`, `provided`, `runtime`, `test`, `system`); `dependencyManagement` | Libraries and when they serve; central versions [S1] | `PackageReference`; central package management |
| `build` and `plugins`; `profiles` | Build steps, code generation included; variants by environment [S1] | MSBuild targets |

The effective POM, inheritance included, comes from `mvn help:effective-pom`. [S1]

## 2. build.gradle (Gradle)

| You read | What it says | C# landmark |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `plugins { }`; `application { mainClass }` | A Groovy or Kotlin script; plugins add tasks; entry point [S2] | `.csproj`, but it is code |
| `implementation`, `api`, `compileOnly`, `runtimeOnly`, `testImplementation` | `api` is visible to whoever compiles against the library; `implementation` is hidden at compile time [S3] | `PrivateAssets` (close) |
| `libs.guava` | A dependency taken from a version catalog [S2] | Central package management |

## 3. Cargo.toml (Rust)

| You read | What it says | C# landmark |
| --- | --- | --- |

| `[dependencies]`, `[dev-dependencies]`, `[build-dependencies]` | Run, test and build-script libraries [S4] | `PackageReference` |
| `[features]`, `[workspace]`, `[profile.*]`, `[lints]` | Conditional compilation; a set of crates; optimization settings; lint levels [S4] | `DefineConstants`; solution [S5] |
